#[test]
fn compound_prefix_conditional_note_names_the_guard_for_its_admitted_callers() {
    let source="table members:\n  group: int\n  principal: principal\n  unique group, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable seals:\n  question: int\n  unique question\n  read <- deny\n  online only\ntable docs:\n  group: int\n  principal: principal\n  question: int\n  read <- (exists members[.group, viewer] and .principal = viewer) or not exists seals[.question]\n  online only\nquery table(g: int):\n  return docs where .group = g and .principal = viewer first 10 by .id\n";
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
    let note=b.notes.iter().find(|n|n.code=="N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE").unwrap();
    assert!(note.message.contains("for callers `exists members[") && note.message.contains("and otherwise remains capped"),"{note:?}");
}

#[test]
fn two_principal_status_scope_and_fixed_plus_per_row_grants_are_complete() {
    let escorts = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable escorts:\n  student: principal\n  volunteerOne: principal ?\n  volunteerTwo: principal ?\n  status: enum('requested', 'assigned', 'arrived', 'walking', 'completed')\n  at: int\n  read <- .student = viewer or exists roles[viewer] or ((.volunteerOne = viewer or .volunteerTwo = viewer) and (.status = 'assigned' or .status = 'arrived' or .status = 'walking'))\n  online only\nquery walks(c: cursor ?):\n  return escorts where (.volunteerOne = viewer or .volunteerTwo = viewer) and (.status = 'assigned' or .status = 'arrived' or .status = 'walking') first 1 by .at after c\n";
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), escorts.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    e.seed([
        ("escorts".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("student", id("s")), ("volunteerOne", id("alice")), ("volunteerTwo", Value::Null), ("status", Value::String("assigned".into())), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
        ("escorts".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("student", id("s")), ("volunteerOne", id("alice")), ("volunteerTwo", id("alice")), ("status", Value::String("walking".into())), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
        ("escorts".into(), object([("id", id("hidden")), ("student", id("s")), ("volunteerOne", id("alice")), ("volunteerTwo", Value::Null), ("status", Value::String("completed".into())), ("at", Value::Int(3))])),
    ]).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "walks", "alice"), ["a", "b"]);

    let grants = "table chapterAccess:\n  chapter: int\n  principal: principal\n  unique chapter, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable chapterSeats:\n  chapter: int\n  principal: principal\n  unique chapter, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versionAccess:\n  version: id\n  principal: principal\n  unique version, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versions:\n  chapter: int\n  at: int\n  read <- (exists chapterAccess[.chapter, viewer] and exists chapterSeats[.chapter, viewer]) and exists versionAccess[.id, viewer]\n  online only\nquery versionsOf(chapter: int, c: cursor ?):\n  return versions where .chapter = chapter first 1 by .at after c\n";
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), grants.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| matches!(n.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE")), "{:?}", b.notes);
    assert!(b.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"));
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    e.seed([
        ("chapterAccess".into(), object([("id", id("ca")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
        ("chapterSeats".into(), object([("id", id("cs")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
        ("versionAccess".into(), object([("id", id("va")), ("version", id("a")), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
        ("versions".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
        ("versions".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
    ]).unwrap();
    let args = |chapter| BTreeMap::from([("chapter".into(), Value::Int(chapter)), ("c".into(), Value::Null)]);
    let r = e.query("versionsOf", Context::new("alice", args(1))).unwrap();
    assert!(r.complete, "{r:?}");
    assert_eq!(r.data.as_array().unwrap().iter().map(|row| row.field("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), ["a"]);
    let r = e.query("versionsOf", Context::new("bob", args(1))).unwrap();
    assert!(r.complete && r.data.as_array().unwrap().is_empty(), "{r:?}");

    // A rule probe sees its target with system authority, while the residual
    // query probe uses ordinary read authority.  Keep PAGE out unless those
    // observations are equivalent for a timeless readable exact point.
    for unsafe_grant in [
        grants.replacen("  read <- .principal = viewer", "  read <- deny", 1),
        grants.replacen("  read <- .principal = viewer", "  until: time ?\n  expire at .until\n  read <- .principal = viewer", 1),
        grants.replacen("  principal: principal\n  unique chapter, principal", "  principal: principal\n  active: bool\n  unique chapter, principal", 1)
            .replace("exists chapterAccess[.chapter, viewer]", "exists chapterAccess[chapter = .chapter, principal = viewer, active = true]"),
    ] {
        let declined = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), unsafe_grant)]);
        assert!(declined.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", declined.diagnostics);
        assert!(declined.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", declined.notes);
    }

    // Validator readability is about the generated residual point after page
    // pins are substituted: roles[viewer] is visible even though the authored
    // fixed guard is roles[.bucket]. Two already-static siblings also prove
    // the full left-associated residual conjunction rather than disappearing.
    let substituted = "table roles:\n  bucket: principal\n  unique bucket\n  read <- .bucket = viewer\n  online only\ntable seats:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable permits:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable access:\n  item: id\n  bucket: principal\n  principal: principal\n  unique item, bucket, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable items:\n  bucket: principal\n  at: int\n  read <- ((exists roles[.bucket] and exists seats[viewer]) and exists permits[viewer]) and exists access[.id, .bucket, viewer]\n  online only\nquery mine(c: cursor ?):\n  return items where .bucket = viewer first 10 by .at after c\n";
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), substituted.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| matches!(n.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE")), "{:?}", b.notes);
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    e.seed([
        ("roles".into(), object([("id", id("r")), ("bucket", id("alice"))])),
        ("seats".into(), object([("id", id("s")), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
        ("permits".into(), object([("id", id("p")), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
        ("access".into(), object([("id", id("x")), ("item", id("a")), ("bucket", id("alice")), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
        ("items".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("bucket", id("alice")), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
        ("items".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("bucket", id("alice")), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
    ]).unwrap();
    let r = e.query("mine", Context::new("alice", BTreeMap::from([("c".into(), Value::Null)]))).unwrap();
    assert!(r.complete, "{r:?}");
    assert_eq!(r.data.as_array().unwrap().iter().map(|row| row.field("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), ["a"]);
}

#[test]
fn standalone_static_grant_leaves_coexist_with_membership_lanes() {
    let tables = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('advisor', 'student')\n  unique principal\n  public\n  online only\ntable access:\n  item: items\n  principal: principal\n  unique item, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\n";
    let static_and_membership = format!("{tables}table items:\n  at: int\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or exists access[.id, viewer]\n  online only\nquery page(c: cursor ?):\n  return items first 10 by .at after c\n");
    let party_static_and_membership = static_and_membership.replace(
        "read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or exists access[.id, viewer]",
        "read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or exists access[.id, viewer]",
    ).replace("table items:\n  at: int", "table items:\n  owner: principal\n  at: int");
    let fixed_and_varying = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('advisor', 'student')\n  unique principal\n  public\n  online only\ntable chapterAccess:\n  chapter: int\n  principal: principal\n  unique chapter, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versionAccess:\n  version: id\n  principal: principal\n  unique version, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versions:\n  chapter: int\n  at: int\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or (exists chapterAccess[.chapter, viewer] and exists versionAccess[.id, viewer])\n  online only\nquery page(chapter: int, c: cursor ?):\n  return versions where .chapter = chapter first 10 by .at after c\n";

    for (name, source) in [
        ("static-membership", static_and_membership.as_str()),
        ("party-static-membership", party_static_and_membership.as_str()),
        ("static-fixed-varying", fixed_and_varying),
    ] {
        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{name}: {:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        assert!(!bound.notes.iter().any(|note| matches!(note.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE")),
            "{name}: {:?}", bound.notes);
        assert!(snapback4_core::ir::body_merges(&bound.programs[0].program.body).iter().any(|merge| merge.branches()),
            "{name}: {:?}", bound.programs);
    }

    // A static point inside the membership conjunction remains a retained
    // ordinary-read guard. Hidden fixed points still decline PAGE.
    let hidden = fixed_and_varying.replacen(
        "  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versionAccess:",
        "  read <- deny\n  online only\ntable versionAccess:",
        1,
    );
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), hidden)]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    assert!(bound.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", bound.notes);

    // Every omitted standalone static alternative must be independently
    // readable. A readable role must not conceal a hidden system-only role.
    let mixed_hidden = static_and_membership.replacen(
        "table roles:",
        "table secretRoles:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- deny\n  online only\ntable roles:",
        1,
    ).replace(
        "read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or exists access[.id, viewer]",
        "read <- exists secretRoles[viewer] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or exists access[.id, viewer]",
    );
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), mixed_hidden)]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    assert!(bound.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", bound.notes);
    assert!(snapback4_core::ir::body_merges(&bound.programs[0].program.body)
        .iter().all(|merge| !merge.branches()), "{:?}", bound.programs);
}

#[test]
fn lean_gate_prices_every_filter_product_pin_before_comparison() {
    let source = "table access:\n  bucket: text <=40\n  principal: principal\n  unique bucket, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable items:\n  bucket: text <=40\n  region: text <=435\n  status: text <=435\n  at: int\n  read <- exists access[.bucket, viewer]\n  online only\nquery mine(c: cursor ?):\n  return items where (.region = 'north' or .region = 'south') and (.status = 'open' or .status = 'held') first 10 by .at after c\n";
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
    assert!(snapback4_core::ir::body_merges(&bound.programs[0].program.body)
        .iter().all(|merge| !merge.branches()), "{:?}", bound.programs);
    let module = snapback4_lang::emit::lean::lean(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(module.program("mine").unwrap_err().reason, "page-membership-key-bound");
}

#[test]
fn equality_filter_note_prints_one_matching_index_and_query() {
    let source = "table skippers:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable logs:\n  starts: time\n  by byStart: starts, id\n  public\n  online only\ntable watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: time\n  by byStart: starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer or exists skippers[viewer]\n  online only\nquery myWatches():\n  ignored = logs first 1 by byStart\n  return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byStart\n";
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let note = b.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap();
    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id"), "{note}");
    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("ignored = logs first 1 by byStart"), "{note}");
    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("return watches[] first 240 by byCrewStarts where .crew = viewer"), "{note}");

    let rewritten = note.rewrite.split("```quarry\n").nth(1).and_then(|rest| rest.split("\n```").next()).unwrap();
    let query = source.find("query myWatches():").unwrap();
    let repaired = format!("{}{}\n", source[..query].replace("  by byStart: starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer",
        "  by byStart: starts, id\n  by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer"), rewritten);
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired)]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);

    // The old order still fits, but adding the authorization prefix would
    // exceed the portable encoded-key ceiling. Do not print an edit that the
    // compiler itself rejects.
    let wide = source.replace("table watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: time", "table watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: text <=430");
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), wide)]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let note = b.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap();
    assert!(!note.rewrite.contains("by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id"), "{note}");

    // An authored bracket value addresses `starts` in the old index. Changing
    // only the index would reinterpret it as `crew`, so omit that repair too.
    let keyed = source.replace("query myWatches():", "query myWatches(s: time):")
        .replace("return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byStart",
            "return watches[s] first 240 by byStart where .crew = viewer");
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), keyed)]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let note = b.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap();
    assert!(!note.rewrite.contains("by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id"), "{note}");
}

#[test]
fn exact_vacancy_pages_publish_only_rule_visible_exhaustion() {
    let source = "table artists:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable designs:\n  oneOfKind: bool\n  at: int\n  read <- exists artists[viewer] or not .oneOfKind or not exists holds[design = .id] or exists holds[design = .id, owner = viewer]\n  online only\ntable holds:\n  design: designs\n  owner: principal\n  expiresAt: time ?\n  unique design\n  read <- .owner = viewer or exists artists[viewer]\n  expire at .expiresAt\n  online only\nquery available(c: cursor ?):\n  return designs first 1 by .at after c\nquery availableAssigned(c: cursor ?):\n  page = designs first 1 by .at after c\n  return page\n";
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
    assert!(b.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"));
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    e.seed([
        ("artists".into(), object([("id", id("artist")), ("principal", id("art"))])),
        ("designs".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("oneOfKind", Value::Bool(false)), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
        ("designs".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("oneOfKind", Value::Bool(true)), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
        ("designs".into(), object([("id", id("c")), ("oneOfKind", Value::Bool(true)), ("at", Value::Int(3))])),
        ("holds".into(), object([("id", id("h")), ("design", id("c")), ("owner", id("alice")), ("expiresAt", Value::Null)])),
    ]).unwrap();
    for ceilings in [
        snapback4_core::Ceilings { examined: 0, ..Default::default() },
        snapback4_core::Ceilings { rule_probes: 0, ..Default::default() },
    ] {
        e.set_ceilings(ceilings);
        let cut = e.query("available", Context::new("bob", BTreeMap::from([("c".into(), Value::Null)]))).unwrap();
        assert!(!cut.complete, "a cut before private vacancy exhaustion must stay incomplete: {cut:?}");
    }
    e.set_ceilings(Default::default());
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "available", "bob"), ["a", "b"]);
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "availableAssigned", "bob"), ["a", "b"]);
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "available", "alice"), ["a", "b", "c"]);
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "available", "art"), ["a", "b", "c"]);

    let non_point = source.replace("  design: designs\n", "  design: designs ?\n")
        .replace(" or exists holds[design = .id, owner = viewer]", "");
    let declined = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), non_point)]);
    assert!(declined.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", declined.diagnostics);
    assert!(declined.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"));
}

## 0. Summary

A list such as

```quarry
query myTasks(c: cursor ?):
  return tasks where .owner = viewer or .assignee = viewer last 50 by .at after c
```

over `read <- .owner = viewer or .assignee = viewer` was never `complete`.
The engine walked one index per page; neither `[owner, at, id]` nor
`[assignee, at, id]` holds every row the caller may read, so the page was
planned on `[at, id]`, checked the rule per row, and could not tell "no more"
from "more you may not read". `check` printed `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` with a
verified per-party rewrite (LLP 3000.036 §12.2, §13.2, §16.1). Across six farm
waves 12, 19 and 16 lanes reported the shape.

Now the one-query form is complete. When a query page's predicate, or its
table's read rule, is a disjunction of branches each of which pins an index
prefix that decides the read rule, the planner reads it as a k-way merge of
the branch ranges in the page's order. A row matching several branches is
emitted once. Each branch's rows are decided by its own pins, so the merged
page is `complete` exactly when every branch's walk finishes. The cursor is
one position in the shared order, which every branch resumes strictly after
(inclusively, for an unfinished first returned shape); its token is a new
version naming the branch set. A meter cut continues from the frontier every
branch has examined. Devices run the same plan with the existing per-scan
coverage rules. The Lean model's C8 gains the rule, with a coverage proof.

Owner-or-staff (`.owner = viewer or exists staff[viewer]`) is the same plan
with one branch guarded by the readable grant of §12.1: held, that branch is
the whole page; not held, it contributes nothing and the owner branch is the
page. A staff page is complete for staff (as before) and an owner's page is
complete for everyone else (new). The guide's owner-or-staff and two-party
recipes are now single queries.

## 1. What the builders met

LLP 3000.036 records the shape in waves 2–6: `#12` (12 lanes), `#3b`
(19 lanes) and the §14.2 and §16.1 notes (16 lanes). The lanes either pasted
the per-party rewrite (two queries, two cursors, a client-side merge and
de-duplication, two loading states) or kept the one-query form and shipped a
list that always said "there may be more". The engine already merges ordered
lanes (LLP 3000 §7, 2026-09-13) and already has the position that makes a
merge resumable (LLP 3000.007.000 §1); this RFC lets one merge read several
indexes of one table.

## 2. The rule

Definitions, over the checked schema:

- An **equality pin** in a conjunction is a conjunct `.col = e` (either
  side) or a bare boolean column (`.flag`, `not .flag`), where `e` reads no
  row column, `.col` is a plain column of the table and not `id`. On a
  nullable column it counts only when `e` is `viewer`: a branch never seeks a
  null the original filter would reject.
- A **party disjunct** of a read rule is `.col = viewer` (either side) on a
  principal column of the row.
- A **guard disjunct** reads no column of the row (§12.1: a role probe, a
  seat such as `viewer = 'service:audit'`), or reads only columns the page's
  own pins fix (§16.3: `exists boards[.board, owner = viewer]` under `.board =
  b`). It is **usable** when `rules::page_guard_decision` takes it: no `not`,
  and every row it probes readable by whoever its key names.
- A prefix **decides** the read rule when every column the rule reads is
  pinned (with the self-witness proof of LLP 3000.007 §16), or the rule is
  true by the pins' form: `allow`, a `.col = viewer` disjunct pinned to
  `viewer`, a `.col = 'x'` disjunct pinned to `'x'`, and `and`/`or` of
  those. This is the core's `rules::rule_true`/`scan_visibility`, the
  binder's `intent_branches::decided` and the model's `decidedPins`.
- A predicate is **total** when it is comparisons (`=`, `!=`, `<`, `<=`,
  `>`, `>=`, `in`) and boolean operators over the row's own columns,
  arguments, literals and `viewer`: its evaluation never refuses and never
  reads. A member of anything but the row (`.meta.ok`, `m.ok`) is not: it
  binds to a read of the referenced row. CUT (2026-10-12) fixes interrupted
  single-index predicates by retrying their row inclusively (LLP 3000.007
  §20.24.1), but does not make such a predicate total: missing/hidden targets
  can still refuse or be unavailable, and distribution changes which reads
  short-circuit. Keep this exclusion and the merge's decided lane frontier.
  Arithmetic is not either,
  not even a time window (`now - 7d` overflows when the clock is at the
  integer bound), so such a page keeps one index. The Lean model has no `in`;
  the emitter names it unsupported.

A **query page** (`t where … first|last N by … [after c]` in a query program;
never in a mutation, job, shared helper or shape, which keep ordinary scans)
is **branch-decided** when all of these hold:

- it names no explicit index or key, does not search (`matching`), has no
  computed order, and reads an ordinary table (not a maintained view, row
  view, ephemeral or timeline table, a `public` declaration or a `read
  through` child), whose read rule is one `read` declaration;
- its own pins do **not** decide the rule (a decided page keeps its plan,
  index and cursor);
- its predicate is total;
- and either
  1. **branches in `where`:** exactly one top-level conjunct is an `or` of
     2–8 alternatives, each holding an equality pin, and each alternative
     with the other conjuncts decides the rule; or
  2. **branches in the read rule:** the rule is an `or` of at least two
     disjuncts, each a party or a usable guard, with at least one party.
     The branches are `base_j and .party_k = viewer` for every base branch
     (case 1's alternatives when `where` has such an `or`, else the whole
     `where`) and party, plus one **grant branch**, the whole `where`, read
     for a caller the rule's guards admit and for the system, which every
     rule admits (a rule of parties alone has no guard: the lane is the
     system's only);
- and every branch plans to an index of at most 8 components whose position
  after its pins is the page's order then `id` (a branch never pins an order
  column: a merge lane keeps such an equality as a seek), with at most 8
  branches.

Otherwise the page keeps its single-index plan and, when undecided,
`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` with its per-party rewrite.

A single party (`read <- .owner = viewer`) is not expanded: its complete form
is the pin the author writes, and expanding it would replan every unpinned
owner page (§12 Q1). 8 is both the index width limit and the point below
which the lazy merge reads a page per lane (`word_merge.rs`); every branch
may be a synthesized index.

### 2.1 Why the merged rows are exactly the readable matches

Write `W` for the predicate and `R` for the read rule; the page returns, in
order, the rows `r` with `W(r) ∧ R(r)`.

- Case 1: `W = rest ∧ (a_1 ∨ … ∨ a_k)`, so `W = ∨_j (a_j ∧ rest)`. Branch
  `j` reads the range its pins select and filters by the rest of `a_j ∧
  rest`; its prefix decides `R`, so every row of the branch is readable
  (verdict true) or none is (verdict false: the branch is empty). The union
  is `∨_j (a_j ∧ rest ∧ R) = W ∧ R`.
- Case 2: `R = ∨_k (.p_k = viewer) ∨ G` with `G` row-independent or reading
  only the page's pinned columns, so for every row the page selects and this
  caller `R(r) = ∨_k (r.p_k = viewer) ∨ g` with `g` one boolean (the guard
  with the pinned values), and
  `W ∧ R = ∨_{j,k} (b_j ∧ .p_k = viewer) ∨ (g ∧ W)`. Each party branch is
  decided true by its pin; the grant branch is all of `W` when `g` holds and
  nothing otherwise, and then it is a superset of every other branch, so the
  plan reads it alone. The Lean model proves the party half
  (`parties_cover_view`): every visible row carries the viewer in a party
  column.

The argument distributes `W` over its alternatives, so it needs `W` total:
with `or (.assignee = viewer and 1 / n = 1)`, a row in both parties skips the
division in the original and would evaluate it in its own branch (GPT
review, finding 1). A branch evaluates the predicate only on rows the
original also reads, so a total predicate gives the same rows.

A row in several branches has the same position in each (§5), so the merge
emits it once. No branch reads a row the caller may not read, so no such row
reaches the merge, the filter, the lookahead or the cursor.

## 3. The plan

**Planning** (`bind/intent_branches.rs`, called from `intent::plan` where the
other lane expansions run). Each branch is an ordinary planned read
(`Requests::plan_lane`): its pins form the prefix (a party pin first, so the
deciding pin is never left residual), the page's order then `id` the suffix;
the planner reuses a declared index with exactly those columns or synthesizes
one. The expansion is dropped, and the read planned as written, when a branch
does not decide the rule (by the binder's check), the branches' order columns
differ, an index would exceed 8 components, or a branch exceeds the portable 2,048-byte key or 512-byte label bound,
or planning a branch raised a diagnostic: a program that compiled before still compiles. The result is the
existing `MergeExpr` with planned lanes, each carrying the branch identity
`$or` (or `$or:grant`) in the field views use for theirs; the planned-merge
binding passes it through to the IR `MergeLane.branch`. No IR type gains a
field, so every existing program's IR and pinned digest is unchanged;
`Merge::branches()` recognises the plan.

**Binding check** (`program/views.rs`). With the core schema in hand, the
binder re-checks the plan with the runtime's own functions: every grant
lane's `rules::covering_grant` (every disjunct that is not a party is a
usable guard of `page_guard_decision` for the lane's pins, §12.1, §16.3) and `rules::branch_merge_lazy` (same table and order, scalar
pins, every non-grant lane `scan_visibility(..).uniform`, a total position,
identical position columns). `covering_grant` compares each non-party
disjunct after the same prefix simplification that produced the page guard;
`allow`, `deny`, `not` and required boolean pins therefore have one identity
through the coverage proof, while a nullable boolean's `null` is never
normalized to `false`. If it fails and the plan has a grant lane, the
read binds as its grant lane alone, which is the read as written: the grant
lane is planned exactly as the single-index page is (not as a merge lane), so
the fallback is 0.4.23's plan, page guard included; when that plan's order
differs from the party lanes' (an equality on the first order column becomes
a pin there), the page is not merged at all (a hidden
roster, `read <- deny` on `staff`, keeps today's plan and note); without a
grant lane the merge stays, sound and, for a lane that is not decided,
incomplete.

**Execution** (`engine/branch_merge.rs`). A branch merge that passes
`branch_merge_lazy` is a lazy merge whose lanes may read different indexes of
one table. Each lane's position is its index components after its prefix,
plus `id` when they do not end in it (LLP 3000.007.000 §1); the binding check
makes the positions comparable, and because they end in the row id, equal
positions are the same row. Grant lanes open first. A grant lane's guard is
decided once with the lane's pinned values (`grant_verdict`, charged as §12.1) and, when it holds, the lane
reads as a decided page (`scan_consumed`'s grant arm, through the runner's
`merge_grant`) and the other lanes are not opened; when it does not hold, the
lane is not read and does not mark the page non-uniform; for the system it
reads every row. Each lane opens with one read of `take + 1` rows; the heap
pops in position order, de-duplicates by id and refills a lane strictly after
its last popped position. A branch merge that is not lazy-eligible takes the
eager path with the same branch descriptor; it is sound and, with a lane
that is not decided, incomplete with no `next`.

**Page guards (LLP 3000.036 §16.3).** Without the merge, 0.4.23 decides a
single-index page once for a caller a pinned guard admits, and walks it per
row (incomplete) for everyone else. With it, a page whose rule is parties and
such guards merges the guard branch with the party branches, so the guard's
holder reads the guard branch alone and everyone else their party branches,
all complete. A page the merge does not cover keeps the single-index guard.

**Older engines.** Older devices refuse a merged backend by its capability
(§5). Were one to run the plan anyway, an engine that does not know the lane
identities (0.4.22) ignores them, so a backend compiled by this release still runs on an older
device: lanes over different indexes take the eager merge, which translates
one cursor across lanes with the same suffix (LLP 3000.007 §20.3) and
de-duplicates by id. It returns the same rows, mints its own cursor form, and
evaluates a grant lane as an ordinary page. For a plan without guards the
pages continue as here. A plan with a guard is only ever run by an older
*device* (servers compile their own IR), and a guard's table never syncs
except for a seat, which the device trusts: a role or parent-probe guard's
table is online only, so the older device's answer is unknown and the client
asks the server, never paging the device's incomplete answer (GPT round 2,
finding 2). `an_older_engine_reads_the_plan_as_an_eager_merge`
runs the plan with the identities stripped and compares every page.

## 4. Completeness and disclosure

- **Exact.** The page is `complete` when no lane is non-uniform, the meter
  did not cut, and the existing query-level conditions hold (position loss,
  device coverage, horizons). By §2.1 its rows are then exactly the first N
  readable matches after the cursor.
- **Nothing outside the rule.** Every lane is admitted by its own prefix
  verdict (`prefix_verdict`) or its grant before any row is read; a false
  verdict reads nothing. This is the admission a direct page on that prefix
  takes.
- **What `complete: true` and the meter reveal.** For a party or `where`
  branch, what a direct page on that prefix reveals: a verdict decided by
  facts the caller could read. For a grant branch, whether the grant holds,
  which §12.1 already reveals through a staff page's `complete` and which
  `grant_decision` restricts to grants whose every fact the caller can read.
  The number of lanes opened (one when a grant holds) carries the same bit.
  A grant charges the rule's static probe count on either verdict.
- **The token.** The version-4 descriptor hashes the branch set's index
  names and grant flags: schema facts. It does not encode row data.
- **Across pages.** The position is shared by every plan of the page, so a
  grant gained or lost between pages continues correctly from the same
  cursor; completeness is per page, as for every page.

## 5. The cursor

The continuation is one position: the shared position values (the order
columns, then `id`) of a row. Every lane resumes strictly after it on its own
index. One position suffices because it ends in the row id, so it totally
orders the union: the first N rows after `p` in the union are the first N
after `p` merged from each branch's rows after `p`, including a row several
branches hold.

The token is **version 4**, or **version 5** when inclusive:

```
[4 | 5, table, "$or:<branch-set SHA-256>", order, suffix, values, scope]
```

the layout of version 3 (an inclusive direct token), so the device decodes it
with the same code (sharing it saved 646 B of the web device). The index
field hashes the branch set in lane order: a domain marker, lane count, and
each index name with its byte length and grant flag, all unambiguously framed;
`scope` carries the argument-presence variant as other tokens do; the suffix
is the shared typed position. Encoding picks 4 or 5 exactly for a branch
descriptor, and decoding re-encodes and compares, so a version-4 or -5 token
without one, or a branch descriptor in an older version, is refused. The descriptor is bounded even when eight lanes reuse a legal 507-byte
index name: it is `$or:` followed by a SHA-256 digest, never the labels themselves.

- **Inclusive positions.** An unfinished first returned shape records its
  row's position inclusively (`shaping.rs`), on merges too (GPT review,
  finding 2). Version 5 carries it; every lane then seeks at or after
  the position, the row is re-read (and de-duplicated), and the existing
  no-progress check (`E_SHAPE_EXCEEDS_METER`) compares the canonical tokens.
- **Another plan's cursor restarts the read.** A token minted by this read's
  earlier one-index plan, or for another branch set, is refused with
  `E_CURSOR_INDEX`: "cursor belongs to another plan of this read; it now
  merges its branches (owner or another party): restart this read with a null
  cursor". This is new in the merge path; ordinary pages still refuse a
  foreign descriptor as `E_INPUT` (Muse review, finding 2).
- **The token is scoped to every lane's pins.** Its scope carries a SHA-256
  digest of the evaluated prefixes of all lanes, in lane order (their
  canonical encoding), and the generation, as a direct page's carries its
  prefix. The digest, not the prefixes: lanes that each fit the index key
  bound can together exceed the token's 4,096 bytes (three 1,600-byte folder
  pins; GPT round 4, finding 3). A cursor followed with other arguments,
  or by another viewer (a party pin is the viewer), refuses `cursor: …
  different prefix or generation` (`E_INPUT`) instead of resuming lanes over
  ranges it never saw (Muse round 3, finding 1).
- **Older engines refuse the token, and the plan.** A 0.4.22 engine or device
  decodes versions 4 and 5 as "cursor token version 4 is unsupported"
  (`E_INPUT`). A backend with a merged plan requires the schema capability
  `branch-merge-v1` (from the operation fact `branchMerge`, set when a
  merge sits anywhere in the program's IR, including a returned row
  shape's field; GPT round 4, finding 2), so an older
  device refuses the whole backend by name ("upgrade the engine/device")
  rather than running the plan or decoding a server's cursor (GPT round 3,
  finding 2).

## 6. Metering and ceilings

The meter charges what the plan does, in the existing units:

- each lane's opening is an ordinary scan: its prefix verdict (the rule's
  static probe count, once), one `examined` per candidate read (a batch of
  `take + 1` per lane), index entries as the store reports;
- one `step` when a lane's first head enters the heap and one per pop,
  including duplicate pops; a refill's push is not charged (as in every lazy
  merge; Muse review, finding 7);
- a grant charges the read rule's static probe count once, on either
  verdict.

With at most 8 lanes and a `take + 1` batch per lane, a lane never runs out
before the page finds its `(N+1)`th distinct row, so a cut can only happen
while the lanes open (GPT review, finding 4). The merge then keeps every row
it can prove is next: each lane records how far it examined (`through`: its
last returned row, or, when the cut fell inside its read, the last
rule-visible candidate it examined, which may be a filter reject;
`scan_consumed` reports it through the runner's `scan_frontier`, armed with
the lane's own table and index so a nested read's key never stands in). The
**frontier** is the least such position over the lanes still open. The page
emits only rows at or before the frontier, so it is a true prefix of the
merged order, and its `next` is the frontier: every lane has examined that
far, so resuming strictly after it loses nothing. When a lane examined nothing
past the cursor (it was never reached), the frontier is the cursor itself:
the page is empty, incomplete, without `next` (a cut while opening, as LLP
3000.007.000 §2). A page that fills before the frontier continues from its
last row as usual. Only the read meters (`examined`, `index_entries`,
`rule_probes`) cut; `steps` and `heap_bytes` abort the read, as everywhere.

## 7. Devices

The device runs the same interpreter over both stores, so the plan is the
same. Each lane applies the existing per-scan coverage rules: a lane over a
held partition is trusted (`trust_synced_read`) and complete when its group is
acquired and its horizon allows; a lane over an online-only table, an
unacquired group or beyond an acquired horizon marks the read unknown, and
the client asks the server (LLP 3000.028). A grant lane whose grant probes a
table is never decided on a device (§12.1): the read is unknown. A **seat**
(`viewer = 'service:audit'`) reads no table and is decided on a device too
(GPT review, finding 7): `read <- .owner = viewer or viewer = 'service:audit'`
syncs to `.owner` (service disjuncts leave the device audience), so the
owner's page answers from the held partition, complete, on both stores.

UNION integration (LLP 3000.036.000.000) admits synced party/group unions.
Those source tables retain ordinary per-arm scan planning: a grant lane that
probes membership would defer on a device despite the union's acquired
coverage. `intent_branches::branches` excludes sources whose sync needs contain
multiple arms. Online sources, held-group where-merges and service-seat plans
retain this RFC's merged-page behavior. The UNION simulator's snapshot-limit,
epoch and exhaustive owner/group worlds qualify the integration; the branch
merge suite continues to qualify its own surfaces.

The device oracle (`run-devices`) originally did not draw group-synced tables:
the first run that did found that a held group's pinned page charges one rule
probe on the server and none on a device, merged or not (the plain pin shows
it alone). That is a meter-parity gap older than this change. CUT (2026-10-12) repairs it:
delivered uniform pages reserve the server's logical prefix verdict
(LLP 3000.007 §20.24.7); the generator now draws group-synced tables.
The resolution is in
`issues/closed/20261012-snapback4-device-group-page-rule-probe-meter.md`.

## 8. Interactions

| Shape | Behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| `first` / `last` | Both directions; the heap orders by the shared position. |
| `after c` | §5. A cursorless top-N merge is complete when it fills (LLP 3000.027 §1). |
| Returned shapes | The page owns the merge site; shape positions use the shared suffix; inclusive positions as §5. |
| `exists t where W` | Case 1 only: `exists (branch 1) or … or exists (branch k)`, each a decided existence read, so `true` or `false`, never §15.12's "true or unavailable". With a grant, unchanged (§12 Q3). |
| `count`, `sum`, `min`, `max` | Unchanged (`E_QUARRY_BOUND` for an undecided total; §12 Q4). |
| Search (`matching`) | Unchanged; `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` keeps the membership-search rewrite (§12 Q5). |
| Maintained views, row views, ephemeral and timeline tables, `read through` | Unchanged (excluded in §2). |
| Mutations, jobs, shared helpers and shapes | Unchanged: an undecided page in a mutation stays `E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT`; a job reads as the system. |
| A capped membership branch (`.owner = viewer or exists shares[.parent, viewer]`) | Expanded under §14 when the row-dependent key is an ordinary target column. A target `id` key keeps the original scan. |
| Arithmetic, calls, `!`, a nested read or a member through a reference in the predicate | Unchanged (not total). |
| An order column a party or alternative would pin | Unchanged (the lane keeps it as a seek, so it would not decide). |
| `check` | `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` iterates the bound scans; a merge's lanes are decided and its grant lane is skipped, so the note and its per-party rewrite are not printed for a page the merge covers, and remain for every shape above. |
| `test.cases.jsonl` | A merged page answers `complete`; the default expectation accepts it. |

## 9. The Lean model

OPEN C8 said "a page is uniform exactly when every row column its read rule
reads is pinned; a non-uniform page is never complete". With 0.4.23 (LN) it
says *decided*, per caller: every column pinned, or a page guard that holds
over the caller's view (`decided`, `granted`). This change adds two syntactic
verdicts (`Eval.lean`): `decidedPins` (`Rule.byForm`, the pins' form, so an
argument that merely equals the viewer decides nothing, as in the engine) and
`branchDecided` (§2's cases 1 and 2 over the model's surface syntax:
totality, eligible pins, first pin per column, no order or `id` pins, the
width bound, 2–8 alternatives, at most 8 branches; queries only, never in a
helper's body; case 2 takes usable guards, `Rule.usableGuard`, which mirrors
the core's prefix simplification and `disclosing`: it substitutes literal and
viewer pins, turns known-false terms into `deny` before folding `and`, `or`
and `not` (so `or` drops a false arm), and
admits a negated Boolean comparison whose remaining field is pinned by a
stable required-Boolean argument. A
nullable `null` pin is never normalized to Boolean `false`, and a negated
probe remains ineligible. The verdict counts the grant lane toward the 8 and declines a
guarded page whose first order column is pinned). `exists` over branches
(case 1 only) is decided too (`existsRead`'s verdict).
The executable model carries those original expression pins into `granted`:
the disjunct must pass the same static `usableGuard` test before its evaluated
scalar pins are normalized. A runtime argument value therefore cannot turn a
compound negation that the planner rejected into a complete page.
The party-and-guard half of "nothing missed" is §2.1's argument; the proofs
cover the party half (`parties_cover_view`). `pageRead` takes the syntactic verdict and
ors it with LN's; the page's rows, order and cursor do not change, and a
merged page keeps `next` after a full page only when another matching row
follows (OPEN C2's merged arm). `pageRead_complete` concludes `decided ∨
form`, `pageRead_complete_store` (LN) holds for a page neither form nor
branches decide, and `pageRead_visible`, `page_rows_readable` and
`pageRead_perm` are restated over the new arguments; `Proofs/Branches.lean`
adds `partyLeaves_cover` and `parties_cover_view` (the party ranges miss no
readable row). `granted_readable` connects the executable model to the
original rule: normalization preserves the rule verdict, the normalized
positive guard lifts from the caller's view to the store, and pin substitution
preserves the selected row. Three examples run the shapes; LN's two examples of a guarded
page's non-holder now expect `complete` (45 examples in all).

The model checks completeness and data; its cut positions are abstract (OPEN
M1), so the frontier continuation is checked by the engine's meter oracle and
the tests, not by the model.

## 11. Alternatives

- **Keep the per-party rewrite** (status quo). Correct, but every client
  implements a two-cursor merge with de-duplication.
- **Per-branch cursors** (a vector of positions). Not needed: one shared
  position is exact (§5).
- **The eager merge** (`take + 1` from every lane, sort, truncate). It is the
  older engines' downgrade path; it cannot continue a cut.
- **New IR fields** (`Merge.branches`, `MergeLane.grant`, the draft's
  proposal). The lane identity needs no new field, keeps every digest, and an
  older decoder's behaviour is the same either way (it ignores unknown
  fields); a capability fence would refuse the whole backend on an older
  device for a plan it runs correctly (GPT review, finding 6).
- **Union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000 §4). Orthogonal: a device could
  hold a two-party table, and the server page would still need this plan.
- **A readers table** (one row per party). The offline recipe; it adds writes
  and a table the author maintains.
- **Expand single-party rules too.** Deferred (Q1).

## 12. Open questions for the author

- **Q1.** Should a single party (`read <- .owner = viewer`) also expand, so
  `todos last 50 by .at` is complete without the pin? It replans every
  unpinned owner page and changes the `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` teaching.
- **Q2.** Is 8 the right branch bound?
- **Q3.** A grant-guarded existence lane, so owner-or-staff `exists` answers
  `false` too.
- **Q4.** Decided totals over branches by inclusion–exclusion
  (`count(a) + count(b) - count(a ∧ b)`).
- **Q5.** Branch merges of search pages (a merge of word merges).
- **Q6 resolved (PAGE, 2026-10-12).** A capped membership branch on an
  ordinary target column is a pinned lane merged with the per-membership
  lanes in §14. A target `id` key remains outside the expansion.
- **Q7 resolved (CUT, 2026-10-12).** Held-group uniform pages reserve the server's prefix-rule charge, including query admission (§7; LLP 3000.007 §20.24.7).

## 13. Review record

Two blind reviews of the draft, one per family, each with the same prompt;
neither saw the other. Both read NOT READY. Revision 1 is the implementation;
it was not re-reviewed.

- **GPT** (`gpt-6.1-sol`, high; `llp/reviews/3000.036.001-merged-pages.gpt.md`):
  1 short circuit — accepted: predicates must be total (§2, §2.1), with a
  test; 2 inclusive cursors — accepted: version 5 carries inclusivity, every
  lane seeks at or after it, tested with the shape retry (§5); 3 order-column
  pins and index width — accepted: both drop the expansion (§2, §3), tested;
  4 drain cuts unreachable and step accounting — accepted: the frontier
  continuation replaces the drain-cut claim, and §6 states the charges and
  which meters cut; 5 Lean needs syntax and scope — accepted: the model
  classifies the surface syntax (`byForm` on pin forms, `branchDecided`) and
  queries only (§9); 6 old engines execute the new plan — accepted as a
  documented, tested downgrade rather than a fence (§3, §11); 7 seats sync —
  accepted: a seat is decided on a device, tested on both stores (§7).
- **Muse** (`muse-spark-1.3-contributor`, high;
  `llp/reviews/3000.036.001-merged-pages.muse.md`): 1 the single-index lazy
  merge — accepted: `branch_merge.rs` and `branch_merge_lazy` (§3); 2 version
  4, `PagePosition` and error codes — accepted: the encoding is specified,
  pages record the shared suffix explicitly, and `E_CURSOR_INDEX` is stated as
  new in this path (§5); 3 grant-false lanes — accepted: they are not read
  and do not mark the page (§3), and the note skips grant lanes (§8); 4
  position proof — accepted: the binding check requires identical typed
  position columns ending in `id` (§3), tests cover overlap and both
  directions; 5 Lean — as GPT 5; 6 disclosure — accepted: §4 now states the
  token, lane-count and cross-page cases; 7 metering — as GPT 4; 8 device
  combination — accepted: per-lane coverage flags combine as for any query,
  and the device tests compare both stores (§7); 9 planning and IR — accepted:
  the binder's check and fallback (§3), the 8 rationale corrected (§2), no IR
  field (§11); 10 exclusions — accepted: one table (§8); 11 compat wording —
  as 2; 12 >8 fallback — accepted: single-index plan with its note, tested.

### Round 2 (revision 2, after the merge with 0.4.23)

Fresh blind reviews of revision 2 and its code at `1e01faac12`:
`llp/reviews/3000.036.001-merged-pages.r2.gpt.md` (NOT READY, seven
material) and `.r2.muse.md` (READY WITH CHANGES, five minor). Revision 3:

- **GPT 1** (a guard fed by an order-column pin lost 0.4.23's guard on
  fallback) — accepted: the grant lane is planned as the single-index page is,
  so the fallback is that plan, and a differing order declines the merge.
  Test `an_order_column_pin_a_guard_needs_keeps_the_guarded_single_read`
  (fails without the fix).
- **GPT 2** (an older engine cannot page a grant plan) — accepted as a
  documented limit, argued in §3: only an older device runs this IR, and a
  role or parent-probe guard's table is online only there, so its answer is
  unknown and the client asks the server; a seat's table is trusted.
- **GPT 3** (`now ± literal` overflows at the clock bound) — accepted: clock
  arithmetic is no longer total (§2). Test `clock_arithmetic_is_not_total`
  (fails without the fix).

  it and checks the grant lane's width and order (§9).

- **GPT 7** (Lean lacked the `exists` rewrite) — accepted: `existsRead` takes
  the branch verdict; example `exists-parties-false`.

  `a_cut_inside_filter_rejects_continues_from_the_frontier` (fails without the

- **Muse 2** (`in` and windows differ from Lean) — windows removed; `in` noted

- **Muse 5** (the grant half of coverage is unproved) — accepted as a
  statement in §9.
