## 14. PAGE: capped owner-or-membership pages

PAGE resolves Q6 for the common rule

```quarry
read <- .owner = viewer or exists members[.parent, viewer]
```

when the membership lookup names an exact unique key and the membership table
declares a finite cap whose grouping columns are fixed by the viewer or other
row-independent expressions. More formally, the read rule's top-level
disjunction has at least one principal-party equality and at least one such
membership probe, and every disjunct is one of those two forms. Every
qualifying probe has one row-dependent exact-key component naming an ordinary
target column (not `id`) that the page has not already pinned, and otherwise
fixed components; its cap group is wholly among the fixed components and its
read rule is decided true by them. The §2–§3 exclusions still apply: total
page predicates, shared order and total cursor suffix, portable keys and
labels, at most eight physical components in both source and target indexes,
and fallback without speculative indexes. The planner first lowers
bare booleans and negated booleans to equality pins, as it does for every
other intent plan. It emits the party lanes, one descriptor per membership
probe, and one system lane. Their static descriptor count remains at most
eight; each declared membership cap bounds its descriptor's dynamic fanout
separately and may itself be greater than eight.

The dynamic descriptor scans the membership range to completion before the
page may emit a row. Each distinct parent in that result opens a target range
pinned to that parent. The membership row is the witness for the rule branch,
so target rows in that range need no second membership probe; their ordinary
target coverage, horizon and expiry checks still apply. The owner and parent
ranges merge in the requested order and `id`, deduplicate overlaps, and share
one version-4/5 position. Source discovery and every opened target scan use
the query's one store transaction/snapshot, so a membership cannot be revoked
between proving a lane and reading it. The system lane reads the whole
original range and suppresses every other lane for a system context.

The cursor descriptor includes the static lanes and the membership source's
table and index. Its scope includes the evaluated static pins and the source
range's fixed viewer/argument pins. It deliberately does not digest the set of
parents returned by that range: membership rows, like target rows, may change
between requests. This is ordinary non-snapshot pagination. The next request
answers completeness for its current remaining range strictly after the
shared position; it makes no claim about rows at or before that position. A
new membership therefore contributes only currently readable rows after the
cursor, and a removed membership contributes no further rows. Schema or
fixed-principal changes still restart because they change the descriptor or
scope.

The backend carries `branch-membership-v1` in addition to `branch-merge-v1`.
An older device understands the static branch cursor but not the mandatory
discovery-before-output step, so it refuses this backend instead of attempting
the eager merge. Handshake negotiation uses the ordinary schema-capability
path.

Membership discovery is an all-or-nothing admission step. Source rows,
distinct-parent retention and every dynamic lane descriptor charge the
ordinary examined, step and heap meters. A meter cut, cap
latch, unknown presence, incomplete source coverage or source horizon before
the source range is known complete returns an empty incomplete page and no
continuation. Minting either a normal continuation or a skip continuation at
that point could omit an undiscovered parent whose first row sorts before an
already emitted owner row. Once discovery finishes, ordinary per-range meter
cuts use the §6 frontier and may return a safe continuation.

The heap ledger applies to the whole membership merge, including owner and
system lane buffers as well as dynamic target buffers. It reserves source
distinct sets, evaluated prefixes and bounds, merge-position and heap slots,
deduplication, the emitted-page vector, frontier decode, and cursor/page-record
scratch before allocation. A source distinct key is first sized by the
allocation-free canonical JSON writer, then charged and encoded into one
bounded buffer of exactly that requested capacity; escaped parent text cannot
cause geometric growth after admission. Compiled scans remain shared; dynamic
descriptors borrow one compiled target scan rather than cloning its filter tree
per parent.

On a device the membership source and every opened target range retain their
normal per-scan coverage decisions. PAGE is an explicit exception to §7's
blanket synced-union exclusion for this proved source shape: it has a
system-only lane rather than a probe-grant lane, so acquired membership and
target union groups can use the same plan and produce the same complete page
as the server. An online or unacquired source makes discovery unknown and
returns the empty incomplete answer above. An unheld or horizon-incomplete
target range makes the result incomplete and triggers the ordinary server
fallback; no partial device result is marked complete. The plan does not
treat a merely uniform denial as an empty membership set: binding validates
that the source pins decide its read rule true. Expiry from membership
discovery joins target expiry at the existing minimum.

The planner and Lean classifier mirror point-probe resolution before accepting
the branch. A positional probe selects the one declared unique whose ordered
column types accept its arguments; equal-arity keys with different types do not
make it ambiguous, while multiple matching keys still refuse the positional
spelling. Named probe columns are duplicate-free and contain every column of a
duplicate-free declared unique, additional columns are fixed equalities, and a
cap covers the fixed discovery prefix. The descriptor bound counts each party
column once, matching runtime party-lane deduplication while retaining every
distinct membership branch. Its soundness lemma says every emitted
party or parent lane row is admitted under the branch-witness assumption; the
logical theorem does not prove that runtime discovery establishes that
witness. Its coverage lemma splits every admitted row into a party witness or
membership witness. Differential cases include
caps above eight and overlap. Runtime meter regressions cover discovery cuts;
simulator regressions include revocation between pages and missing acquisition;
server and both device stores exercise the same shared-cursor walk.

The implicit `byId` also proves an exact named probe containing `id`; any
additional named components remain required residual equalities. This does not
invent a multi-part positional key: positional probes still resolve through
their typed declared unique, apart from the ordinary single-ID spelling.

A fixed named component may remain an exact equality in the discovery scan's
residual filter rather than its capped prefix, as in
`members[id = 'm1', parent = .parent, principal = viewer]`. PAGE accepts that
component only when the equality is a necessary conjunct over the source row;
an alternative or computed implication is not witness evidence. The retained
filter therefore restricts discovery to `m1`, and a row with the same parent
and principal under another id cannot open a target lane. The planner checks
that every fixed probe component survives as a prefix pin or such a residual
before committing candidate indexes, and the bound-IR validator repeats the
same check without copying the filter. A denial, missing witness, expired
witness, incomplete source horizon or unsupported residual cannot expose an
unproved target lane. Uniform denial and unsupported residuals keep the
ordinary fallback without speculative PAGE indexes. A missing or expired
witness opens no lane under complete discovery, so the owner merge remains
complete; an incomplete source horizon returns the empty incomplete result
described above.

Fixed scalar equalities compare their canonical values across the rule and
expression representations. In particular, unary minus applied to an integer
literal is canonicalized to that negative integer before PAGE validates the
residual and before candidate indexes are committed. Thus `slot = -1` is the
same necessary residual in planning, bound validation and the Lean model; it
does not cause a late fallback that leaves speculative indexes behind.

The intrinsic source `id` is always such a residual, even when fixed. It stays
in the exact logical witness and its equality must hold, but it is not a
discovery-prefix component and cannot satisfy the discovery cap. Thus `cap by
id` alone keeps the ordinary incomplete scan; a separate cap over fixed
discovery columns such as `principal` is required for PAGE.

An optional discovered parent may be `null` only when the exact point probe
identifies the source row without that parent component. For example,
`members[id = 'm1', parent = .parent, principal = viewer]` addresses `m1`
through `byId` and checks `parent = null` as a residual equality, so a matching
source row opens one null-parent target range. Discovery uses its ordinary
null-covering physical index, including the stored row-id suffix, and applies
the fixed residual before distinct-parent deduplication. If the varying parent
is instead a component of the probe's nullable unique key, a null argument has
the ordinary point result of no row and cannot open a PAGE lane. The same is
true when any other component of that point key is null. This rule neither
turns nullable uniques into total uniques nor changes their storage or
collision semantics.

A varying source field may be the discovered parent even when it is not the
next physical index component, provided the fixed discovery prefix covers a
plain total unique index. Such a prefix selects at most one source row, so PAGE
may project any field from that row: the implicit `id` in
`groups[id = .parent, principal = viewer]`, or an ordinary `parent` in
`groups[parent = .parent, principal = viewer]` under `unique principal`.
The total unique may be a proper subset of the probe's fixed source columns:
`unique principal` also admits
`groups[parent = .parent, slot = 0, principal = viewer]`. PAGE keeps `slot =
0` as an exact residual on the selected source row; a missing row, `slot =
null`, or any other slot does not open a target range. The provisional wider
discovery index is discarded before requests are committed. When several
covered total uniques exist, PAGE chooses only among points whose prefix
independently proves the source read rule and the discovery cap; an earlier
`unique a` cannot displace an eligible `unique principal` by moving
`principal = viewer` into the residual.
The projected field may be optional under the null-witness rule above. The
source cap, readability, complete-discovery, resource and horizon checks still
apply. Every component of the one-row unique itself must be non-optional; a
nullable unique does not supply this proof and declines before PAGE indexes
are committed. In all other cases the parent remains the next physical
source-index component.

The Lean surface gate prices the same physical PAGE candidate before invoking
the logical oracle. Equality-prefix columns use the planner's canonical sorted
order followed by the exact scan-order suffix; the permutation allowed for an
extremum is not available to a PAGE discovery scan. A total declared unique in
that exact order may omit the trailing physical `id`, because its components
already identify the row. If two declared indexes both match the candidate and
the author selected neither, native optional planning declines the ambiguous
PAGE candidate and the emitter reports `page-membership-index-selection`
instead of asking an index-free logical model to choose one. Component, byte or
label overflow reports `page-membership-key-bound`; a reused nullable unique
that cannot prove the varying-`id` one-row case reports
`page-membership-source-unique`. Each stops before the index-free model.

## 15. PAGE2: ordinary exact memberships, row ids and parent owners

Wave 10 showed that §14's cap requirement excluded the ordinary membership
schema it was meant to serve:

```quarry
table members:
  parent: parents
  principal: principal
  unique parent, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer

table documents:
  owner: principal
  parent: parents
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists members[.parent, viewer]
```

`unique parent, principal` proves that the rule's point probe has at most one
witness for one document and caller. It does **not** prove that a principal has
only finitely many membership rows: fixing `principal` leaves arbitrarily many
`parent` values. PAGE2 therefore does not call this source range statically
capped. It admits the exact total-unique witness and runs the inverted
`members where .principal = viewer` discovery under the ordinary read meter.
The §14 discovery fence already gives the required failure semantics: until
the source range is known complete, no owner or membership target row is
emitted; an `examined`, `index_entries`, `rule_probes` or discovery `steps`
cut, unknown device coverage or incomplete horizon returns an empty
`complete: false` page with no continuation. `heap_bytes` exhaustion retains
the existing `E_BOUND` read refusal. Every
distinct value, evaluated prefix and dynamic lane is sized and charged through
§14's heap ledger before allocation, so an uncapped population cannot allocate
past the request ceiling. Retrying a read-meter cut may complete under a larger
budget or smaller population. It cannot skip an undiscovered lane or turn
partial discovery into a negative authorization fact.

This admission proves a safe plan, not completion for every population size.
For a caller with `K` memberships, discovery and target-head work grow with
`K`; once a read ceiling is crossed the fenced result is empty and incomplete,
so a sufficiently large audience can be temporarily unavailable under the
ordinary budget. A cap is therefore optional for correctness and remains the
product's availability bound. If an app must guarantee that the page completes
within a chosen budget, the writer that grants memberships must enforce a cap
on the fixed enrollment group (for example `principal`). A writer allowed to
grant arbitrary memberships controls `K`; point uniqueness alone does not.

A declared cap is a separate static proof only when all of its grouping
columns are fixed by the discovery scan and null exemption cannot apply. Any
maximum is admissible: a cap above the request's examined ceiling may still
cut safely under the preceding rule. A cap grouped by `parent` does not bound a
scan that fixes only `principal`; a cap grouped by `principal` does. A fully
pinned total unique remains the one-row case. Planner, bound-IR validator and
Lean emitter use these same alternatives:

1. the rule probe selects the intrinsic `id` or one declared plain unique,
   and every other probe component is an exact necessary equality;
2. either that selected point is total, the discovery itself is a fully pinned
   total unique, or a declared cap covers the fixed discovery prefix;
3. the fixed pins decide the source read rule true, and all existing index,
   key, label, descriptor, total-predicate and shared-position checks pass.

A nullable original unique is not a total point proof. It can still use PAGE
when discovery pins every column of a separate total unique, or an applicable
cap bounds its discovery group. For example, nullable `unique parent, principal`
remains the positional rule probe while required `unique principal` bounds the
inverted `principal = viewer` discovery to one row. This does not rewrite the
original probe or its null semantics: a null in that selected point retains the
ordinary result of no witness. If a cap is the only missing condition,
`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` gives the exact declaration to add, using the source's
fixed non-null columns: for the usual shape, `cap 50 by principal`; with no
fixed group, `cap 50`. The note also says that a cap is a stored write
constraint: adding it can refuse existing adoption or later inserts once that
group holds 50 live rows (and physically retained rows where cap validation
requires them), so the author should add it only when it is a real product
limit. The note is withheld when a cap cannot repair the
shape, including multiple row-dependent components, a hidden source, a
non-exact or ambiguous probe, a multi-hop target, incompatible ordering, or a
physical key bound. It must not prescribe code that still cannot plan.

The varying target column may now be the target row's own `id`:

```quarry
read <- .owner = viewer or exists shares[document = .id, principal = viewer]
```

Discovery yields document ids, and each dynamic target lane pins `id`. A
one-row id range is trivially ordered, but it still reports the page's shared
logical position, for example `.at, id`: the row supplies `.at`, and `id` is
the final tie-breaker just as in every other lane. All lanes therefore share
the existing version-4/5 cursor and frontier rules. Before any row is emitted,
the engine opens every discovered id lane and reads its one possible head. If
an `examined`, `index_entries`, `rule_probes` or `steps` cut leaves even one
unfinished lane without a head/`through`, §6's frontier is absent and the page
is empty, incomplete and has no continuation: no returned row can sort ahead
of an unread point. Only
after every point lane is exhausted or has a head can the heap emit the true
`.at, id` prefix. Heap exhaustion remains `E_BOUND` as above. The implicit
`byId` is a valid exact source point too, which admits the parent-owner form

```quarry
read <- .owner = viewer or exists offers[id = .offer, baker = viewer]
```

when `baker = viewer` decides the offer source readable. Its inverted source
scan uses or synthesizes the plain `baker, id` discovery order (within the
existing width, byte, label and ambiguity checks), discovers the caller's
offer ids in the same transaction snapshot, and target lanes pin `.offer`; it
does not require `cap N by baker`. Both forms preserve the rule's exact
witness, snapshot, expiry, device-coverage and meter requirements.

Discovery and target scans use their ordinary lifetime filter. A source row
before `not before` is not a witness and opens no lane; a future target row is
not a lane head and cannot be emitted early. PAGE contributes no lifetime
bypass or separate clock. Source and target opening commits invalidate the
ordinary watched table revisions; expiry joins the existing minimum deadline.
No opening timestamp is exported in the cursor or `nextExpiry`.

The schema requires `branch-membership-v2` when a membership operation uses a
metered discovery source or pins the target row's intrinsic `id`. The optional
operation fact preserves that classification across schema serialization;
the existing `branch-membership-v1` remains required for every membership
merge, so capped §14 plans remain v1-compatible. A v1-only engine or device
refuses a PAGE2 backend by the ordinary capability handshake instead of
applying its older admission validator or downgrading the plan to an eager
merge.

The Lean classifier admits row-`id` targets and the same three source proofs:
a total original point probe, fully pinned total-unique discovery, or an
applicable cap. The fully pinned proof is checked on the discovery pins and
does not replace a nullable original positional probe. Its logical `ownerMembership_sound` and
`ownerMembership_cover` theorems remain about the exact rule leaves; executable
examples add uncapped composite membership, row-id membership and parent-owner
pages. Native, Lean and device differential generators draw the same forms,
including source populations that cross the examined meter and must return the
empty incomplete discovery result, and id populations whose target-head phase
cuts before every lane opens. The language, simulator and both device stores
walk each form through one shared cursor and test overlap de-duplication.

### PAGE2 implementation and qualification — 2026-10-13

§15 is implemented at `24a644c511fc5a6888597af9335d894790eaeb97`. Both
independent [Muse](./reviews/3000.036-page2.r12.muse.md) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.036-page2.r12.codex.md) mark this amendment READY,
using the same frozen §15 and implementation checkpoint. This records the
amendment's implementation/readiness; it leaves the parent RFC's legacy Review
status intact and makes no author-acceptance or publication claim.

The concrete fully pinned discovery-unique parity case passes after its old
model control fails completion. The intrinsic-id competing-unique case passes
after its old planner control has no merge. The discovery step sweep passes
after positive old-code ceilings 2/3/6/8/11/12/14/15 produce E_BOUND. Final
checks: language page2 membership 5/5 and branch merge 57/57; Lean difftests 32/32;
smoke 17/17; Lean 58-theorem standard-axiom audit, 71/71 examples, 7/7 fixtures;
core discovery 1/1 and meter 9/9; simulator membership 8/8; device branch merge 12/12
on Memory/SQLite. Heap and non-step/pending errors retain their semantics.

Final 400-case native/device/Lean campaigns have no new signatures. Native
20,619 and device 13,114 executions have zero disagreements; Lean 1,072
executions has only three instances of its two pre-existing classified
pagination signatures. Additional generated native seed 715 and Lean 551
actually compare the discovery-unique shape, with zero signatures; its
uncovered device source remains unknown/incomplete. Full results and commands
are in [PAGE2 evidence](../snapback4/reports/20261013-page2/README.md).

All 50 copied odd wave-10 final schemas compile. `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` falls from 20 notes in 13
schemas to 18 notes in 11 schemas ; 001 incomingRequests and 037 myChildren clear, and named
even 002/098 each clear their sole note. The remaining hidden-source, pure
membership, role-grant and compound shapes are explicitly excluded; no claim
is made that the raw 20-note corpus or all membership-shaped notes met the
large-majority target. [Final survey](../snapback4/reports/20261013-page2/final-schema-survey.md)
binds source/binary/output hashes at the final semantic checkpoint.

The clean Linux web artifact at `10d27f62cc` is 2,305,135 B raw / 703,255 B gzip /
531,512 B brotli, budget 703,755 B = measured gzip + 500 B. Serializer-family and
forbidden-absence audits and locked wasm/client builds pass. No push.

## 16. PAGE3: finish the ordinary private-page plans

Commissioned 2026-10-13; implemented locally. Muse and fresh Codex both
READY in round 5 at `65d505289f`; verbatim reviews and frozen brief are
`llp/reviews/3000.036-page3.r5.{muse,codex,brief}.md`.
PAGE2 left 18 notes in 11 of the 50 odd wave-10 final schemas: 12
party/static-grant pages, three compound rules, two hidden parent sources
and one pure membership. This amendment extends the existing plan rather
than asking builders to merge lists themselves. It changes no read authority,
replicated data or runtime resource ceiling.

**Static grants and whole-group reads.** An owner-or-readable-static-grant
rule uses the existing party/grant merge on synced union tables too. The
server evaluates the positive readable grant once; its holder reads the
original whole range, everyone else the party ranges. An explicit `first` or
`last` is a page bound, not a table cap: a role holder may page an unbounded
table, under the usual request meter. An implicit whole-group read is expanded
only if its original fixed prefix has an applicable stored cap or fully
pinned total unique, at or below the examined ceiling. That original bound
applies to the union. A cap on owner does not bound a staff's whole table.
A fully pinned total unique may supply a one-row lane with the implicit id
position, even when its physical index has no unpinned component.

**Full union devices.** The same descriptor and scope serve server and device
cursors. On a device a full union target's grant lane reads its held authorized
rows, with the ordinary acquisition, coverage and meter checks; it does not
probe a remote grant. That held range covers every other target lane, so the
others are not opened. Missing acquisition stays unknown. Online-only and
single-arm narrowed sync retain their existing grant deferral. This amends
LLP 3000.036.000.000 §5.3's planner restriction, while preserving its holding
and acquisition invariants.

**Pure membership.** Remove the syntactic party requirement from §15. A rule
`exists members[.id, viewer]` or `exists members[.parent, viewer]` has a system
range and exact membership descriptors, no party descriptors. All source
readability, unique/cap proofs, total filters, index/key bounds, shared
positions and discovery/head fences remain. If the source discovery is cut,
no target rows or cursor are emitted. An empty complete discovery means an
empty complete page. Point uniqueness still does not cap memberships.

**Positive compound rules.** Distribute positive `and`/`or` into at most seven
alternatives plus the original system range. Each alternative retains all
of its row equality conditions. It contains either one exact membership
witness, or row equality pins and positive row-independent guards. A membership
conjunct supplies precisely its own authority; all remaining conjuncts must
be true by the target lane's pins. A pinned membership target instead uses
a readable page guard, without discovering hidden source rows. No negated
probe, arithmetic, fallible predicate or second varying source component is
admitted. Candidates are speculative and fall back when the physical plan
or bound-IR validator cannot prove the whole admitted shape.

A new guarded-range descriptor evaluates `page_guard_decision` on its own
pins. A true readable guard opens only that range, not the other alternatives;
a false guard opens none. It does not cover other lanes. The system range
covers every lane only for the system. Status/party lanes, discovered parent
lanes and guarded lanes share one ordered id-tied position and deduplicate
there, as in §§3–6. Thus if `R = ∨i (Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi)` and W is the total page
predicate, each lane returns exactly `W ∧ Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi`; their union is W ∧ R.
Guard facts and membership sources must be readable, as in §15, so neither
empty results nor completeness reveal hidden witness rows. Read-meter cuts
and frontier rules are unchanged; heap exhaustion remains a refusal.

A nullable small enum's `field != null` has a finite positive expansion into
its declared non-null values. This retains null exclusion exactly; ordinary
nullable inequalities against other values keep their original scan. The
wave-10 archive with four species therefore gets four enum ranges plus its
owner, readable reviewer and system ranges. Compound volunteer-open and
parent-teacher/status rules preserve each status condition when opening lanes.

**Readable static predicates.** A `where` disjunction may repeat a readable
static point, such as `.gleaner = viewer or exists coordinators[viewer]`.
Every point-key expression must be an infallible, read-free name or literal
(including canonical signed numeric literals); arithmetic, assertions, calls
and member reads keep the original scan and short circuit. The point must be
a total unique with fixed terms, readable by those pins or
its identical self-witness rule, without a lifetime or read-through boundary.
It remains in the original predicate for every caller, including the system.
This extension belongs to static party/where branch merges only. Membership
discovery and compound-rule expansion retain their read-free total filter
requirement, mirrored separately by model cases 3 and 4. The physical point
can cut even though its logical predicate is total. The
branch frontier therefore excludes an interrupted filter candidate; it may
continue only from an earlier fully evaluated candidate, so retry cannot
skip the interrupted row. A source its holders cannot read keeps its scan.

A discovered/guard-admitted merge range pays its admission reservation once
on both runtime hosts, including empty ranges. Device acquired-row trust does
not add a second prefix-rule reservation to an already admitted lane.

**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v2` fences guarded descriptors,
held-union merge behavior and fully pinned point-lane admission; it is derived
from bound plans, serialized as an optional operation fact, and older engines
refuse it through the ordinary handshake. Existing descriptor bytes remain
unchanged when none of these new lane types is present. Membership-only plans
retain the existing membership capability rules. The Lean surface model
removes its party requirement, and models the admitted positive rule ranges.
Enums refuse the entire model module with the existing explicit `type-enum`
reason before any program is declared supported. Compound where-branch
composition, nullable constant pins and physically nonportable/ambiguous
compound plans have their own named emitter exclusions. Native static
predicate keys that are arguments or signed expression nodes use the named
`page-static-key-expression` model boundary until their separate readability
classifier is extended; they are unsupported, never counted as oracle passes;
unsupported fragments remain explicitly named rather than accepted by the
oracle. Its logical leaf soundness/coverage proof includes pure membership.

**Hidden-source boundary and exact repairs.** PAGE3 does not scan private
parent rows whose owner cannot necessarily read them. No cap/index alone
repairs that absence proof. Copy the parent's immutable owner onto the child,
keep that copy and its parent reference immutable, keep the parent non-expiring,
and retain the reference with `on delete restrict`, and check the copy against the parent in the child's
insert rule (and in every trusted seed/backfill). Replace the parent-owner
read-rule leaf with the copied owner comparison. The copy preserves authority
only while the parent owner is immutable; a mutable owner needs transactional
propagation or the original rule. Existing data must be backfilled before
adoption. The diagnostic names the actual table, reference and owner column
and supplies exact source edits. It never recommends making parents public.

**PAGE3 qualification and review dispositions (2026-10-14).** Round 1
repaired null-exempt bounds, parent reference/lifetime preservation, outer
conjunction guards and capability scope; round 2 repaired fallible static keys,
block-lowered probe fencing and defensive model bounds. Round 3 named static
argument/signed-key model exclusions, with zero-oracle Unsupported regressions.
Round 4 separated readable-static model filters from the read-free gates for
membership/compound plans, with actual native/model data and completeness
comparisons. Round 5's two independent reviewers marked the same revision
READY. No unsupported model case is counted as a pass.

All 50 copied odd wave-10 final schemas compile: 18 notes in 11 schemas
become 2 in one (both hidden parents). Applying the exact checked copy repair
to a further copied corpus gives 0 in all 50. Per-shape counts, input/output
hashes and source edits are in `snapback4/reports/20261013-page3/`. Final
400-case campaigns have 22,277 native and 12,686 device executions without
disagreements; Lean's 1,108 executions retain only the three existing C2
pagination occurrences, with no new signature. Lean audits 59 theorems with
standard axioms only, runs 74 examples and seven fixtures. Focused language,
model, simulator and both device-store suites pass.

The clean locked Linux web artifact at `f11a5e72cd` is 2,310,393 B raw /
704,662 B gzip / 532,356 B brotli; budget 705,162 B = measured gzip + 500 B.
Family/absence audits and wasm/client builds pass. Later edits affect only
language/model/tests, preserving these device bytes. No push or publication.

## 17. PAGE4: complete multi-audience pages and exact scopes

Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-11 fix #11. This amendment extends the
existing bounded planner. No read authority, replicated audience, runtime
ceiling or cursor codec changes. Query unions containing a public status
are separate from union *sync*: a public sync branch remains excluded.

**Mixed positive alternatives.** Positive distribution also applies to a
simple disjunction mixing party equality, public/status equality, exact
membership and readable static grants. Each alternative retains every
conjunct and the complete original query predicate. Equality lanes,
guarded ranges and exact discovered membership lanes share the existing
ordered id-tied merge, with duplicate positions emitted once. At most seven
alternatives plus the system range fit. Speculative source/target indexes
are retained only when the bound validator accepts the whole plan.

Named plain page indexes normalize to their declared column order only in
a speculative merge candidate. A declined candidate keeps its original read.
An exact owner/member rule whose varying component is necessarily pinned
can use the existing party/guard merge; alternative-only pins do not qualify.

**Exact where witnesses.** A positive whole `where` conjunct identical to a
whole read-rule disjunct can define an exact narrower audience: for example
`posts where exists authors[.id, viewer] first 20 by .at after c`. Discover
that readable witness under §§14–16's total point and complete-discovery
proofs. Other audiences cannot contribute rows to W. Every other conjunct
must be read-free and total. The original predicate remains in admission
validation; the system descriptor retains the authored witness, because
system bypasses R but never W. Its prefix also binds the fixed target pins
into the shared cursor scope. A hidden source keeps the original scan.

**Pinned components of exact witnesses.** A positive alternative may pin
one component of a multi-column rule probe. Substitute only a necessary
infallible fixed equality, leaving one varying source component; the core
validator independently checks the original field term against that exact
target prefix. Example: `.manager = viewer and exists reporting[report =
.report, manager = .manager]` discovers reporting rows with manager=viewer,
then their report values. The other party's alternative inverts the other
component. Extra named point equalities survive. No arithmetic, missing
pin, second varying component or hidden source is treated as proof.

**Parent snapshots.** Selecting `s.id` or a stored scalar field of a required
point-read row image is a fixed lexical value, not a new row read. In a
standalone page's predicate (returned or assigned), the compiler binds those
selections once before planning. Optional rows, reference hops, private/maintained fields,
rebindings and joined paths keep the original predicate. A snapshot does
not prove that a child's copied band/manager equals its parent's field;
source repairs must say when their extra pins narrow the authored scope.

**Membership word search.** The existing compact ordinary server search may
derive its exact two-component total membership audience on an online-only
table too. Provision the reverse readable source index and scoped words
index in one checked speculative candidate. Synced targets retain their
same exact audience requirement. The device program and sync holdings do
not acquire a predicate; empty needles retain the original browse arm.
Readability, non-expiring source, portable keys, opening meter refusal,
word-head merge and original cursor binding remain LLP 3000.028 §3.3's.

**Honest incomplete forms.** A fallback note distinguishes an analyzer
limitation (the current admitted ranges cannot prove this positive shape)
from a truly partial read (its range/source cannot establish absence under
its declared authority). It names the exact complete line when one can be
verified; scope-narrowing pins say that they narrow the answer. A hidden
parent retains PAGE3's checked copy/backfill repair. No cap/index-only
suggestion is offered for an unreadable witness, and no copied relationship
is assumed from a successful parent read alone.

**Readable negative static points and finite enums.** A negated static
probe is a page guard only when its selected key is total unique, its source
is readable at that key and has no maintained-state, read lifetime or read-through boundary.
Its absence is then an observable authorized fact. A hidden negative probe
still cannot open a complete range. Required enum inequality against a
literal expands to its exact remaining variants; optional enum inequality
against a non-null value stays residual. No varying negative source is
inverted. This completes the renovation selectionsFor page (its unpaged budget aggregation
retains an analyzer limitation) without
turning timed privacy into an unsupported absence claim.

A discovered/guard-admitted merge range pays its admission reservation once
on both runtime hosts, including empty ranges. Device acquired-row trust does
not add a second prefix-rule reservation to an already admitted lane.

**Compatibility and model.** Existing descriptor bytes and semantics serve
mixed unions and exact where witnesses. `branch-pages-v3` fences pinned multi-column witnesses, readable negative
static guarded/covering ranges and the compiler-owned `RequireUncapped`
primitive before an older validator or decoder can encounter them. A nullable
row assertion in a query checks the read meter after evaluating its point;
a cut raises the internal E_BOUND that the query boundary reports as an
unavailable incomplete answer, instead of inventing the author's absence error.
Ordinary absent/hidden rows still use the authored error, and mutation
assertions retain their transactional policy.
The independent Lean surface classifier must reproduce positive mixed
ranges, pinned witness substitution and exact where scope. Static snapshot
binding is an explicit syntax normalization, like where-guard lowering.
Word search stays a named unsupported model fragment; it must execute zero
Lean oracle cases, while native/device tests cover its existing words engine.
The direct-grant soundness theorem remains positive: negative-grant scalar
absence/aggregation, fallible-filter fallback and descriptor overflow have the
explicit `page-negative-grant-fallback` model boundary, including negative
probes nested in conjunctions and made static by prefix pins, and overflow of
distributed alternatives inside conjunctions, with zero oracle
executions. Standard bounded negative-grant pages use the independent range
classifier and are exercised against the model. No unsupported case is counted
as a pass. Fresh Muse and Codex reviews both mark semantic revision
`193f94ea9da7a046f4ce6fe76d7b527e1686ef90` READY. See the
[verbatim paired reviews and provenance](reviews/3000.036-page4.provenance.md).
Qualification is recorded separately in the PAGE4 workstream report.

Model fidelity: lexical witness pins use an opaque classifier-only parameter
term, preserving variable identity instead of converting observed argument
values to literals or viewer proofs. The oracle rejects that term in authored
rule JSON; executable authority always evaluates the original rule. Named
plain orders normalize only within the independent unfiltered mixed-merge
fragment. Filtered/one-party named candidates outside that fragment (including declined
native candidates) have the explicit
`page-named-index-fallback` boundary and execute zero oracle cases. No such excluded candidate is claimed to be a native failure. This
retains native fallback semantics instead of claiming a complete owner pin
on an authored fixed index. Both review counterexamples have regressions;
the generator includes argument-valued pinned pairs.

Delivered-history integration: an acquired device history is an audience in
its own right under LLP 3000.007 §18.2, even after live membership expires.
A membership merge on such a target selects its existing whole-range system
descriptor on the device without changing the actual viewer. It retains the
authored W, acquisition, lifetime, retention, meters and shared cursor scope;
only current-source rediscovery is bypassed. Server execution is unchanged.
`branch-pages-v3` fences this device interpretation. Existing expiry/paged
snapshot history regressions run on both stores. NARR retains ownership of
P9's general unbounded-sync/empty-partition narration; this compatibility
correction arose from PAGE4 named-order planning.

## 18. PAGE5: bounded products, fixed guards and exact vacancies

Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-12 fix #12. PAGE5 extends the bounded PAGE
planner only where every admitted lane still has a constructive exhaustion
argument. It does not turn arbitrary Boolean authorization into a complete
query plan.

**Bounded products.** The planner distributes independent positive
alternatives in the authorization rule across conjunctions before it
classifies the resulting lanes.
For example, two exact principal alternatives and three exact status
alternatives produce six lanes, each retaining both selected predicates and
the original query predicate. Distribution is all-or-nothing and remains
inside the existing eight-lane limit, including the system lane. Overflow,
negative alternatives and a residual second varying witness retain the
analyzer-limitation fallback.

The same bounded Cartesian construction applies to positive membership PAGE
query filters. Every resulting branch must contain an eligible equality pin,
the system descriptor plus every party or membership descriptor must remain
within eight, and every original predicate stays in its lane. A product that
pins the membership witness's varying outer field, overflows, or leaves a
branch without a usable pin stays on the ordinary incomplete scan. The Lean
classifier constructs every factor before applying those same exclusions; it
never treats a failed product as an undistributed complete lane or a second
`or` predicate as absent. A non-alternative equality or required bare Boolean
shared by every product branch supplies an eligible pin to each branch.
Repeated party leaves name one physical audience prefix and count once per
product branch.

**Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
be accompanied by one or more fixed `exists` guards, independent of authored
conjunction grouping. A fixed guard is admissible only when
the lane pins every field term in its target key and the target exposes a
required, total, unique point that is caller-readable after those exact lane
pins are substituted. The target must have no hidden source, read-through boundary,
`not before` boundary or expiry. The compiler substitutes the exact lane pin
into the guard and keeps that complete guard in the generated query filter;
an already-static point such as `exists roles[viewer]` needs no substitution
but remains in that filter. The residual may contain three or more fixed
guards; its exact short-circuit conjunction chain remains necessary in full.
The core validator independently matches it to the original rule expression
and lane pin. Thus the generated guard observes every fixed grant that could
authorize the authored rule. A hidden, timed, non-total or extra-equality
guard remains incomplete. Two varying grant sources also remain outside this
fragment.

A standalone static grant disjunct remains its ordinary guarded lane beside
party and membership lanes. It is not a residual sibling of the membership
witness: if it admits the caller, that guarded lane reads the whole authored
range; otherwise it contributes no rows. Every standalone static alternative
must independently expose a required, total, unique, timeless and
caller-readable point before any guarded lane is admitted. A different
readable disjunct cannot cover a hidden or timed alternative. Only a static
point in the same conjunction as another atom enters the retained-filter
proof above. This distinction preserves complete `static grant or membership`
and `party or static grant or membership` pages without dropping any
alternative or conjunct.

**Exact vacancy lanes.** The server may invert a top-level
`not exists holds[.id]` alternative when `holds.id` is a required, unique,
plain point and the paged table is online-only. Its vacancy lane scans the
same full ordered outer range as the system lane and privately authorizes each
candidate against the original rule; it publishes completion only after that
authorized walk is exhausted. A predicate-meter or authorization-walk cut
therefore produces an incomplete page. Filtered vacancy reads, synchronized
outer tables, compound or non-`id` keys, non-unique points and any shortcut
around the ordinary per-row authorization check keep the fallback. The target
may itself be hidden or timed: the private server walk evaluates the authored
rule's system probe for each candidate, then exposes only the authorized outer
row. This is a vacancy predicate over the outer row, not a claim that a private
target's absence is globally observable.
The intrinsic `id` point is always required and unique even though schema
column and unique lists omit it; other vacancy keys require both declarations.

**Index rewrite note.** When every missing rule field is already fixed by an
independent exact query equality, the note prints one concrete declaration
using the actual table and collision-free index name, followed by the complete
rewritten query using that same index. The rewrite locates the focal read by
its source-map span, even when an earlier read uses the same index name, and
the binder recompiles the printed declaration and query together in tests.
It omits the repair when the added components or emitted label would exceed
the portable index bounds, or when authored bracket keys would change column
meaning under the prepended prefix.
When those exact equalities do not establish the rule prefix, the note
does not offer this rewrite.

**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v4` fences the new validator
interpretations: fixed residual point guards and exact vacancy lanes. A device
that only understands `branch-pages-v3` must refuse this IR before decoding or
executing it; bounded products reuse the already validated positive-branch
lanes, and existing PAGE4 plans keep their prior capability. The Lean surface
classifier mirrors the three admissions and the private authorization walk.
Generated differential cases exercise the new lane families on native,
simulator and device hosts. Forms outside the fragment remain honest analyzer
limitations rather than completeness claims.

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! Bounded positive DNF of a read rule. Each conjunct is retained in its
//! range; a guard opens only that range, never the other rule branches.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#16 — complete compound pages use the same bounded merge and discovery fence.
use super::{catalog::TableInfo, intent::{self, Requests}, intent_branches as branches};
use crate::ast::*;
use snapback4_core::ir::{BRANCH_LANE, GUARDED_LANE, SYSTEM_LANE, VACANCY_LANE};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

type Tables = BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>;

/// Comparisons and readable static points have a total logical predicate.
/// A physical point can still cut: the merge frontier excludes an unfinished
/// filter candidate, so continuation retries it rather than skipping it.
pub(super) fn total_readable(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, focal: &TableInfo, value: &Expr) -> bool {
    if branches::total(value) { return true; }
    match value {
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" || op == "or" =>
            total_readable(tables, synthesized, focal, left) && total_readable(tables, synthesized, focal, right),
        Expr::Exists { read: probe, .. } if branches::columns_within(value, &[]) => {
            if probe.range.is_some() || probe.filter.is_some() || probe.matching.is_some() || !probe.seeks.is_empty() { return false; }
            if !probe.keys.iter().all(|key| fixed_key_total(&key.value)) { return false; }
            let Some(target) = tables.get(&probe.table) else { return false };
            if target.through.is_some() || target.decl.not_before.is_some() || target.decl.expire.is_some() { return false; }
            let Some(columns) = point_columns(tables, synthesized, focal, probe) else { return false };
            if !target.indexes.iter().any(|index| target.total_unique(index) && index.columns.iter().all(|column| columns.contains(column))) { return false; }
            let mut point = probe.clone();
            for (key, column) in point.keys.iter_mut().zip(columns) { key.column = Some(column); }
            target.decl.public.is_some() || branches::read_rule(target).is_some_and(|source_rule|
                branches::by_form(source_rule, &point) || crate::print::expression(source_rule) == crate::print::expression(value))
        }
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// Resolve a point whose declared unique key is positional while additional
/// fixed equalities name columns (`roles[viewer, role = 'advisor']`). Named
/// equalities constrain the point but do not widen its identity key.
fn point_columns(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, focal: &TableInfo, probe: &ReadExpr) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    if probe.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some()) {
        return Some(probe.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.clone()).collect());
    }
    let mut positional = probe.clone();
    positional.keys.retain(|key| key.column.is_none());
    let mut resolved = super::intent_lanes::probe_columns(tables, synthesized, focal, &positional)?.into_iter();
    probe.keys.iter().map(|key| key.column.clone().or_else(|| resolved.next())).collect()
}

/// Distribution must not evaluate a fallible key on an overlapping party's
/// row where the original short circuit skipped it. Signed numeric literals
/// are bound constants; arithmetic, assertions, calls and reads stay put.
fn fixed_key_total(value: &Expr) -> bool {
    matches!(value, Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. })
        || matches!(value, Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "-" && matches!(value.as_ref(), Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Number(_), .. }))
}

pub(super) fn expand(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, requests: &mut Requests, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<MergeExpr> {
    let range = read.range.as_ref()?;
    let table = tables.get(&read.table)?;
    if read.index.is_some() || !read.keys.is_empty() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.computed.is_empty() || read.cursor_prefix.is_some()
        || table.maintained.is_some() || table.through.is_some() || table.decl.ephemeral.is_some() || !table.captures.is_empty() || table.decl.public.is_some()
        || read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(|filter| !branches::total(filter)) { return None; }
    let rule = branches::read_rule(table)?;
    if let Some(merge) = vacancy(tables, synthesized, requests, read) { return Some(merge); }
    // Keep the published simple plans and descriptors. This pass handles
    // compound rules, not the existing party/grant or exact membership rule.
    if !compound(rule) && !matches!(rule, Expr::Binary { op, .. } if op == "or") { return None; }
    let leaves = dnf(table, rule)?;
    if leaves.len() + 1 > branches::MAX_BRANCHES { return None; }
    let mut candidate = requests.clone();
    let errors = candidate.error_count();
    let mut lanes = Vec::new();
    let mut order = None;
    let mut system = read.clone(); candidate.read(tables, &mut system, false);
    if branches::decided(tables, &system) { return None; }
    branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, system, SYSTEM_LANE, None, None, read.filter.clone())?;
    for leaf in leaves {
        let mut pins = Vec::new();
        let mut probes = Vec::new();
        for atom in &leaf {
            if let Expr::Exists { .. } = atom {
                if branches::columns_within(atom, &[]) && leaf.len() == 1 {
                    // The core chooses one usable page guard for a guarded
                    // lane. Prove this particular omitted alternative too,
                    // so another readable static disjunct cannot conceal a
                    // hidden or timed one.
                    if !total_readable(tables, synthesized, table, atom) { return None; }
                } else {
                    // A static point beside another atom is a fixed guard too;
                    // omitting it from the retained residual would weaken the
                    // authored conjunction. A sole static point is instead the
                    // ordinary guarded lane for that top-level rule alternative.
                    probes.push(atom.clone());
                }
            } else if intent::equality(atom, table).is_some() {
                pins.push(atom.clone());
            } else if matches!(atom, Expr::Unary { .. }) && !branches::columns_within(atom, &[]) {
                // A bare negated boolean pin was handled above. Negated
                // probes are outside this positive distribution classifier.
                return None;
            } else if !branches::columns_within(atom, &[]) { return None; }
        }
        let mut lane_read = read.clone();
        if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { pins.push(*filter.clone()); }
        lane_read.filter = intent::conjunction(pins);
        // A conjunction may require one grant fixed by the authored page
        // prefix and one per-row grant discovered by PAGE.  Retain the fixed
        // point as an exact residual predicate on every discovered target
        // lane; the bound validator independently verifies that predicate
        // against the original rule before admitting the merge.
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — a pinned grant may guard one varying per-row grant.
        let mut fixed_read = lane_read.clone();
        let mut fixed_candidate = candidate.clone();
        fixed_candidate.plan_lane(tables, &mut fixed_read);
        let pinned: BTreeMap<_, _> = fixed_read.keys.iter().filter_map(|key|
            key.column.as_ref().filter(|_| fixed_key_total(&key.value)).map(|column| (column.clone(), key.value.clone()))).collect();
        let mut witness = None;
        let mut fixed_guards = Vec::new();
        for mut probe in probes {
            let fields: Vec<_> = match &probe { Expr::Exists { read, .. } => read.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| intent::column(&key.value)).collect(), _ => Vec::new() };
            // A point with no outer fields is already fixed; a point with
            // outer fields becomes fixed only after every field is pinned.
            if fields.iter().all(|field| pinned.contains_key(*field)) {
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut probe, &mut |value| {
                    if let Some(replacement) = intent::column(value).and_then(|column| pinned.get(column)) { *value = replacement.clone(); }
                });
                // The residual runs as an ordinary authored read, while the
                // rule probe itself has system visibility.  They are
                // equivalent only for an exact, timeless point the caller is
                // structurally allowed to read.
                if !total_readable(tables, synthesized, table, &probe) { return None; }
                fixed_guards.push(probe);
            } else if witness.replace(probe).is_some() { return None; }
        }
        if let Some(mut witness) = witness {
            // Substitute only necessary equality pins with infallible fixed
            // values. This leaves at most one varying source component in a
            // two-party parent witness; no copied-parent relationship is assumed.
            let mut fixed_read = lane_read.clone();
            let mut fixed_candidate = candidate.clone();
            fixed_candidate.plan_lane(tables, &mut fixed_read);
            let pinned: BTreeMap<_, _> = fixed_read.keys.iter().filter_map(|key|
                key.column.as_ref().filter(|_| fixed_key_total(&key.value)).map(|column| (column.clone(), key.value.clone()))).collect();
            let target_pinned = if let Expr::Exists { read: probe, .. } = &witness {
                let fields: Vec<_> = probe.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| intent::column(&key.value)).collect();
                !fields.is_empty() && fields.iter().all(|field| pinned.contains_key(*field))
            } else { false };
            if target_pinned {
                candidate.plan_lane(tables, &mut lane_read);
                branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, lane_read, GUARDED_LANE, None, None, read.filter.clone())?;
                continue;
            }
            // Reuse PAGE's complete source admission on exactly this witness.
            // The real schema still has the entire compound rule; the bound
            // validator verifies its remaining conjuncts from these pins.
            if leaf.iter().any(|atom| !matches!(atom, Expr::Exists { .. }) && intent::equality(atom, table).is_none()) { return None; }
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut witness, &mut |value| {
                if let Some(replacement) = intent::column(value).and_then(|column| pinned.get(column)) { *value = replacement.clone(); }
            });
            let mut local = tables.clone();
            local.get_mut(&read.table)?.decl.rules.iter_mut().find(|rule| rule.effect == Effect::Read)?.value = witness;
            let merge = branches::membership(&local, synthesized, &mut candidate, &lane_read)?;
            for mut lane in merge.lanes.into_iter().filter(|lane| lane.branch.as_deref() != Some(SYSTEM_LANE)) {
                if !fixed_guards.is_empty() {
                    let planned = lane.planned.as_mut()?;
                    let mut predicates = planned.filter.take().map(|filter| vec![*filter]).unwrap_or_default();
                    predicates.extend(fixed_guards.iter().cloned());
                    planned.filter = intent::conjunction(predicates);
                }
                if order.as_ref() != Some(&lane.planned.as_ref()?.order_keys) { return None; }
                lanes.push(lane);
                if lanes.len() > branches::MAX_BRANCHES { return None; }
            }
        } else {
            candidate.plan_lane(tables, &mut lane_read);
            let branch = if branches::decided(tables, &lane_read) { BRANCH_LANE } else { GUARDED_LANE };
            // Binding repeats guard readability against the real core schema.
            // Do not put a grant probe in the residual row predicate.
            branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, lane_read, branch, None, None, read.filter.clone())?;
        }
    }
    if candidate.error_count() != errors { return None; }
    *requests = candidate;
    Some(MergeExpr { view_order: Vec::new(), table: read.table.clone(), matching: None, filter: None, index: String::new(), order: range.order,
        take: range.take, cursor: range.cursor.clone(), lanes, site: read.site.clone() })
}

/// A top-level `not exists point[.id]` explicitly makes vacancy observable.
/// One server lane checks the original rule for every candidate and may report
/// exhaustion only after the physical range ends. It carries no ordinary
/// filter because rule probes use wider system visibility.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — exact vacancy is the sole non-uniform exhaustion lane.
pub(super) fn vacancy(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, requests: &mut Requests,
    read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<MergeExpr> {
    let range = read.range.as_ref()?;
    let table = tables.get(&read.table)?;
    let rule = branches::read_rule(table)?;
    if read.filter.is_some() || read.index.is_some() || !read.keys.is_empty() || read.matching.is_some()
        || !read.computed.is_empty() || read.cursor_prefix.is_some()
        || (table.decl.sync.is_some() && !table.decl.online_only) { return None; }
    fn alternatives<'a>(value: &'a Expr, out: &mut Vec<&'a Expr>) {
        match value {
            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => { alternatives(left, out); alternatives(right, out); }
            other => out.push(other),
        }
    }
    let mut disjuncts = Vec::new(); alternatives(rule, &mut disjuncts);
    let admitted = disjuncts.iter().any(|value| {
        let Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } = value else { return false };
        let Expr::Exists { read: probe, .. } = value.as_ref() else { return false };
        if op != "not" || probe.range.is_some() || probe.filter.is_some() || probe.matching.is_some()
            || !probe.seeks.is_empty() || probe.keys.len() != 1 || intent::column(&probe.keys[0].value) != Some("id") { return false; }
        let Some(target) = tables.get(&probe.table) else { return false };
        let Some(columns) = super::intent_lanes::probe_columns(tables, synthesized, table, probe) else { return false };
        target.indexes.iter().any(|index| target.total_unique(index) && index.columns == columns)
    });
    if !admitted { return None; }
    let mut candidate = requests.clone(); let errors = candidate.error_count();
    let mut system = read.clone(); candidate.read(tables, &mut system, false);
    let mut lane = read.clone(); candidate.plan_lane(tables, &mut lane);
    let mut lanes = Vec::with_capacity(2); let mut order = None;
    branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, system, SYSTEM_LANE, None, None, None)?;
    branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, lane, VACANCY_LANE, None, None, None)?;
    if candidate.error_count() != errors { return None; }
    *requests = candidate;
    Some(MergeExpr { view_order: Vec::new(), table: read.table.clone(), matching: None, filter: None,
        index: String::new(), order: range.order, take: range.take, cursor: range.cursor.clone(), lanes, site: read.site.clone() })
}

fn compound(rule: &Expr) -> bool {
    match rule {
        Expr::Binary { op, .. } if op == "and" || op == "!=" => true,
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => compound(left) || compound(right),
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// At most seven positive alternatives plus the system range. A nullable
/// enum's `!= null` is exactly its finite set of non-null values.
fn dnf(table: &TableInfo, value: &Expr) -> Option<Vec<Vec<Expr>>> {
    let out = match value {
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => {
            let mut out = dnf(table, left)?; out.extend(dnf(table, right)?); out
        }
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => {
            let a = dnf(table, left)?; let b = dnf(table, right)?;
            if a.len().saturating_mul(b.len()) >= branches::MAX_BRANCHES { return None; }
            a.into_iter().flat_map(|a| b.iter().map(move |b| { let mut out = a.clone(); out.extend(b.clone()); out })).collect()
        }
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, site } if op == "!=" => {
            let (column, term) = if let Some(column) = intent::column(left) { (column, right) } else { (intent::column(right)?, left) };
            let kind = table.columns.get(column)?;
            let TypeKind::Enum { variants } = &kind.kind else { return None; };
            let include = |variant: &String| match term.as_ref() {
                Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, .. } if kind.optional => Some(true),
                Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(excluded), .. } if !kind.optional => Some(variant != excluded),
                _ => None,
            };
            if variants.iter().any(|variant| include(variant).is_none()) { return None; }
            variants.iter().filter(|variant| include(variant).unwrap()).map(|variant| vec![Expr::Binary { op: "=".into(), left: Box::new(Expr::Member {
                base: Box::new(Expr::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name: column.into(), site: site.clone() }),
                right: Box::new(Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(variant.clone()), site: site.clone() }), site: site.clone() }]).collect()
        }
        other => vec![vec![other.clone()]],
    };
    (out.len() < branches::MAX_BRANCHES).then_some(out)
}

+distinction preserves complete `static grant or membership` and

+#[test]
+fn standalone_static_grant_leaves_coexist_with_membership_lanes() {
+    let tables = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('advisor', 'student')\n  unique principal\n  public\n  online only\ntable access:\n  item: items\n  principal: principal\n  unique item, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\n";
+    let static_and_membership = format!("{tables}table items:\n  at: int\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or exists access[.id, viewer]\n  online only\nquery page(c: cursor ?):\n  return items first 10 by .at after c\n");
+    let party_static_and_membership = static_and_membership.replace(
+        "read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or exists access[.id, viewer]",
+        "read <- .owner = viewer or exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or exists access[.id, viewer]",
+    ).replace("table items:\n  at: int", "table items:\n  owner: principal\n  at: int");
+    let fixed_and_varying = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('advisor', 'student')\n  unique principal\n  public\n  online only\ntable chapterAccess:\n  chapter: int\n  principal: principal\n  unique chapter, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versionAccess:\n  version: id\n  principal: principal\n  unique version, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versions:\n  chapter: int\n  at: int\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'advisor'] or (exists chapterAccess[.chapter, viewer] and exists versionAccess[.id, viewer])\n  online only\nquery page(chapter: int, c: cursor ?):\n  return versions where .chapter = chapter first 10 by .at after c\n";
+
+    for (name, source) in [
+        ("static-membership", static_and_membership.as_str()),
+        ("party-static-membership", party_static_and_membership.as_str()),
+        ("static-fixed-varying", fixed_and_varying),
+    ] {
+        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{name}: {:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+        assert!(!bound.notes.iter().any(|note| matches!(note.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE")),
+            "{name}: {:?}", bound.notes);
+        assert!(snapback4_core::ir::body_merges(&bound.programs[0].program.body).iter().any(|merge| merge.branches()),
+            "{name}: {:?}", bound.programs);
+    }
+
+    // A static point inside the membership conjunction remains a retained
+    // ordinary-read guard. Hidden fixed points still decline PAGE.
+    let hidden = fixed_and_varying.replace("  public\n  online only\ntable chapterAccess:", "  read <- deny\n  online only\ntable chapterAccess:");
+    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), hidden)]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    assert!(bound.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", bound.notes);
+}
+
 #[test]
 fn lean_gate_prices_every_filter_product_pin_before_comparison() {
     let source = "table access:\n  bucket: text <=40\n  principal: principal\n  unique bucket, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable items:\n  bucket: text <=40\n  region: text <=435\n  status: text <=435\n  at: int\n  read <- exists access[.bucket, viewer]\n  online only\nquery mine(c: cursor ?):\n  return items where (.region = 'north' or .region = 'south') and (.status = 'open' or .status = 'held') first 10 by .at after c\n";

2026-10-14 (§18 PAGE5 amendment); 2026-10-14 (§17 PAGE4 amendment); 2026-10-14 (PAGE3 §16: party/static-role, pure-membership and positive compound pages; original-prefix bounds, checked hidden-parent rewrites, full-union device pages and separate model admission gates; Muse and fresh Codex both READY at `65d505289f`); 2026-10-13 (PAGE2: exact unique membership witnesses no longer need a separately declared discovery cap; unbounded inverted discovery remains all-or-nothing under the meter; row-own-`id` and parent-owner lanes; exact cap advice for shapes a cap can repair; §15); 2026-10-13 (the Lean classifier and executable model share prefix-normalized Boolean guards without equating nullable `null` and `false`; known-false compound guards and negative fixed literals use one canonical form; §9, §14); 2026-10-12 (PAGE: resolves Q6 with capped owner-or-membership lanes; one cursor, discovery-cut and device semantics; §14); 2026-10-12 (CUT: inclusive interrupted-predicate retry and held-group prefix-meter parity; LLP 3000.007 §20.24.1, §20.24.7); 2026-10-12 (UNION integration: synced unions retain ordinary per-arm scans; the online grant lane defers membership probes on devices; §7); 2026-10-12 (revision 6: round-5 GPT size-bound and nested-note findings; bounded descriptor digest, portable-key fallback, case-1-only existence wording; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 5: GPT's round-4 review, NOT READY; a member through a reference is not total, the capability walks the whole body, the token's scope is a digest of the lanes' pins; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 4: round-3 reviews, both NOT READY; case 2 always plans its grant lane, which the system reads whole; `exists` merges case 1 only; branch tokens are scoped to every lane's pins; capability `branch-merge-v1` fences older devices; the Lean model normalizes the planner's pins; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 3: round-2 reviews, GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES; the grant lane is planned as the read as written, clock arithmetic is not total, the frontier capture is armed and scoped to its lane, the eager path's foreign token restarts the read, and the Lean model mirrors the planner's guard, helper, existence and lane-count conditions; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 2: merged with 0.4.23, whose LN workstream made a disjunct the page's pins decide a page guard (LLP 3000.036 §16.3); case 2 now takes such guards beside parties, §2, §3, §9, §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 1: implemented; folds the blind GPT and Muse reviews of the draft, both NOT READY — totality, inclusive shape positions, the frontier continuation, order-column and index-width exclusions, seats on devices, the encoding and the downgrade; dispositions in §13); 2026-10-12 (draft for review, first numbered 3000.038, renumbered here because sibling workstreams took 3000.038)

+/// Resolve a point whose declared unique key is positional while additional

+fn point_columns(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, focal: &TableInfo, probe: &ReadExpr) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
+    if probe.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some()) {
+        return Some(probe.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.clone()).collect());
+    }
+    let mut positional = probe.clone();
+    positional.keys.retain(|key| key.column.is_none());
+    let mut resolved = super::intent_lanes::probe_columns(tables, synthesized, focal, &positional)?.into_iter();
+    probe.keys.iter().map(|key| key.column.clone().or_else(|| resolved.next())).collect()
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs
index 1da7c11948..240e06a945 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/rules.rs
@@ -805,17 +805,31 @@ fn fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
         return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
             || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
     }
-    // The IR preserves short-circuit `a and b` as a tiny Boolean block.
-    // Accept only that exact lowering: both operands are necessary, while an
-    // `or` block assigns on the false branch and cannot establish either one.
-    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter
-        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
-            condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = body.as_slice()
-        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice()
-        && else_body.is_empty() && condition == name && assigned == name
-        && matches!(value.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(result) if result == name) {
-        return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
-            || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+    // The compiler lowers a left-associated `a and b and c` into successive
+    // Let/If pairs. Accept only that exact dataflow chain: every right operand
+    // assigns the current result on its true branch, while `or` assigns on the
+    // false branch and any extra statement or mismatched name is rejected.
+    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter {
+        if body.is_empty() || body.len() % 2 != 0 { return false; }
+        let mut previous: Option<&String> = None;
+        let mut matched = false;
+        let mut at = 0;
+        while at < body.len() {
+            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
+                condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = &body[at..at + 2] else { return false };
+            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice() else { return false };
+            if !else_body.is_empty() || condition != name || assigned != name { return false; }
+            if let Some(previous) = previous {
+                if !matches!(left, Expr::Variable(input) if input == previous) { return false; }
+            } else {
+                matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins);
+            }
+            matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+            previous = Some(name);
+            at += 2;
+        }
+        if !matches!((value.as_ref(), previous), (Expr::Variable(result), Some(previous)) if result == previous) { return false; }
+        return matched;
     }
     let (Expr::Exists(read), RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. }) = (filter, rule) else { return false };
     let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };
