+
+/// Four binary equality factors require sixteen membership lanes and must not
+/// collapse into one undistributed complete descriptor after product overflow.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — alternative overflow retains the ordinary incomplete scan.
+pub fn page_membership_filter_overflow(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::Witness;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    for name in ["p", "q", "r", "s"] { case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column(name, Type::Int)); }
+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };
+    case.program.domain = Some("(.p = 0 or .p = 1) and (.q = 0 or .q = 1) and (.r = 0 or .r = 1) and (.s = 0 or .s = 1)".into());
+    case.program.residual = None; case.program.order = Some(vec!["label".into(), "score".into()]);
+    case.program.named_page_order = None; case.program.prelude.clear(); case.program.lets.clear(); case.steps.clear();
+    for (table, row) in &mut case.rows { if table == "items" {
+        for (at, name) in ["p", "q", "r", "s"].into_iter().enumerate() { row[name] = json!(((seed as usize + at) % 3) as i64); }
+    }}
+    case
+}
+
+/// A shared equality supplies the physical prefix for every Cartesian lane;
+/// the alternative factors themselves may remain total residual comparisons.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — common positive pins count in every product branch.
+pub fn page_membership_shared_filter_product(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::Witness;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column("region", Type::Text));
+    case.program.tables[0].columns.push(column("rank", Type::Int));
+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };
+    case.program.domain = Some(".region = 'north'".into());
+    case.program.residual = Some("(.score > 0 or .score > 10) and (.rank > 0 or .rank > 10)".into());
+    case.program.order = Some(vec!["label".into(), "score".into()]);
+    case.program.named_page_order = None; case.program.prelude.clear(); case.program.lets.clear(); case.steps.clear();
+    for (table, row) in &mut case.rows { if table == "items" {
+        row["region"] = json!(if seed % 3 == 0 { "south" } else { "north" });
+        row["rank"] = json!((seed % 15) as i64);
+    }}
+    case
+}
+
+/// Vacancy may use the target table's always-present required unique id even
+/// though the explicit column/unique lists omit that intrinsic point.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — implicit id is a required exact vacancy point.
+pub fn page5_implicit_id_vacancy(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = page5(seed, 2);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::VacancyImplicitId;
+    case
+}
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
index 7d6a20da18..f27427ef2c 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/smoke.rs
@@ -796,3 +796,33 @@ fn membership_page_with_two_equality_alternative_factors_is_complete_in_lean_and
         }
     }
 }
+
+#[test]
+fn page5_review_boundaries_agree_in_generated_native_and_lean_cases() {
+    let have_model = model_built();
+    for seed in 0..4 {
+        for (case, complete, model_boundary) in [
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_filter_overflow(seed), false, Some("page-membership-filter-product")),
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page_membership_shared_filter_product(seed), true, None),
+            (snapback4_difftest::generate::page5_implicit_id_vacancy(seed), true, None),
+        ] {
+            let source = case.program.source();
+            execute::compile(&source).unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("seed {seed}: {error}\n{source}"));
+            let mut engine = execute::engine(&case, &source).unwrap();
+            let outcome = execute::execute(&mut engine, &case, "run", &case.args);
+            assert_eq!(outcome.value["complete"], complete, "seed {seed}: {outcome:?}\n{source}");
+            let mut report = Report::default(); campaign::check(&case, &mut report, |_| {});
+            assert!(!report.failed(), "seed {seed}: {report:?}\n{source}");
+            if have_model {
+                let checked = snapback4_difftest::lean::compare(&case, &source).unwrap();
+                if let Some(reason) = model_boundary {
+                    assert!(matches!(&checked.verdict, Verdict::Unsupported(actual) if actual == reason),
+                        "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+                    assert_eq!(checked.executed, 0, "seed {seed}: model boundary executed\n{source}");
+                } else {
+                    assert!(matches!(checked.verdict, Verdict::Pass), "seed {seed}: {:?}\n{source}", checked.verdict);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs

+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs
@@ -1,6 +1,54 @@
 //! Physical page eligibility derived solely from Quarry surface declarations.
 use super::*;
 
+enum PagePins {
+    Plain(Vec<String>),
+    Product { system: Vec<String>, branches: Vec<Vec<String>> },
+    Invalid,
+}
+
+/// Reconstruct the native planner's bounded Cartesian product from the
+/// authored filter.  This stays surface-derived: the executable model cannot
+/// trust the binder's proof metadata when deciding whether comparison is safe.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — every modeled PAGE filter lane has its own exact physical prefix.
+fn page_pins(table: &Table, read: &ReadExpr) -> PagePins {
+    let Some(filter) = read.filter.as_deref() else { return PagePins::Plain(Vec::new()) };
+    let mut conjuncts = Vec::new();
+    flatten_and(filter, &mut conjuncts);
+    let system = model_pin_columns(table, &conjuncts, &read.order_keys);
+    let mut branches = vec![Vec::new()];
+    let mut distributed = false;
+    for conjunct in &conjuncts {
+        let mut alternatives = Vec::new();
+        flatten_or(conjunct, &mut alternatives);
+        distributed |= alternatives.len() > 1;
+        if branches.len().saturating_mul(alternatives.len()) > 8 { return PagePins::Invalid; }
+        branches = branches.into_iter().flat_map(|prefix| alternatives.iter().map(move |alternative| {
+            let mut lane = prefix.clone();
+            flatten_and(alternative, &mut lane);
+            lane
+        })).collect();
+    }
+    if !distributed || branches.len() < 2 { return PagePins::Plain(system); }
+    if branches.iter().any(|branch| !branch.iter().any(|atom| page_pin(table, atom))) {
+        return PagePins::Invalid;
+    }
+    PagePins::Product {
+        system,
+        branches: branches.into_iter().map(|branch| model_pin_columns(table, &branch, &read.order_keys)).collect(),
+    }
+}
+
+fn page_pin(table: &Table, expression: &Expr) -> bool {
+    let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = expression else { return false };
+    if op != "=" { return false; }
+    let (column, value) = if let Some(column) = column_of(left) { (column, right.as_ref()) }
+        else if let Some(column) = column_of(right) { (column, left.as_ref()) }
+        else { return false };
+    column != "id" && model_independent(value) && table.cols.iter().find(|field| field.name == column)
+        .is_some_and(|field| !field.nullable || matches!(value, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer"))
+}
+

+#[test]
+fn lean_gate_prices_every_filter_product_pin_before_comparison() {
+    let source = "table access:\n  bucket: text <=40\n  principal: principal\n  unique bucket, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable items:\n  bucket: text <=40\n  region: text <=435\n  status: text <=435\n  at: int\n  read <- exists access[.bucket, viewer]\n  online only\nquery mine(c: cursor ?):\n  return items where (.region = 'north' or .region = 'south') and (.status = 'open' or .status = 'held') first 10 by .at after c\n";

+}

    11	/// Comparisons and readable static points have a total logical predicate.
    12	/// A physical point can still cut: the merge frontier excludes an unfinished
    13	/// filter candidate, so continuation retries it rather than skipping it.
    14	pub(super) fn total_readable(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, focal: &TableInfo, value: &Expr) -> bool {
    15	    if branches::total(value) { return true; }
    16	    match value {
    17	        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" || op == "or" =>
    18	            total_readable(tables, synthesized, focal, left) && total_readable(tables, synthesized, focal, right),
    19	        Expr::Exists { read: probe, .. } if branches::columns_within(value, &[]) => {
    20	            if probe.range.is_some() || probe.filter.is_some() || probe.matching.is_some() || !probe.seeks.is_empty() { return false; }
    21	            if !probe.keys.iter().all(|key| fixed_key_total(&key.value)) { return false; }
    22	            let Some(target) = tables.get(&probe.table) else { return false };
    23	            if target.through.is_some() || target.decl.not_before.is_some() || target.decl.expire.is_some() { return false; }
    24	            let Some(columns) = point_columns(tables, synthesized, focal, probe) else { return false };
    25	            if !target.indexes.iter().any(|index| target.total_unique(index) && index.columns.iter().all(|column| columns.contains(column))) { return false; }

    27	            for (key, column) in point.keys.iter_mut().zip(columns) { key.column = Some(column); }
    28	            target.decl.public.is_some() || branches::read_rule(target).is_some_and(|source_rule|
    29	                branches::by_form(source_rule, &point) || crate::print::expression(source_rule) == crate::print::expression(value))

    31	        _ => false,
    32	    }
    33	}
    34	
    35	/// Resolve a point whose declared unique key is positional while additional

    37	/// equalities constrain the point but do not widen its identity key.
    38	fn point_columns(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, focal: &TableInfo, probe: &ReadExpr) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    39	    if probe.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some()) {
    40	        return Some(probe.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.clone()).collect());

    56	pub(super) fn expand(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, requests: &mut Requests, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<MergeExpr> {

   186	
   187	/// A top-level `not exists point[.id]` explicitly makes vacancy observable.
   188	/// One server lane checks the original rule for every candidate and may report
   189	/// exhaustion only after the physical range ends. It carries no ordinary
   190	/// filter because rule probes use wider system visibility.
   191	// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — exact vacancy is the sole non-uniform exhaustion lane.
   192	pub(super) fn vacancy(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &BTreeSet<(String, String)>, requests: &mut Requests,
   193	    read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<MergeExpr> {

   202	            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => { alternatives(left, out); alternatives(right, out); }

   222	    branches::push_membership_lane(&candidate, table, &mut lanes, &mut order, lane, VACANCY_LANE, None, None, None)?;

   226	        index: String::new(), order: range.order, take: range.take, cursor: range.cursor.clone(), lanes, site: read.site.clone() })
   227	}
   228	
   229	fn compound(rule: &Expr) -> bool {
   230	    match rule {
   231	        Expr::Binary { op, .. } if op == "and" || op == "!=" => true,
   232	        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => compound(left) || compound(right),
   233	        _ => false,
   234	    }
   235	}
   236	
   237	/// At most seven positive alternatives plus the system range. A nullable
   238	/// enum's `!= null` is exactly its finite set of non-null values.
   239	fn dnf(table: &TableInfo, value: &Expr) -> Option<Vec<Vec<Expr>>> {
   240	    let out = match value {
   241	        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => {
   242	            let mut out = dnf(table, left)?; out.extend(dnf(table, right)?); out
   243	        }
   244	        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => {
   245	            let a = dnf(table, left)?; let b = dnf(table, right)?;
   246	            if a.len().saturating_mul(b.len()) >= branches::MAX_BRANCHES { return None; }
   247	            a.into_iter().flat_map(|a| b.iter().map(move |b| { let mut out = a.clone(); out.extend(b.clone()); out })).collect()
   248	        }

   250	            let (column, term) = if let Some(column) = intent::column(left) { (column, right) } else { (intent::column(right)?, left) };
   251	            let kind = table.columns.get(column)?;
   252	            let TypeKind::Enum { variants } = &kind.kind else { return None; };
   253	            let include = |variant: &String| match term.as_ref() {
   254	                Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, .. } if kind.optional => Some(true),
   255	                Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(excluded), .. } if !kind.optional => Some(variant != excluded),
   256	                _ => None,
   257	            };
   258	            if variants.iter().any(|variant| include(variant).is_none()) { return None; }
   259	            variants.iter().filter(|variant| include(variant).unwrap()).map(|variant| vec![Expr::Binary { op: "=".into(), left: Box::new(Expr::Member {
   260	                base: Box::new(Expr::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name: column.into(), site: site.clone() }),
   261	                right: Box::new(Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(variant.clone()), site: site.clone() }), site: site.clone() }]).collect()
   262	        }
   263	        other => vec![vec![other.clone()]],
   264	    };
   265	    (out.len() < branches::MAX_BRANCHES).then_some(out)
   266	}

# LLP 3000.036.001: Merged pages for owner-or-X lists

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Review (revision 8 implemented in `sb4-loop`; PAGE2 §15 and PAGE3 §16 implemented, each with both final reviews READY)
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language (`snapback4-lang`: intent planning `bind/intent_branches.rs`, planned-merge binding `bind/program/views.rs`, `N_NEVER_COMPLETE`, the reads/indexes/sharing/recipes/cheatsheet cards), core (`snapback4-core`: `ir.rs` lane identities, `engine/branch_merge.rs`, the page walk's grant arm and frontier, `rules::branch_merge_lazy` and `covering_grant`, cursor versions 4 and 5), the device (`snapback4-device`, both stores), the Lean model (`snapback4/lean`: OPEN C8 and C2, `Proofs/Branches.lean`), the difftest generator (`widen.rs`) and its Lean features
**Author:** Claude Opus 5.5 for Charlie Cheever (workstream MERGE)
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Revised:** 2026-10-15 (revision 8: §18 requires independent readability proof for every standalone static grant alternative); 2026-10-15 (revision 7: §18 preserves a standalone static grant disjunct as its guarded lane beside membership branches while retaining static points inside conjunctions); 2026-10-14 (§18 PAGE5 amendment); 2026-10-14 (§17 PAGE4 amendment); 2026-10-14 (PAGE3 §16: party/static-role, pure-membership and positive compound pages; original-prefix bounds, checked hidden-parent rewrites, full-union device pages and separate model admission gates; Muse and fresh Codex both READY at `65d505289f`); 2026-10-13 (PAGE2: exact unique membership witnesses no longer need a separately declared discovery cap; unbounded inverted discovery remains all-or-nothing under the meter; row-own-`id` and parent-owner lanes; exact cap advice for shapes a cap can repair; §15); 2026-10-13 (the Lean classifier and executable model share prefix-normalized Boolean guards without equating nullable `null` and `false`; known-false compound guards and negative fixed literals use one canonical form; §9, §14); 2026-10-12 (PAGE: resolves Q6 with capped owner-or-membership lanes; one cursor, discovery-cut and device semantics; §14); 2026-10-12 (CUT: inclusive interrupted-predicate retry and held-group prefix-meter parity; LLP 3000.007 §20.24.1, §20.24.7); 2026-10-12 (UNION integration: synced unions retain ordinary per-arm scans; the online grant lane defers membership probes on devices; §7); 2026-10-12 (revision 6: round-5 GPT size-bound and nested-note findings; bounded descriptor digest, portable-key fallback, case-1-only existence wording; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 5: GPT's round-4 review, NOT READY; a member through a reference is not total, the capability walks the whole body, the token's scope is a digest of the lanes' pins; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 4: round-3 reviews, both NOT READY; case 2 always plans its grant lane, which the system reads whole; `exists` merges case 1 only; branch tokens are scoped to every lane's pins; capability `branch-merge-v1` fences older devices; the Lean model normalizes the planner's pins; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 3: round-2 reviews, GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES; the grant lane is planned as the read as written, clock arithmetic is not total, the frontier capture is armed and scoped to its lane, the eager path's foreign token restarts the read, and the Lean model mirrors the planner's guard, helper, existence and lane-count conditions; §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 2: merged with 0.4.23, whose LN workstream made a disjunct the page's pins decide a page guard (LLP 3000.036 §16.3); case 2 now takes such guards beside parties, §2, §3, §9, §13); 2026-10-12 (revision 1: implemented; folds the blind GPT and Muse reviews of the draft, both NOT READY — totality, inclusive shape positions, the frontier continuation, order-column and index-width exclusions, seats on devices, the encoding and the downgrade; dispositions in §13); 2026-10-12 (draft for review, first numbered 3000.038, renumbered here because sibling workstreams took 3000.038)
**Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (§7 2026-09-13 "wide uniform merges read heads"), [LLP 3000.007.000](./3000.007.000-merge-position-and-drain-cut-continuation.spec.md) (the merge position and the drain-cut continuation), [LLP 3000.027](./3000.027-top-n-answer-is-complete.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.035](./3000.035-quarry-semantics-in-lean.plan.md) (the Lean model), [LLP 3000.036](./3000.036-what-the-app-farm-asks-of-the-rule-language.rfc.md) §12.1 (readable grants), §12.2, §13.2, §16.1 (owner-or-X lists and the complete form), [LLP 3000.036.000](./3000.036.000-sharing-shapes-at-sync-and-union-audiences.rfc.md) §4 (union audiences, orthogonal), the reviews `llp/reviews/3000.036.001-merged-pages.{gpt,muse}.md`, the ticket `issues/20261012-snapback4-device-group-page-rule-probe-meter.md`

## 0. Summary

A list such as

```quarry
query myTasks(c: cursor ?):
  return tasks where .owner = viewer or .assignee = viewer last 50 by .at after c
```

over `read <- .owner = viewer or .assignee = viewer` was never `complete`.
The engine walked one index per page; neither `[owner, at, id]` nor
`[assignee, at, id]` holds every row the caller may read, so the page was
planned on `[at, id]`, checked the rule per row, and could not tell "no more"
from "more you may not read". `check` printed `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` with a
verified per-party rewrite (LLP 3000.036 §12.2, §13.2, §16.1). Across six farm
waves 12, 19 and 16 lanes reported the shape.

Now the one-query form is complete. When a query page's predicate, or its
table's read rule, is a disjunction of branches each of which pins an index
prefix that decides the read rule, the planner reads it as a k-way merge of
the branch ranges in the page's order. A row matching several branches is
emitted once. Each branch's rows are decided by its own pins, so the merged
page is `complete` exactly when every branch's walk finishes. The cursor is
one position in the shared order, which every branch resumes strictly after
(inclusively, for an unfinished first returned shape); its token is a new
version naming the branch set. A meter cut continues from the frontier every
branch has examined. Devices run the same plan with the existing per-scan
coverage rules. The Lean model's C8 gains the rule, with a coverage proof.

Owner-or-staff (`.owner = viewer or exists staff[viewer]`) is the same plan
with one branch guarded by the readable grant of §12.1: held, that branch is
the whole page; not held, it contributes nothing and the owner branch is the
page. A staff page is complete for staff (as before) and an owner's page is
complete for everyone else (new). The guide's owner-or-staff and two-party
recipes are now single queries.

## 1. What the builders met

LLP 3000.036 records the shape in waves 2–6: `#12` (12 lanes), `#3b`
(19 lanes) and the §14.2 and §16.1 notes (16 lanes). The lanes either pasted
the per-party rewrite (two queries, two cursors, a client-side merge and
de-duplication, two loading states) or kept the one-query form and shipped a
list that always said "there may be more". The engine already merges ordered
lanes (LLP 3000 §7, 2026-09-13) and already has the position that makes a
merge resumable (LLP 3000.007.000 §1); this RFC lets one merge read several
indexes of one table.

## 2. The rule

Definitions, over the checked schema:

- An **equality pin** in a conjunction is a conjunct `.col = e` (either
  side) or a bare boolean column (`.flag`, `not .flag`), where `e` reads no
  row column, `.col` is a plain column of the table and not `id`. On a
  nullable column it counts only when `e` is `viewer`: a branch never seeks a
  null the original filter would reject.
- A **party disjunct** of a read rule is `.col = viewer` (either side) on a
  principal column of the row.
- A **guard disjunct** reads no column of the row (§12.1: a role probe, a
  seat such as `viewer = 'service:audit'`), or reads only columns the page's
  own pins fix (§16.3: `exists boards[.board, owner = viewer]` under `.board =
  b`). It is **usable** when `rules::page_guard_decision` takes it: no `not`,
  and every row it probes readable by whoever its key names.
- A prefix **decides** the read rule when every column the rule reads is
  pinned (with the self-witness proof of LLP 3000.007 §16), or the rule is
  true by the pins' form: `allow`, a `.col = viewer` disjunct pinned to
  `viewer`, a `.col = 'x'` disjunct pinned to `'x'`, and `and`/`or` of
  those. This is the core's `rules::rule_true`/`scan_visibility`, the
  binder's `intent_branches::decided` and the model's `decidedPins`.
- A predicate is **total** when it is comparisons (`=`, `!=`, `<`, `<=`,
  `>`, `>=`, `in`) and boolean operators over the row's own columns,
  arguments, literals and `viewer`: its evaluation never refuses and never
  reads. A member of anything but the row (`.meta.ok`, `m.ok`) is not: it
  binds to a read of the referenced row. CUT (2026-10-12) fixes interrupted
  single-index predicates by retrying their row inclusively (LLP 3000.007
  §20.24.1), but does not make such a predicate total: missing/hidden targets
  can still refuse or be unavailable, and distribution changes which reads
  short-circuit. Keep this exclusion and the merge's decided lane frontier.
  Arithmetic is not either,
  not even a time window (`now - 7d` overflows when the clock is at the
  integer bound), so such a page keeps one index. The Lean model has no `in`;
  the emitter names it unsupported.

A **query page** (`t where … first|last N by … [after c]` in a query program;
never in a mutation, job, shared helper or shape, which keep ordinary scans)
is **branch-decided** when all of these hold:

- it names no explicit index or key, does not search (`matching`), has no
  computed order, and reads an ordinary table (not a maintained view, row
  view, ephemeral or timeline table, a `public` declaration or a `read
  through` child), whose read rule is one `read` declaration;
- its own pins do **not** decide the rule (a decided page keeps its plan,
  index and cursor);
- its predicate is total;
- and either
  1. **branches in `where`:** exactly one top-level conjunct is an `or` of
     2–8 alternatives, each holding an equality pin, and each alternative
     with the other conjuncts decides the rule; or
  2. **branches in the read rule:** the rule is an `or` of at least two
     disjuncts, each a party or a usable guard, with at least one party.
     The branches are `base_j and .party_k = viewer` for every base branch
     (case 1's alternatives when `where` has such an `or`, else the whole
     `where`) and party, plus one **grant branch**, the whole `where`, read
     for a caller the rule's guards admit and for the system, which every
     rule admits (a rule of parties alone has no guard: the lane is the
     system's only);
- and every branch plans to an index of at most 8 components whose position
  after its pins is the page's order then `id` (a branch never pins an order
  column: a merge lane keeps such an equality as a seek), with at most 8
  branches.

Otherwise the page keeps its single-index plan and, when undecided,
`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` with its per-party rewrite.

A single party (`read <- .owner = viewer`) is not expanded: its complete form
is the pin the author writes, and expanding it would replan every unpinned
owner page (§12 Q1). 8 is both the index width limit and the point below
which the lazy merge reads a page per lane (`word_merge.rs`); every branch
may be a synthesized index.

### 2.1 Why the merged rows are exactly the readable matches

Write `W` for the predicate and `R` for the read rule; the page returns, in
order, the rows `r` with `W(r) ∧ R(r)`.

- Case 1: `W = rest ∧ (a_1 ∨ … ∨ a_k)`, so `W = ∨_j (a_j ∧ rest)`. Branch
  `j` reads the range its pins select and filters by the rest of `a_j ∧
  rest`; its prefix decides `R`, so every row of the branch is readable
  (verdict true) or none is (verdict false: the branch is empty). The union
  is `∨_j (a_j ∧ rest ∧ R) = W ∧ R`.
- Case 2: `R = ∨_k (.p_k = viewer) ∨ G` with `G` row-independent or reading
  only the page's pinned columns, so for every row the page selects and this
  caller `R(r) = ∨_k (r.p_k = viewer) ∨ g` with `g` one boolean (the guard
  with the pinned values), and
  `W ∧ R = ∨_{j,k} (b_j ∧ .p_k = viewer) ∨ (g ∧ W)`. Each party branch is
  decided true by its pin; the grant branch is all of `W` when `g` holds and
  nothing otherwise, and then it is a superset of every other branch, so the
  plan reads it alone. The Lean model proves the party half
  (`parties_cover_view`): every visible row carries the viewer in a party
  column.

The argument distributes `W` over its alternatives, so it needs `W` total:
with `or (.assignee = viewer and 1 / n = 1)`, a row in both parties skips the
division in the original and would evaluate it in its own branch (GPT
review, finding 1). A branch evaluates the predicate only on rows the
original also reads, so a total predicate gives the same rows.

A row in several branches has the same position in each (§5), so the merge
emits it once. No branch reads a row the caller may not read, so no such row
reaches the merge, the filter, the lookahead or the cursor.

## 3. The plan

**Planning** (`bind/intent_branches.rs`, called from `intent::plan` where the
other lane expansions run). Each branch is an ordinary planned read
(`Requests::plan_lane`): its pins form the prefix (a party pin first, so the
deciding pin is never left residual), the page's order then `id` the suffix;
the planner reuses a declared index with exactly those columns or synthesizes
one. The expansion is dropped, and the read planned as written, when a branch
does not decide the rule (by the binder's check), the branches' order columns
differ, an index would exceed 8 components, or a branch exceeds the portable 2,048-byte key or 512-byte label bound,
or planning a branch raised a diagnostic: a program that compiled before still compiles. The result is the
existing `MergeExpr` with planned lanes, each carrying the branch identity
`$or` (or `$or:grant`) in the field views use for theirs; the planned-merge
binding passes it through to the IR `MergeLane.branch`. No IR type gains a
field, so every existing program's IR and pinned digest is unchanged;
`Merge::branches()` recognises the plan.

**Binding check** (`program/views.rs`). With the core schema in hand, the
binder re-checks the plan with the runtime's own functions: every grant
lane's `rules::covering_grant` (every disjunct that is not a party is a
usable guard of `page_guard_decision` for the lane's pins, §12.1, §16.3) and `rules::branch_merge_lazy` (same table and order, scalar
pins, every non-grant lane `scan_visibility(..).uniform`, a total position,
identical position columns). `covering_grant` compares each non-party
disjunct after the same prefix simplification that produced the page guard;
`allow`, `deny`, `not` and required boolean pins therefore have one identity
through the coverage proof, while a nullable boolean's `null` is never
normalized to `false`. If it fails and the plan has a grant lane, the
read binds as its grant lane alone, which is the read as written: the grant
lane is planned exactly as the single-index page is (not as a merge lane), so
the fallback is 0.4.23's plan, page guard included; when that plan's order
differs from the party lanes' (an equality on the first order column becomes
a pin there), the page is not merged at all (a hidden
roster, `read <- deny` on `staff`, keeps today's plan and note); without a
grant lane the merge stays, sound and, for a lane that is not decided,
incomplete.

**Execution** (`engine/branch_merge.rs`). A branch merge that passes
`branch_merge_lazy` is a lazy merge whose lanes may read different indexes of
one table. Each lane's position is its index components after its prefix,
plus `id` when they do not end in it (LLP 3000.007.000 §1); the binding check
makes the positions comparable, and because they end in the row id, equal
positions are the same row. Grant lanes open first. A grant lane's guard is
decided once with the lane's pinned values (`grant_verdict`, charged as §12.1) and, when it holds, the lane
reads as a decided page (`scan_consumed`'s grant arm, through the runner's
`merge_grant`) and the other lanes are not opened; when it does not hold, the
lane is not read and does not mark the page non-uniform; for the system it
reads every row. Each lane opens with one read of `take + 1` rows; the heap
pops in position order, de-duplicates by id and refills a lane strictly after
its last popped position. A branch merge that is not lazy-eligible takes the
eager path with the same branch descriptor; it is sound and, with a lane
that is not decided, incomplete with no `next`.

**Page guards (LLP 3000.036 §16.3).** Without the merge, 0.4.23 decides a
single-index page once for a caller a pinned guard admits, and walks it per
row (incomplete) for everyone else. With it, a page whose rule is parties and
such guards merges the guard branch with the party branches, so the guard's
holder reads the guard branch alone and everyone else their party branches,
all complete. A page the merge does not cover keeps the single-index guard.

**Older engines.** Older devices refuse a merged backend by its capability
(§5). Were one to run the plan anyway, an engine that does not know the lane
identities (0.4.22) ignores them, so a backend compiled by this release still runs on an older
device: lanes over different indexes take the eager merge, which translates
one cursor across lanes with the same suffix (LLP 3000.007 §20.3) and
de-duplicates by id. It returns the same rows, mints its own cursor form, and
evaluates a grant lane as an ordinary page. For a plan without guards the
pages continue as here. A plan with a guard is only ever run by an older
*device* (servers compile their own IR), and a guard's table never syncs
except for a seat, which the device trusts: a role or parent-probe guard's
query plan.

**Bounded products.** The planner distributes independent positive
alternatives in the authorization rule across conjunctions before it
classifies the resulting lanes.
For example, two exact principal alternatives and three exact status
alternatives produce six lanes, each retaining both selected predicates and
the original query predicate. Distribution is all-or-nothing and remains
inside the existing eight-lane limit, including the system lane. Overflow,
negative alternatives and a residual second varying witness retain the
analyzer-limitation fallback.

The same bounded Cartesian construction applies to positive membership PAGE
query filters. Every resulting branch must contain an eligible equality pin,
the system descriptor plus every party or membership descriptor must remain
within eight, and every original predicate stays in its lane. A product that
pins the membership witness's varying outer field, overflows, or leaves a
branch without a usable pin stays on the ordinary incomplete scan. The Lean
classifier constructs every factor before applying those same exclusions; it
never treats a failed product as an undistributed complete lane or a second
`or` predicate as absent. A non-alternative equality or required bare Boolean
shared by every product branch supplies an eligible pin to each branch.
Repeated party leaves name one physical audience prefix and count once per
product branch.

**Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
be accompanied by one or more fixed `exists` guards, independent of authored
conjunction grouping. A fixed guard is admissible only when
the lane pins every field term in its target key and the target exposes a
required, total, unique point that is caller-readable after those exact lane
pins are substituted. The target must have no hidden source, read-through boundary,
`not before` boundary or expiry. The compiler substitutes the exact lane pin
into the guard and keeps that complete guard in the generated query filter;
an already-static point such as `exists roles[viewer]` needs no substitution
but remains in that filter. The residual may contain three or more fixed
guards; its exact short-circuit conjunction chain remains necessary in full.
The core validator independently matches it to the original rule expression
and lane pin. Thus the generated guard observes every fixed grant that could
authorize the authored rule. A hidden, timed, non-total or extra-equality
guard remains incomplete. Two varying grant sources also remain outside this
fragment.

A standalone static grant disjunct remains its ordinary guarded lane beside
party and membership lanes. It is not a residual sibling of the membership
witness: if it admits the caller, that guarded lane reads the whole authored
range; otherwise it contributes no rows. Every standalone static alternative
must independently expose a required, total, unique, timeless and
caller-readable point before any guarded lane is admitted. A different
readable disjunct cannot cover a hidden or timed alternative. Only a static
point in the same conjunction as another atom enters the retained-filter
proof above. This distinction preserves complete `static grant or membership`
and `party or static grant or membership` pages without dropping any
alternative or conjunct.

**Exact vacancy lanes.** The server may invert a top-level
`not exists holds[.id]` alternative when `holds.id` is a required, unique,
plain point and the paged table is online-only. Its vacancy lane scans the
same full ordered outer range as the system lane and privately authorizes each
candidate against the original rule; it publishes completion only after that
authorized walk is exhausted. A predicate-meter or authorization-walk cut
therefore produces an incomplete page. Filtered vacancy reads, synchronized
outer tables, compound or non-`id` keys, non-unique points and any shortcut
around the ordinary per-row authorization check keep the fallback. The target
may itself be hidden or timed: the private server walk evaluates the authored
rule's system probe for each candidate, then exposes only the authorized outer
row. This is a vacancy predicate over the outer row, not a claim that a private
target's absence is globally observable.
The intrinsic `id` point is always required and unique even though schema
column and unique lists omit it; other vacancy keys require both declarations.

**Index rewrite note.** When every missing rule field is already fixed by an
independent exact query equality, the note prints one concrete declaration
using the actual table and collision-free index name, followed by the complete
rewritten query using that same index. The rewrite locates the focal read by
its source-map span, even when an earlier read uses the same index name, and
the binder recompiles the printed declaration and query together in tests.
It omits the repair when the added components or emitted label would exceed
the portable index bounds, or when authored bracket keys would change column
meaning under the prepended prefix.
When those exact equalities do not establish the rule prefix, the note
does not offer this rewrite.

**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v4` fences the new validator
interpretations: fixed residual point guards and exact vacancy lanes. A device
that only understands `branch-pages-v3` must refuse this IR before decoding or
executing it; bounded products reuse the already validated positive-branch
lanes, and existing PAGE4 plans keep their prior capability. The Lean surface
classifier mirrors the three admissions and the private authorization walk.
Generated differential cases exercise the new lane families on native,
simulator and device hosts. Forms outside the fragment remain honest analyzer
limitations rather than completeness claims.

//! Membership and bounded-list predicates enumerate ordinary metered lanes.
use super::{catalog::TableInfo, intent};
use crate::ast::*;
use crate::types::Ty;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

pub(super) fn expand(tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<MergeExpr> {
    if read.index.is_some() || !read.keys.is_empty() { return None; }
    let mut predicates = Vec::new();
    intent::split(*read.filter.clone()?, &mut predicates);
    let name = "$intentLane";
    for position in 0..predicates.len() {
        let predicate = &predicates[position];
        let (column, source, value) = match predicate {
            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "in" => {
                if !intent::independent(right) { continue; }
                let column = intent::column(left)?.to_owned();
                (column, *right.clone(), Expr::Name { name: name.into(), site: predicate.site().clone() })
            }
            Expr::Binary { op, .. } if op == "!=" => {
                let Some((column, source)) = enum_complement(predicate, tables.get(&read.table)?) else { continue; };
                (column, source, Expr::Name { name: name.into(), site: predicate.site().clone() })
            }
            Expr::Exists { read: probe, .. } => {
                if probe.filter.is_some() || probe.matching.is_some() || !probe.seeks.is_empty() || probe.range.is_some() { continue; }
                let columns = probe_columns(tables, synthesized, tables.get(&read.table)?, probe)?;
                let mut pins = Vec::new();
                let mut varying = None;
                for (at, key) in probe.keys.iter().enumerate() {
                    let target_column = key.column.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| columns[at].clone());
                    if let Some(column) = intent::column(&key.value) {
                        if varying.is_some() { return None; }
                        varying = Some((column.to_owned(), target_column));
                    } else {
                        if !intent::independent(&key.value) { return None; }
                        pins.push(Expr::Binary { op: "=".into(), left: Box::new(focal(&target_column, &key.site)), right: Box::new(key.value.clone()), site: key.site.clone() });
                    }
                }
                let (column, target_column) = varying?;
                let source = Expr::Read(ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), table: probe.table.clone(), keys: Vec::new(), seeks: Vec::new(), filter: intent::conjunction(pins),
                    index: None, range: None, matching: None, order_keys: vec![target_column.clone()], site: probe.site.clone() });
                let value = Expr::Member { base: Box::new(Expr::Name { name: name.into(), site: probe.site.clone() }), name: target_column, site: probe.site.clone() };
                (column, source, value)
            }
            _ => continue,
        };
        let admission = matches!(predicate, Expr::Exists { .. }).then(|| read.filter.clone().unwrap());
        let distinct = value.clone();
        let mut lane_read = read.clone();
        let nullable_in = matches!(predicate, Expr::Binary { op, .. } if op == "in")
            // Range lanes produce integers only; no fallback index is needed.
            && !matches!(&source, Expr::Call { name, .. } if name == "range")
            && tables.get(&read.table)?.columns.get(&column).is_some_and(|ty| ty.optional);
        predicates.remove(position);
        let nulls = nullable_in.then(|| {
            let mut null_read = read.clone();
            null_read.filter = intent::conjunction(predicates.clone());
            (column.clone(), Box::new(null_read))
        });
        predicates.push(Expr::Binary { op: "=".into(), left: Box::new(focal(&column, &read.site)), right: Box::new(value), site: read.site.clone() });
        lane_read.filter = intent::conjunction(predicates);
        let range = read.range.clone().unwrap_or(RangeSpec { order: Order::Asc, take: 8192, cursor: None, site: read.site.clone() });
        return Some(MergeExpr { view_order: Vec::new(), table: read.table.clone(), matching: None, filter: None, index: String::new(), order: range.order, take: range.take,
            cursor: range.cursor, lanes: vec![MergeLane { admission, nulls, distinct: Some(distinct), branch: None, table: None, planned: Some(Box::new(lane_read)), project: None, prefix: Vec::new(), columns: None,
                loop_: Some((name.into(), source)), site: read.site.clone() }], site: read.site.clone() });
    }
    None
}

/// Resolve a probe exactly as positional point binding does when rule terms
/// expose their types. Program-only expressions without a type at this phase
/// retain the older unique-by-arity fallback.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#14 — PAGE discovery uses the rule probe's canonical typed point key.
pub(super) fn probe_columns(tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>,
    focal: &TableInfo, probe: &ReadExpr) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    if probe.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some()) {
        return Some(probe.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.clone()).collect());
    }
    let target = tables.get(&probe.table)?;
    let indexes = if let Some(types) = positional_types(focal, probe) {
        super::positional_keys::candidates(target, probe, &types, synthesized).into_iter()
            .filter(|index| index.is_plain()).collect::<Vec<_>>()
    } else {
        target.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == probe.keys.len())
            .filter(|index| probe.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| &index.name == name)).collect()
    };
    let [index] = indexes.as_slice() else { return None };
    Some(index.columns.clone())
}

fn positional_types(focal: &TableInfo, probe: &ReadExpr) -> Option<Vec<(Ty, bool)>> {
    probe.keys.iter().map(|key| match &key.value {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" => Some((Ty::Principal, false)),
        Expr::Member { base, name, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }) => {
            if name == "id" { Some((Ty::Ref(focal.decl.name.clone()), false)) }
            else { focal.columns.get(name).map(|kind| (Ty::from_scalar(kind), false)) }
        }
        Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(text), .. } => Some((Ty::Text(text.chars().count() as u32), true)),
        Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Number(_), .. } => Some((Ty::Int, false)),
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "-" && matches!(value.as_ref(), Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Number(_), .. }) => Some((Ty::Int, false)),
        Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(_), .. } => Some((Ty::Bool, false)),
        Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, .. } => Some((Ty::Null, false)),
        _ => None,
    }).collect()
}

fn focal(name: &str, site: &crate::diag::Site) -> Expr { Expr::Member { base: Box::new(Expr::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name: name.into(), site: site.clone() } }

// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.1 — small required enums have a finite complement;
// nullable enums keep total inequality as a residual, including null rows.
fn enum_complement(predicate: &Expr, table: &TableInfo) -> Option<(String, Expr)> {
    let Expr::Binary { left, right, site, .. } = predicate else { return None; };
    let (column, value) = if let Some(column) = intent::column(left) { (column, right.as_ref()) }
        else { (intent::column(right)?, left.as_ref()) };
    let ty = table.columns.get(column)?;
    let TypeKind::Enum { variants } = &ty.kind else { return None; };
    if ty.optional || variants.len() > 8 { return None; }
    let values = |exclude: Option<&str>| Expr::Array { site: site.clone(), items: variants.iter()
        .filter(|variant| Some(variant.as_str()) != exclude)
        .map(|variant| Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(variant.clone()), site: site.clone() }).collect() };
    let source = match value {
        Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(exclude), .. } => values(Some(exclude)),
        // Stable bindings are safe to test while opening lanes. Fallible
        // expressions stay under their original row predicate's guards.
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if !matches!(name.as_str(), "new" | "now" | "viewer") => {
            variants.iter().rev().fold(values(None), |otherwise, variant| Expr::If {
                condition: Box::new(Expr::Binary { op: "=".into(), left: Box::new(value.clone()),
                    right: Box::new(Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(variant.clone()), site: site.clone() }), site: site.clone() }),
                then: Box::new(values(Some(variant))), otherwise: Box::new(otherwise), site: site.clone() })
        }
        _ => return None,
    };
    Some((column.into(), source))
}

// Conservative authorization proof before schema binding: every source-row
// field read by the rule must be pinned, including any nullable fallback.
// Declining an optimization preserves the ordinary scan's continuation; it
// does not change the source rule or the merge runtime's disclosure contract.
pub(super) fn prefixes_decide_rules(tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, merge: &MergeExpr) -> bool {
    merge.lanes.iter().all(|lane| lane.planned.iter().map(Box::as_ref)
        .chain(lane.nulls.iter().map(|(_, read)| read.as_ref()))
        .chain(lane.loop_.iter().filter_map(|(_, source)| if let Expr::Read(read) = source { Some(read) } else { None }))
        .all(|read| prefix_decides_rule(tables, read)))
}

pub(super) fn prefix_decides_rule(tables: &BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
    let Some(table) = tables.get(&read.table) else { return false; };
    if table.maintained.is_some() { return false; }
    let pinned = read.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.as_ref().map(|name| (name.as_str(), &key.value))).collect::<BTreeMap<_, _>>();
    table.decl.rules.iter().filter(|rule| rule.effect == Effect::Read).all(|rule| {
        let mut predicate = rule.value.clone();
        // An exact probe of this very row is true, even when some of its
        // fields vary. This is the existing membership self-witness proof.
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut predicate, &mut |value| {
            if let Expr::Exists { read: probe, site } = value && self_witness(table, probe, &pinned) {
                *value = Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(true), site: site.clone() };
            }
        });
        let mut decided = true;
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut predicate, &mut |value| {
            if let Some(column) = intent::column(value) && !pinned.contains_key(column) { decided = false; }
        });
        decided
    })
}

fn self_witness(table: &TableInfo, probe: &ReadExpr, pins: &BTreeMap<&str, &Expr>) -> bool {
    if probe.table != table.decl.name || probe.filter.is_some() || probe.matching.is_some() || !probe.seeks.is_empty() { return false; }
    let columns = if probe.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some()) {
        let columns: Vec<_> = probe.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.clone()).collect();
        if !table.indexes.iter().any(|index| table.total_unique(index) && index.columns.iter().all(|name| columns.contains(name))) { return false; }
        columns
    } else {
        let indexes: Vec<_> = table.indexes.iter().filter(|index| table.total_unique(index) && index.columns.len() == probe.keys.len()

        || pins.get(column.as_str()).is_some_and(|value| crate::print::expression(value) == crate::print::expression(&key.value)))
}

/// discloses nothing beyond the caller's own readable rows: each probe's
/// target row must be readable by its own read rule once the probe key pins
/// it (`.principal = viewer`, `public`, or the same probe), and a negated
/// probe is never a grant.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — the role-guard completeness proof deferred in §11, server-side and per caller.
pub fn grant_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    guard_decision(schema, scan, false)
}

/// The disjuncts of `scan`'s read rule that decide the page for a caller they
/// admit: the readable grants (`grant_decision`), and each disjunct whose every
/// row column the scan's prefix pins and whose probed rows its holders can
/// read (`pinned_probe_readable`), such as `exists boards[.board, owner =
/// viewer]` under `.board = b`. The page walk (`read.rs`) evaluates them once,
/// with the prefix's values for the pinned columns: when one holds, every row
/// the scan can visit is readable, so the page is decided as a uniform page
/// is. Each verdict reads only rows the caller may read, so `complete`
/// discloses nothing beyond them.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#16.3 — a parent probe the pins decide is a page guard, as a grant is.
pub fn page_guard_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    guard_decision(schema, scan, true)
}

fn guard_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan, pinned_guards: bool) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
    if table.through.is_some() { return None; }
    let rule = &table.rules.read;
    if !matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Or(_) | RuleExpr::And(_)) { return None; }
    let pinned = if pinned_guards { scan_pinned(table, scan) } else { Vec::new() };
    let pinned_map: BTreeMap<_, _> = pinned.iter().map(|(column, value)| (*column, *value)).collect();
    let alternatives = positive_ranges(rule).unwrap_or_else(|| rule.disjuncts().into_iter().cloned().collect());
    let guards: Vec<RuleExpr> = alternatives.iter()
        .filter_map(|disjunct| {
            let simplified = simplify_prefix(disjunct, &scan.table, table, &pinned_map);
            let mut fields = BTreeSet::new();
            simplified.fields(&mut fields);
            let admitted = if fields.is_empty() { self_disclosing(schema, &simplified) }
                else { fields.iter().all(|field| pinned.iter().any(|(column, _)| *column == field)) && pinned_probe_readable(schema, &simplified) };
            admitted.then_some(simplified)
        })
        .collect();
    (!guards.is_empty()).then(|| if guards.len() == 1 { guards.into_iter().next().unwrap() } else { RuleExpr::Or(guards) })
}

// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#16 — positive compound guards retain every conjunct,
// with the same seven-alternative bound as compile-time distribution.
fn positive_ranges(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Option<Vec<RuleExpr>> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Or(parts) => {
            let mut out = Vec::new();
            for part in parts {
                let alternatives = positive_ranges(part)?;
                if out.len() + alternatives.len() > 7 { return None; }
                out.extend(alternatives);
            }
            Some(out)
        }
        RuleExpr::And(parts) => {
            let mut out = vec![Vec::new()];
            for part in parts {
                let alternatives = positive_ranges(part)?;
                if out.len().saturating_mul(alternatives.len()) > 7 { return None; }
                out = out.into_iter().flat_map(|prefix| alternatives.iter().map(move |part| {
                    let mut conjunction = prefix.clone(); conjunction.push(part.clone()); conjunction
                })).collect();
            }
            Some(out.into_iter().map(RuleExpr::And).collect())
        }
        _ => Some(vec![rule.clone()]),
    }
}

/// The columns the scan's prefix pins, with their value expressions.
fn scan_pinned<'s>(table: &'s Table, scan: &'s Scan) -> Vec<(&'s String, &'s Expr)> {
    let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { return Vec::new() };
    index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).filter_map(|(part, key)| part.column().zip(key.scalar())).collect()
}

/// Every fact the grant reads is one the caller may read itself.
fn self_disclosing(schema: &Schema, grant: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
    disclosing(schema, grant, false)
}

/// A disjunct whose row columns a page pins (`exists cats[.cat, owner =
/// viewer]` under `.cat = cat`): whenever it holds, the caller can read every
/// row it probes, so a program's own `exists cats[cat, owner = viewer]` (which
/// sees only readable rows) answers exactly as the rule does. Pinned columns
/// are arbitrary values here: they pin nothing on the probed row's rule.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#16.1 — a printed branch on a parent probe must not narrow the probe's holders.
pub fn pinned_probe_readable(schema: &Schema, disjunct: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
    disclosing(schema, disjunct, true)
}

fn disclosing(schema: &Schema, grant: &RuleExpr, fields: bool) -> bool {

        RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().all(|item| disclosing(schema, item, fields)),
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#17 — negative evidence is admissible only on
        // a total, readable, lifetime-free point; hidden probes stay partial.

            schema.maintain_for(table).is_none() && schema.table(table).is_some_and(|table| table.index(index).is_some_and(|index|
                index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.components.iter().all(|part|

            let Some(target_table) = schema.table(target) else { return false };
            let Some(target_index) = target_table.index(index) else { return false };
            if target_table.through.is_some() || target_table.read_lifetime_sensitive() { return false; }
            let value = |term: &RuleTerm| match term {

            let pinned = |name: &str| pins.get(name);
            // The witness row's own read rule, decided by what the probe pins,

/// party or `where` lane must be decided by its prefix; a grant lane is

pub fn branch_merge_lazy(schema: &Schema, merge: &Merge) -> bool {

        let total_unique = index.unique && plain && index.components.iter().all(|component| component.column()

/// Exhaustion is observable only when the authored read rule itself contains
/// a top-level absence check on an exact required point keyed by this row's
/// intrinsic id. The lane stays private and evaluates the complete rule.
fn vacancy_branch(schema: &Schema, lane: &crate::ir::MergeLane) -> bool {
    if lane.source.is_some() || lane.scan.filter.is_some() || !lane.scan.prefix.is_empty() { return false; }
    let Some(outer) = schema.table(&lane.scan.table) else { return false };
    if outer.sync.is_some() { return false; }
    outer.rules.read.disjuncts().iter().any(|rule| {
        let RuleExpr::Not(inner) = rule else { return false };
        let RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } = inner.as_ref() else { return false };
        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };
        let Some(point) = target.index(index) else { return false };

            && point.unique && point.is_plain() && point.components.len() == 1
            && point.components[0].column().is_some_and(|column|
                target.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|kind| !kind.is_optional()))
    })
}

/// An intrinsic-id membership lane retains id as its logical merge position
/// even though the physical byId scan pins that sole component.
pub fn membership_point_id(schema: &Schema, lane: &crate::ir::MergeLane) -> bool {
    if !lane.membership() || lane.scan.range.is_some() { return false; }
    let Some(table) = schema.table(&lane.scan.table) else { return false };
    let Some(index) = table.index(&lane.scan.index) else { return false };

    }, &|rule| rule_true(rule, &lane.scan.table, outer_table, &pinned)

/// Every sibling of a varying membership witness must already hold. Preserve
/// authored Boolean grouping: every fixed conjunct is required, while proving
/// one fixed alternative is enough for an authored disjunction.
fn fixed_rule_holds<'a>(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
    outer_name: &str, outer_table: &Table, pinned: &impl Fn(&str) -> Option<&'a Expr>,
    pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
    if rule_true(rule, outer_name, outer_table, pinned) { return true; }
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(parts) => parts.iter().all(|part|
            fixed_rule_holds(schema, filter, part, outer_name, outer_table, pinned, pins)),
        RuleExpr::Or(parts) => parts.iter().any(|part|
            fixed_rule_holds(schema, filter, part, outer_name, outer_table, pinned, pins)),
        _ => fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, filter, rule, pins),
    }
}

/// A generated membership lane may retain prefix-fixed point grants
/// as a residual predicate.  Recognize only the exact bound point expression:
/// this proves that every row emitted by the lane satisfies that conjunct,
/// while a missing, reordered or merely similar probe keeps the merge out.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — fixed grants may guard one varying per-row grant.
fn fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
    pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
    if let Expr::Binary { op: crate::ir::BinaryOp::And, left, right } = filter {
        return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
            || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
    }
    // The compiler lowers a left-associated `a and b and c` into successive
    // Let/If pairs. Accept only that exact dataflow chain: every right operand
    // assigns the current result on its true branch, while `or` assigns on the
    // false branch and any extra statement or mismatched name is rejected.
    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter {
        if body.is_empty() || body.len() % 2 != 0 { return false; }
        let mut previous: Option<&String> = None;
        let mut matched = false;
        let mut at = 0;
        while at < body.len() {
            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
                condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = &body[at..at + 2] else { return false };
            let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice() else { return false };
            if !else_body.is_empty() || condition != name || assigned != name { return false; }
            if let Some(previous) = previous {
                if !matches!(left, Expr::Variable(input) if input == previous) { return false; }
            } else {
                matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins);
            }
            matched |= fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
            previous = Some(name);
            at += 2;
        }
        if !matches!((value.as_ref(), previous), (Expr::Variable(result), Some(previous)) if result == previous) { return false; }
        return matched;
    }
    let (Expr::Exists(read), RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. }) = (filter, rule) else { return false };
    let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };
    let Some(point) = target.index(index) else { return false };
    equalities.is_empty() && target.through.is_none() && target.not_before.is_none() && !target.is_expiring()
        && point.unique && point.is_plain() && point.components.iter().all(|part| part.column()
            .and_then(|column| target.columns.get(column)).is_some_and(|kind| !kind.is_optional()))
        && &read.table == table && &read.index == index && read.key.len() == key.len()
        && read.key.iter().zip(key).all(|(expression, term)| rule_term_expression(term, expression, pins))
        && point_read_is_visible(schema, read)
}

/// A rule probe uses system authority, but the retained residual point uses
/// ordinary caller authority. Re-prove that the generated, pin-substituted
/// point is visible instead of asking about the unsubstituted authored terms.
fn point_read_is_visible(schema: &Schema, read: &crate::ir::Read) -> bool {
    let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { return false };
    let Some(index) = table.index(&read.index) else { return false };
    if !index.is_plain() || index.components.len() != read.key.len() { return false; }
    let pinned = |name: &str| index.components.iter().zip(&read.key).find_map(|(part, value)|
        (part.column().is_some_and(|column| column == name)).then_some(value));
    visibility(&read.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned).decided_true
}

fn rule_term_expression(term: &RuleTerm, expression: &Expr, pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
    match (term, expression) {
        (RuleTerm::Viewer, Expr::Input(Input::Viewer)) => true,
        (RuleTerm::Literal(value), Expr::Literal(actual)) => value == actual,
        (RuleTerm::Field(field), expression) => pins.iter().find_map(|(name, value)|
            (name.as_str() == field).then_some(*value)).is_some_and(|value| value == expression),
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// A witness supplies one conjunct; every other conjunct must already hold
/// by the lane's pins. Distribution does not drop a status/role condition.
fn membership_rule(rule: &RuleExpr, witness: &impl Fn(&RuleExpr) -> bool, pinned: &impl Fn(&RuleExpr) -> bool) -> bool {
    match rule {
/// The guard a branch merge's grant lane is decided by: the page guards of
/// `page_guard_decision` (readable grants, and disjuncts the lane's pins
/// decide such as `exists boards[.board, owner = viewer]` under `.board = b`),
/// only when they are every disjunct of the read rule that is not a party
/// (`.col = viewer`). Otherwise a caller could be admitted by a disjunct the
/// lane cannot evaluate, and its page would miss rows.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#2 — every non-party disjunct must be a usable guard, or the read binds as its grant lane alone.
pub fn covering_grant(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
    let party = |d: &RuleExpr| matches!(d, RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(_), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(_)));
    let pinned = scan_pinned(table, scan);
    let pinned_map: BTreeMap<_, _> = pinned.iter().map(|(column, value)| (*column, *value)).collect();
    // `page_guard_decision` returns these prefix-simplified forms. Compare
    // the read rule in the same domain, including exact boolean pins.
    let others: Vec<RuleExpr> = table.rules.read.disjuncts().into_iter().filter(|d| !party(d))
        .map(|disjunct| simplify_prefix(disjunct, &scan.table, table, &pinned_map)).collect();
    // A rule of parties alone: the grant lane holds for nobody but the system,
    // which reads every row (GPT round 3, finding 1).
    if others.is_empty() { return Some(RuleExpr::Deny); }
    let guard = page_guard_decision(schema, scan)?;
    let guards: Vec<&RuleExpr> = match &guard { RuleExpr::Or(guards) => guards.iter().collect(), one => vec![one] };
    others.iter().all(|d| guards.contains(&d)).then_some(guard)
}

//! Shared type-directed positional point selection for programs and rules.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — identity wins; otherwise ordered compatible declared keys only.
use super::catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo};
use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, diag::Diagnostic, types::Ty};

pub(super) fn candidates<'a>(info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, types: &[(Ty, bool)], synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>) -> Vec<&'a IndexDecl> {
    let identity = read.index.is_none() && types.len() == 1 &&
        (types[0].0.required() == Ty::Id || types[0].0.required() == Ty::Ref(read.table.clone())
            || (!types[0].0.is_optional() && matches!(types[0].0.required(),Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t) if t==read.table)));
    info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
        .filter(|index| match &read.index {
            Some(name) => &index.name == name,
            None if identity => index.name == "byId",
            None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len(),
        })
        // Explicit indexes keep their existing mismatch diagnostics and range semantics.
        .filter(|index| read.index.is_some() || types.iter().zip(&index.columns).all(|((ty, literal), column)| {
            compatible(ty, *literal, &column_ty(info, index, column))
        })).collect()
}

fn column_ty(info: &TableInfo, index: &IndexDecl, column: &str) -> Ty {
    if index.computed.contains_key(column) { Ty::Int }
    else if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
    else { info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) }
}

fn compatible(ty: &Ty, literal: bool, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
    ty.comparable(expected)
        || (!ty.is_optional() && matches!((ty.required(), expected.required()), (Ty::Row(a) | Ty::WriteRow(a), Ty::Ref(b)) if a == b))
        || (literal && expected.is_identity())
}

pub(super) fn refusal(
    info: &TableInfo,
    read: &ReadExpr,
    types: &[(Ty, bool)],
    candidates: &[&IndexDecl],
    catalog: &Catalog,
) -> Diagnostic {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — lead a wrong-table positional lookup with the foreign reference's actual table.
    if candidates.is_empty() && let Some((position, source)) = types.iter().enumerate().find_map(|(position, (ty, literal))| {
        match ty.required() {
            Ty::Ref(source) | Ty::Row(source) | Ty::WriteRow(source) if source != read.table
                && !info.indexes.iter()
                    .filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
                    .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_some_and(|name| name == &index.name)
                        || (read.index.is_none() && index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len()))
                    .any(|index| index.columns.get(position)
                        .is_some_and(|column| compatible(ty, *literal, &column_ty(info, index, column)))) => Some((position, source)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }) {
        let value = read.keys.get(position).map(|key| crate::print::expression(&key.value)).unwrap_or_else(|| "this value".into());
        let mut expected = info.indexes.iter()
            .filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
            .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_some_and(|name| name == &index.name)
                || (read.index.is_none() && index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len()))
            .filter_map(|index| index.columns.get(position).map(|column| column_ty(info, index, column).name()))
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        expected.sort();
        expected.dedup();
        let expected = if expected.is_empty() { "a value accepted by a declared key".into() } else { expected.join(" or ") };
        return Diagnostic::new(
            &read.site,
            "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN",
            format!("`{value}` is a {source} reference, but {} key position {} expects {expected}; a reference from another table cannot select it", read.table, position + 1),
            format!("replace `{value}` with {expected}, and name the intended key columns or choose its index with `by`; if this is a population read, use `{} where .<column> = <value> first 20 by .id`", read.table),

        .guide("reads");
    }
    let alternatives = if candidates.is_empty() && read.index.is_some() {
        info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>()
    } else { candidates.to_vec() };
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.7 — `budgets['general']` (lane 020): a text value names the declared
    // text key far more often than a row id, so that named form leads (and is --fix's choice).
    let mut alternatives = alternatives;
    alternatives.sort_by_key(|index| index.columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id"));
    let mut changes = Vec::new();
    for index in &alternatives {
        // Same-column opposite-order indexes need the explicit name as well.
        let duplicate = info.indexes.iter().any(|other| other.name != index.name && other.unique
            && other.columns.len() == index.columns.len() && other.columns.iter().all(|c| index.columns.contains(c)));

        format!("{} has no declared unique key accepting these positional argument types in order; name the key columns, or use where for a population", read.table)
    } else {
        format!("{} positional argument types leave {} candidate keys; name the intended key columns or choose a compatible unique index explicitly", read.table, candidates.len())
    };
    let diagnostic = Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", message, "").guide("reads").repair_read(&read.site, changes);

pub(super) fn expand(tables: &Tables, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, requests: &mut Requests, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<MergeExpr> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#16 — implicit whole-group reads retain their proven schema bound.
    let mut authored = read.clone();
    // A named plain range states the same column order. Normalize only a
    // speculative merge input; a declined candidate retains the authored read.
    if authored.range.is_some() && authored.keys.is_empty() && authored.matching.is_none()
        && authored.computed.is_empty() && authored.cursor_prefix.is_none()
        && let Some(index) = authored.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| tables.get(&authored.table)?.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name))
        && index.is_plain() {
        authored.order_keys = index.columns.clone(); authored.index = None;
    }
    let normalized = bounded_page(tables, requests, &authored)?;
    let read = &normalized;
    // Vacancy is an exact negative lane, rather than a generic positive
    // grant branch. Classify it before the positive branch planner.
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — exact vacancy retains a full private scan.
    if let Some(merge) = super::intent_compound::vacancy(tables, synthesized, requests, read) { return Some(merge); }
    if let Some(merge) = scoped_membership(tables, synthesized, requests, read) { return Some(merge); }
    if let Some(merge) = membership(tables, synthesized, requests, read) { return Some(merge); }
    // An exact owner-or-membership rule belongs wholly to PAGE. If one of its
    // stricter eligibility checks declines, keep the original scan instead of
    // reinterpreting its probe as a generic grant branch.
    if owner_membership_shape(tables, synthesized, read) && !pinned_memberships(tables, synthesized, read) { return None; }
    let range = read.range.as_ref()?;
    let Some(branches) = branches(tables, synthesized, requests, read, true) else {
        return super::intent_compound::expand(tables, synthesized, requests, read);
    };
    let mut candidate = requests.clone();
    let errors = candidate.error_count();
    let mut lanes = Vec::with_capacity(branches.len());
    let mut order: Option<Vec<String>> = None;
    for (conjuncts, grant) in branches {
        let mut lane = read.clone();
        lane.filter = intent::conjunction(conjuncts);
        // The grant lane is the read as written: plan it exactly as the
        // single-index plan would, so the binding check's fallback to it is
    let Some(table) = tables.get(&planned.table) else { return false };
    if super::intent_lanes::prefix_decides_rule(tables, planned) { return true; }
    read_rule(table).is_some_and(|rule| by_form(rule, planned))
}

/// `allow`, `.col = viewer` pinned to `viewer`, `.col = 'x'` pinned to `'x'`,
/// and `or`/`and` of those.
pub(super) fn by_form(rule: &Expr, planned: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
    let pinned = |column: &str| planned.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_deref() == Some(column)).map(|key| &key.value);
    match rule {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "allow" => true,
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => by_form(left, planned) || by_form(right, planned),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => by_form(left, planned) && by_form(right, planned),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "=" => {
            let (column, value) = match (intent::column(left), intent::column(right)) {
                (Some(column), None) => (column, right.as_ref()),
                (None, Some(column)) => (column, left.as_ref()),
                _ => return false,
            };
            match (value, pinned(column)) {
                (value, Some(pin)) if is_viewer(value) => is_viewer(pin),
                (Expr::Literal { value, .. }, Some(Expr::Literal { value: pin, .. })) => value == pin && *value != Literal::Null,
                _ => false,
            }
        }
        _ => false,
    }
}

pub(super) fn read_rule(table: &TableInfo) -> Option<&Expr> {
    match table.decl.rules.iter().filter(|rule| rule.effect == Effect::Read).collect::<Vec<_>>().as_slice() {
        [rule] => Some(&rule.value),
        _ => None,
    }
}

fn disjuncts(value: &Expr, out: &mut Vec<Expr>) {
    if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = value && op == "or" { disjuncts(left, out); disjuncts(right, out); }
    else { out.push(value.clone()); }
}

/// `.col = viewer` (either side) on a principal column of the row.
fn party(table: &TableInfo, value: &Expr) -> Option<String> {
    let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = value else { return None };
    if op != "=" { return None; }
    let column = match (intent::column(left), intent::column(right)) {
        (Some(column), None) if is_viewer(right) => column,
        (None, Some(column)) if is_viewer(left) => column,
        _ => return None,
    };
    (table.columns.get(column)?.kind == TypeKind::Principal).then(|| column.to_owned())
}

/// A rule disjunct every row column of which the page pins: none for a role
/// or a seat, the parent's column for a parent probe under its pin.
pub(super) fn columns_within(value: &Expr, pinned: &[String]) -> bool {
    let mut copy = value.clone();
    let mut within = true;
    super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |value| {
        if let Expr::Member { base, name, .. } = value && matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }) && !pinned.contains(name) { within = false; }
        else if let Expr::Focal { .. } = value { /* reached through a member, checked there */ }
    });
    within && !has_focal_without_member(value)
}

/// A bare `.` (the whole row) is not a pinned column.
fn has_focal_without_member(value: &Expr) -> bool {
    match value {
        Expr::Focal { .. } => true,
        Expr::Member { base, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }) => false,
        Expr::Member { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => has_focal_without_member(base),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => has_focal_without_member(left) || has_focal_without_member(right),
        Expr::Exists { read, .. } => read.keys.iter().any(|key| has_focal_without_member(&key.value)) || read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(has_focal_without_member),
        Expr::Call { args, .. } | Expr::Array { items: args, .. } => args.iter().any(has_focal_without_member),
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// A predicate whose evaluation never refuses and never reads: comparisons,
/// `in` and boolean operators over the row's own columns, arguments, literals
/// and `viewer`. Arithmetic (even `now - 7d`, which overflows at the clock's
/// bound), assertions, calls and reads are left to the single-index plan, and
/// so is a member of anything but the row (`.meta.ok`, `m.ok`), which binds to
/// a read of the referenced row that a meter can cut (GPT round 4, finding 1).
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#2 — totality: no read, so a lane is cut only between candidates
pub(super) fn total(value: &Expr) -> bool {
    match value {
        Expr::Literal { .. } | Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } => true,
        Expr::Member { base, .. } => matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }),
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } => op == "not" && total(value),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } => matches!(op.as_str(), "=" | "!=" | "<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=" | "and" | "or" | "in") && total(left) && total(right),
        Expr::Array { items, .. } => items.iter().all(total),
        _ => false,
    }
}

fn is_viewer(value: &Expr) -> bool { matches!(value, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer") }
