//! A positional unique key followed by named tests is named-key sugar.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — only declared unique keys resolve positions; extra equalities test one row.
use super::{catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo}, intent_walk as walk};
use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, Site}, print};
use std::{borrow::Cow, collections::BTreeMap};

pub(super) fn keys<'a>(table: &TableInfo, parts: &'a [KeyPart], index: Option<&str>, site: &Site, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, [KeyPart]>> {
    if !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_some()) || !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none()) { return Ok(Cow::Borrowed(parts)); }
    let prefix = parts.iter().take_while(|key| key.column.is_none()).count();
    if let Some(late) = parts[prefix..].iter().find(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
        let ordered = parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_none()).chain(parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_some())).map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        return Err(Diagnostic::new(&late.site, "E_QUARRY_KEY_ORDER", "positional key components come before named column tests",
            format!("{}[{ordered}]", table.decl.name)).guide("reads"));
    }
    let declared: Vec<_> = table.indexes.iter().filter(|key| key.unique
        && !synthesized.contains(&(table.decl.name.clone(), key.name.clone()))).collect();
    let candidates: Vec<_> = if let Some(types) = types {
        let read = ReadExpr { table: table.decl.name.clone(), keys: parts[..prefix].to_vec(), index: index.map(str::to_owned), site: site.clone(),
            cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new() };
        super::positional_keys::candidates(table, &read, types, synthesized).into_iter()
            .filter(|key| key.unique && key.columns.len() == prefix).collect()
    } else {
        // Before types are known, do not silently turn `roles[viewer,
        // role = 'staff']` into an id lookup merely because byId has the
        // positional prefix's arity. A declared composite key covered by the
        // positional prefix plus named suffix must reach the typed pass, which
        // prints the fully named repair.
        let named: Vec<&str> = parts[prefix..].iter().filter_map(|part| part.column.as_deref()).collect();
        let partial_composite = declared.iter().any(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix && key.columns.len() <= parts.len()
            && key.columns[prefix..].iter().all(|column| named.contains(&column.as_str()))
            && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name));
        if partial_composite { Vec::new() }
        else { declared.iter().copied().filter(|key| key.columns.len() == prefix && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name)).collect() }
    };
    let chosen = match candidates.as_slice() {
        [chosen] if table.total_unique(chosen) => *chosen,
        _ => {
            let suggested = candidates.first().copied().or_else(|| declared.iter().copied().find(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix)).or_else(|| declared.first().copied());
            let descriptions = declared.iter().map(|key| format!("({})", key.columns.join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or ");
            let rewrite = if let Some(key) = suggested {
                let mut named = key.columns.iter().enumerate().map(|(at, column)| {
                    let value = parts.get(at).filter(|_| at < prefix).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value))
                        .or_else(|| parts.iter().find(|part| part.column.as_ref() == Some(column)).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value)))
                        .unwrap_or_else(|| format!("<{column}>"));
                    format!("{column} = {value}")
                }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                named.extend(parts[prefix..].iter().filter(|part| !key.columns.contains(part.column.as_ref().unwrap())).map(spelling));
                format!("{}[{}]", table.decl.name, named.join(", "))
            } else { format!("{}[id = <id>, {}]", table.decl.name, parts[prefix..].iter().map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) };
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN",
                format!("{} needs one full declared unique key with non-null columns before named tests; {prefix} positional components match {} keys; declared keys: {descriptions}", table.decl.name, candidates.len()), rewrite.clone())
                .source_rewrite(crate::diag::SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey, rewrite).guide("reads"));
        }
    };
    let mut named = parts.to_vec();
    for (part, column) in named.iter_mut().zip(&chosen.columns) { part.column = Some(column.clone()); }
    Ok(Cow::Owned(named))
}

fn spelling(part: &KeyPart) -> String {
    match &part.column { Some(column) => format!("{column} = {}", print::expression(&part.value)), None => print::expression(&part.value) }
}

pub(super) fn read<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    read_typed(table, read, catalog, None)
}

pub(super) fn read_typed<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    Ok(match keys(table, &read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site, &catalog.synthesized, types)? {
        Cow::Borrowed(_) => Cow::Borrowed(read),
        Cow::Owned(keys) => {
            let index = types.and_then(|types| super::positional_keys::candidates(table, read, types, &catalog.synthesized)
                .first().map(|index| index.name.clone())).or_else(|| read.index.clone());
            Cow::Owned(ReadExpr { keys, index, ..read.clone() })
        },
    })
}

/// Normalize before planning and sync inference, including helpers and shapes.
/// Invalid keys remain for the ordinary binder to diagnose at their use site.
pub(super) fn normalize(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    let tables: BTreeMap<_, _> = catalog.tables.clone();
    let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| {
        let (name, parts, index, site) = match value {
            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => (&read.table, &mut read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site),
            Expr::Write { table, keys, site, .. } => (&*table, keys, None, &*site),
            _ => return,
        };
        if let Some(table) = tables.get(name)
            && let Ok(Cow::Owned(named)) = keys(table, parts, index, site, &catalog.synthesized, None) { *parts = named; }
    };
    for table in catalog.tables.values_mut() {
        for rule in &mut table.decl.rules { walk::expression(&mut rule.value, &mut visit); }
        for (value, _) in &mut table.decl.final_exists { walk::expression(value, &mut visit); }
    }
    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
        walk::document(doc, &mut visit);
        for view in &mut doc.views { for branch in &mut view.branches {
            if let Some(predicate) = &mut branch.predicate { walk::expression(predicate, &mut visit); }
            walk::shape(&mut branch.fields, &mut visit);
        } }
    }
    catalog.shapes = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.shapes.iter().cloned().map(|shape| (shape.name.clone(), shape))).collect();
    catalog.functions = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.functions.iter().cloned().map(|function| (function.name.clone(), function))).collect();
}
//! Admission of replica-independent unique point keys. Populations and
//! maintained null groups have their own semantics.
use super::catalog::TableInfo;
use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, Diagnostic};

// Arguments remain in authored order, with the binder's normalized column
// names and presence facts (including control-flow narrowing).
pub(super) fn check(table: &TableInfo, index: &IndexDecl, read: &ReadExpr, arguments: &[(Option<&str>, bool)], rule: bool) -> Result<(), Diagnostic> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — the complete maintained group is unique even with nulls.
    // Extra predicates (such as value = 1) may use a secondary access index.
    // A partial group or a value-only key must still pass ordinary admission.
    if table.maintained.is_some() && table.indexes.iter().any(|key| key.name == "byGroup"
        && key.columns.iter().all(|column| index.columns.contains(column) && !index.computed.contains_key(column))) {
        return Ok(());
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — a fully pinned superset of a required unique key is itself unique.
    // Preserve role-bearing rule/postcondition indexes without adding a
    // redundant uniqueness constraint or changing their physical selection.
    let unique = index.unique || table.indexes.iter().any(|key| table.total_unique(key)
        && key.columns.iter().all(|column| index.columns.contains(column) && !index.computed.contains_key(column)));
    if !unique {
        let repair = if rule {
            "declare a non-null unique key for this probe; table rules cannot scan a population, so move population work into a query or mutation before storing the fact the rule tests"
        } else {
            "use a bounded population read (`where … first N`) or declare a non-null unique key"
        };
        return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POINT_KEY",
            format!("{}.{} is not unique: a point probe can choose different tied rows across replicas, even with non-null arguments", read.table, index.name),
            repair).guide("indexes"));
    }
    if !index.unique || table.maintained.is_some() { return Ok(()); }
    let named = arguments.iter().all(|(column, _)| column.is_some());
    let mut guards = Vec::new();
    let mut nulls = Vec::new();
    for (position, (column, present)) in arguments.iter().enumerate() {
        let column = if named { column.unwrap() } else { index.columns[position].as_str() };
        if index.columns.iter().any(|key| key == column) && !index.computed.contains_key(column)
            && table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|ty| ty.optional) && !present {
            if matches!(&read.keys[position].value, crate::ast::Expr::Literal { value: crate::ast::Literal::Null, .. }) { nulls.push(column); }
            else { guards.push(column); }
        }
    }
    if guards.is_empty() && nulls.is_empty() { return Ok(()); }
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — null ties have no replica-independent point;
    // require proof of presence without changing total population equality.
    let mut advice = Vec::new();
    if !nulls.is_empty() { advice.push(format!("replace literal null with a non-null value for {}", nulls.join(", "))); }
    if !guards.is_empty() {
        advice.push(format!("prove non-null arguments for {} with required types or presence guards", guards.join(", ")));
    }
    // Recovery choices are design advice. Do not print a source edit without
    // a context proof (a population can need a different index or bound).
    Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_NULL_KEY",
        format!("{}.{} is a nullable unique key: a null argument can tie several rows; a point is ambiguous across replicas because each device may hold different tied rows. {}; alternatively read a bounded population or key the row by id (guide indexes)", read.table, index.name, advice.join("; ")),
        "").guide("indexes"))
}

            }

        _ => false,
    }
}

/// `weaker` holds whenever `stronger` does: the same probe with fewer extra equalities.
fn implied_by(weaker: &RuleExpr, stronger: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
    match (weaker, stronger) {
        (RuleExpr::Exists { table: a, index: ai, key: ak, equalities: ae, .. }, RuleExpr::Exists { table: b, index: bi, key: bk, equalities: be, .. }) =>
            a == b && ai == bi && ak == bk && ae.iter().all(|pair| be.contains(pair)),

+witness: if it admits the caller, that guarded lane reads the whole authored
+range; otherwise it contributes no rows. Only a static point in the same
+conjunction as another atom enters the retained-filter proof above. This
+distinction preserves complete `static grant or membership` and
+`party or static grant or membership` pages without dropping any conjunct.

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR: a table rule uses an expression rules do not
//! evaluate (an ordered comparison outside insert rules and named update
//! transitions, the clock, arithmetic or a helper call). The rewrite moves the
//! author's own comparison into the mutation as `require … else CODE`.

use crate::ast::Expr;
use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Site};

/// What the rule language lacks for this term, if anything.
pub(super) fn unsupported_side(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&'static str> {
    match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "now" => Some("the clock (`now`)"),
        Expr::Call { .. } => Some("a helper or function call"),
        Expr::Binary { op, .. } if matches!(op.as_str(), "+" | "-" | "*" | "/" | "%" | "??") => Some("arithmetic"),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// The effect a rule judges, as the author spelled it.
pub(super) fn effect_word(insert: bool, update: bool, read: bool) -> &'static str {
    if insert { "insert" } else if update { "update" } else if read { "read" } else { "delete" }
}

/// The refusal for one comparison `cmp` in a `table`'s `effect` rule.
pub(super) fn diagnostic(site: &Site, table: &str, effect: &str, transition: bool, cmp: &Expr, what: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(cmp));
    let field = first_field(&text).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".to_owned());
    let code = format!("INVALID_{}", screaming(&field));
    let message = format!("`{text}` in the {effect} rule of {table} uses {what}, which table rules do not evaluate: rules compare columns, viewer and literals with = and !=, and order (<, <=, >, >=) only an insert row's own required int, time, decimal or money columns and integer literals (minor units beside decimal or money), or old/next in a named update (old, next) rule");
    // The mutation names the stored row `row` and supplies new values as arguments.
    let stored = |image: Option<&str>, name: &str| match image { Some("next") => name.to_owned(), _ => format!("row.{name}") };
    let inserted = |_: Option<&str>, name: &str| name.to_owned();
    let rewrite = match effect {
        "insert" => {
            let mutation = map_fields(&text, &inserted);
            if what == "an ordered comparison" {
                format!("compare this row's own required int or time columns or an integer literal in the insert rule, or check it in the mutation that inserts the row: `require {mutation} else {code}`")
            } else {
                format!("check it in the mutation that inserts the row, using the values it inserts: `require {mutation} else {code}`")
            }
        }
        "update" if !transition && what == "an ordered comparison" => {
            let next = map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!("next.{name}"));
            format!("name both images and bound the new one: `update (old, next) <- {next}`; or check it in the mutation: `row = {table}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` then `require {} else {code}`", map_fields(&text, &stored))
        }
        "read" if what.contains("now") => match direct_focal_field(cmp) {
            Some(expires) => format!("a read rule is an audience, which cannot change with time: hide rows past this row's stored time with `expire at .{expires}`, or filter the query (`{table} where {} …`)", map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!(".{name}"))),
            None => format!("a read rule is an audience, which cannot change with time: copy the deciding deadline into a required time column on {table} and use `expire at .expiresAt`, or filter the query (`{table} where {} …`); `expire at` accepts a local time column, not a reference hop", map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!(".{name}"))),
        },
        "read" => format!("a read rule is an audience, which cannot compare magnitudes: filter the query instead (`{table} where {} …`)", map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!(".{name}"))),
        _ => format!("check it in the mutation before the write: `row = {table}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` then `require {} else {code}`", map_fields(&text, &stored)),
    };
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR", message, rewrite).guide("rules")
}

/// An unnamed `update <- R` holds for the row before and after the write, so
/// its named form is `update (old, next) <- R(old) and R(next)`: every clause
/// kept, the ordered one included (lane 062). The mutation form follows.
pub(crate) fn named_update(table: &str, rule: &Expr, comparison: &str) -> String {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(rule));
    let wrap = |s: String| if text.contains(" or ") { format!("({s})") } else { s };
    let old = wrap(map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!("old.{name}")));
    let next = wrap(map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!("next.{name}")));
    let field = first_field(comparison).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".to_owned());
    let _ = table;
    format!("name both images; an unnamed update rule holds before and after the write, so the named form keeps both: `update (old, next) <- {old} and {next}`; or drop `{comparison}` from the rule and check the new value in the mutation: `require {} else INVALID_{}`", map_fields(comparison, &|_, name| name.to_owned()), screaming(&field))
}

/// Pasteable named form for a rule that already names one image. Bare focal
/// fields in a named transition refer to the new image.
pub(crate) fn named_update_source(rule: &Expr) -> String {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — preserve authored old/next images and map bare fields to next in named transitions.
    let text = crate::print::expression(rule);
    let body = map_fields(&text, &|image, name| match image {
        Some(image) => format!("{image}.{name}"),
        None => format!("next.{name}"),
    });
    format!("update (old, next) <- {body}")
}

/// A named transition's comparison the rule language cannot type, as the
/// mutation check that states the same condition.
pub(crate) fn transition_require(table: &str, cmp: &Expr) -> String {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(cmp));
    let field = first_field(&text).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".to_owned());
    let stored = |image: Option<&str>, name: &str| match image { Some("next") => name.to_owned(), _ => format!("row.{name}") };
    format!("check it in the mutation that updates the row: `row = {table}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` then `require {} else INVALID_{}` (the new values are the mutation's arguments)", map_fields(&text, &stored), screaming(&field))
}

/// A table rule's predicate as the check a mutation states before writing:
/// the row's fields become the values the mutation writes.
pub(crate) fn mutation_check(rule: &Expr) -> String {
    map_fields(&strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(rule)), &|_, name| name.to_owned())
}

/// `else CODE` after a table rule: the refusal of a rule is E_RULE.
pub(crate) fn rule_else(else_site: &Site, effect: &str, rule: &Expr, code: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(rule));
    let code = if code.chars().next().is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_uppercase()) { code } else { "NOT_ALLOWED" };
    Diagnostic::new(else_site, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
        format!("a table rule takes no `else`: a write its {effect} rule refuses is E_RULE; only `require … else CODE` and `unique … else CODE` name a refusal"),
        format!("keep `{effect} <- {text}`, and name the refusal in the mutation before the write: `require {} else {code}`", mutation_check(rule))).guide("rules")
}

fn strip_parens(text: &str) -> String {
    let trimmed = text.trim();
    if trimmed.starts_with('(') && trimmed.ends_with(')') {
        // Only strip a pair that encloses the whole text.
        let mut depth = 0i32;
        for (i, c) in trimmed.char_indices() {
            match c { '(' => depth += 1, ')' => depth -= 1, _ => {} }
            if depth == 0 && i + 1 < trimmed.len() { return trimmed.to_owned(); }
        }
        return trimmed[1..trimmed.len() - 1].to_owned();
    }
    trimmed.to_owned()
}

fn ident(c: char) -> bool { c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' }
fn ident_start(c: char) -> bool { c.is_ascii_alphabetic() || c == '_' }

/// The first column the comparison names: `.stars`, `old.highBid` or `next.at`.
fn first_field(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();
    for (i, c) in chars.iter().enumerate() {
        if *c == '.' && chars.get(i + 1).is_some_and(|c| ident_start(*c)) {
            return Some(chars[i + 1..].iter().take_while(|c| ident(**c)).collect());
        }
    }
    None
}

/// A time field owned by the focal row. Do not descend through a member
/// chain: `.hearing.releaseAt` must never become `expire at .hearing`.
fn direct_focal_field(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&str> {
    match expr {
        Expr::Member { base, name, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }) => Some(name),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => direct_focal_field(left).or_else(|| direct_focal_field(right)),
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } => direct_focal_field(value),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Rewrite each focal field `.f`, `old.f` and `next.f` through `map(image, f)`,
/// leaving other member accesses alone.
fn map_fields(text: &str, map: &dyn Fn(Option<&str>, &str) -> String) -> String {
    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();
    let mut out = String::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    let name_at = |at: usize| -> String { chars[at..].iter().take_while(|c| ident(**c)).collect() };
    while i < chars.len() {
        let c = chars[i];
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — field-looking text inside a literal is data, not a row-image reference.
        // Field-looking text inside a literal is data, not syntax. Preserve
        // the original spelling, including escaped quote characters.
        if matches!(c, '\'' | '"') {
            let quote = c;
            out.push(c);
            i += 1;
            while i < chars.len() {
                let literal = chars[i];
                out.push(literal);
                i += 1;
                if literal == '\\' && i < chars.len() {
                    out.push(chars[i]);
                    i += 1;
                } else if literal == quote {
                    break;
                }
            }
            continue;
        }
        let previous = if i == 0 { None } else { Some(chars[i - 1]) };
        if c == '.' && previous.is_none_or(|p| !ident(p) && p != ')' && p != ']') && chars.get(i + 1).is_some_and(|c| ident_start(*c)) {
            let name = name_at(i + 1);
            out.push_str(&map(None, &name));
            i += 1 + name.chars().count();
            continue;
        }
        if ident(c) && previous.is_none_or(|p| !ident(p) && p != '.') {
            let word = name_at(i);
            let after = i + word.chars().count();
            if matches!(word.as_str(), "old" | "next") && chars.get(after) == Some(&'.') && chars.get(after + 1).is_some_and(|c| ident_start(*c)) {
                let name = name_at(after + 1);
                out.push_str(&map(Some(&word), &name));
                i = after + 1 + name.chars().count();
                continue;
            }
            out.push_str(&word);
            i = after;
            continue;
        }
        out.push(c);
        i += 1;
    }
    out
}

fn screaming(name: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    for (i, c) in name.chars().enumerate() {
        if c.is_ascii_uppercase() && i > 0 { out.push('_'); }
        out.push(c.to_ascii_uppercase());
    }
    out
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn fields_move_into_the_mutation() {
        let stored = |image: Option<&str>, name: &str| match image { Some("next") => name.to_owned(), _ => format!("row.{name}") };
        assert_eq!(map_fields(".stars >= 1 and .stars <= 5", &|_, name| name.to_owned()), "stars >= 1 and stars <= 5");
        assert_eq!(map_fields("next.highBid >= old.highBid + old.increment", &stored), "highBid >= row.highBid + row.increment");
        assert_eq!(map_fields(".deadline > now", &stored), "row.deadline > now");
        assert_eq!(map_fields("helper(.a, viewer)", &stored), "helper(row.a, viewer)");
        assert_eq!(first_field("(old.highBid + 1)"), Some("highBid".into()));
        assert_eq!(screaming("drawAt"), "DRAW_AT");
        assert_eq!(strip_parens("(.a >= 1)"), ".a >= 1");
        assert_eq!(strip_parens("(.a) + (.b)"), "(.a) + (.b)");
    }
}

use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, value::object};
use snapback4_lang::compile;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

const UNION: &str = include_str!("fixtures/page4-union.q");
fn id(s: &str) -> Value { Value::Id(s.into()) }
fn setup() -> Engine {
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), UNION.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
    assert!(b.programs.iter().all(|p| snapback4_core::ir::body_merges(&p.program.body).iter().any(|m| m.branches())), "{:?}", b.programs);
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    let mut rows = vec![("members".into(), object([("id",id("m")), ("group",Value::Int(2)), ("principal",id("alice"))]))];
    for (at,(name,owner,group,open)) in [("a", "alice", 1, false), ("b", "bob", 2, false), ("c", "alice", 2, true), ("d", "bob", 3, true), ("e", "bob", 3, false)].into_iter().enumerate() {
        rows.push(("docs".into(),object([("id",id(name)),("owner",id(owner)),("group",Value::Int(group)),("open",Value::Bool(open)),("at",Value::Int(at as i64))])));
    }
    e.seed(rows).unwrap();
    e
}
fn walk(e: &mut Engine, query: &str, viewer: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut cursor=Value::Null; let mut out=Vec::new();
    for _ in 0..20 {
        let r=e.query(query,Context::new(viewer,BTreeMap::from([("c".into(),cursor)]))).unwrap();
        assert!(r.complete,"{r:?}");
        out.extend(r.data.as_array().unwrap().iter().map(|r| match r.field("id").unwrap() { Value::Id(s)=>s.clone(), _=>panic!() }));
        match r.next {Some(c)=>cursor=Value::String(c), None=>return out}
    }
    panic!("cursor did not terminate")
}
#[test]
fn owner_public_member_union_orders_and_deduplicates_every_audience() {
    let mut e=setup();
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e,"list","alice"),["a","b","c","d"]);
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e,"list","bob"),["b","c","d","e"]);
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e,"list","outsider"),["c","d"]);
}
#[test]
fn exact_where_witness_keeps_only_its_scope_even_for_the_system() {
    let mut e=setup();
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e,"scoped","alice"),["b","c"]);
    assert!(walk(&mut e,"scoped","outsider").is_empty());
    let mut cx=Context::system();cx.args.insert("c".into(),Value::Null);
    let r=e.query("scoped",cx).unwrap(); assert!(r.complete);assert_eq!(r.data,Value::Array(vec![]));
}

#[test]
fn two_party_exact_parent_witness_fixes_one_component_per_lane() {
    let source = "table reporting:
  manager: principal
  report: principal
  unique report
  read <- .manager = viewer or .report = viewer
  online only
table agendas:
  manager: principal
  report: principal
  at: int
  read <- (.manager = viewer or .report = viewer) and exists reporting[report = .report, manager = .manager]
  online only
query list(c: cursor ?):
  return agendas first 1 by .at after c
";
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_NEVER_COMPLETE"),"{:?}",b.notes);
    let mut e=Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap();
    e.seed([("reporting", "r1", "alice", "bob", 0), ("reporting", "r2", "carol", "alice", 0),
            ("agendas", "a", "alice", "bob", 1), ("agendas", "b", "carol", "alice", 2),
            ("agendas", "hidden", "alice", "erin", 3)].into_iter().map(|(table,name,manager,report,at)| {
        let mut fields=vec![("id",id(name)),("manager",id(manager)),("report",id(report))];
        if table=="agendas" {fields.push(("at",Value::Int(at)));}
        (table.into(),object(fields))
    })).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e,"list","alice"),["a","b"]);
    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e,"list","bob"),["a"]);
    assert!(walk(&mut e,"list","erin").is_empty());
}

    let p=&b.programs[0].program;
    assert!(snapback4_core::search_plan::ordinary::plan(&b.schema,p).is_some(),"{p:?}");
    assert!(b.schema.tables.values().all(|t|t.sync.is_none()));
    let mut e=Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap();
    e.seed([("members".into(),object([("id",id("m")),("group",Value::Int(2)),("principal",id("alice"))])),
            ("docs".into(),object([("id",id("a")),("group",Value::Int(2)),("owner",id("bob")),("open",Value::Bool(false)),("at",Value::Int(1)),("body",Value::String("granite core".into()))])),
            ("docs".into(),object([("id",id("hidden")),("group",Value::Int(3)),("owner",id("bob")),("open",Value::Bool(false)),("at",Value::Int(2)),("body",Value::String("granite core".into()))]))]).unwrap();
    for (who, count) in [("alice",1),("bob",0)] {
        let r=e.query("search",Context::new(who,BTreeMap::from([("q".into(),Value::String("gran".into())),("c".into(),Value::Null)]))).unwrap();
        assert!(r.complete,"{r:?}");assert_eq!(r.data.as_array().unwrap().len(),count);
    }
}

table members:

  read <- .principal = viewer

table children:
  parent: parents
  band: int
  read <- exists members[.band, viewer]
  sync to members[.band]
query list(parent: parents, c: cursor ?):
  p = parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return children where .parent = p.id first 1 by .id after c
";
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);

    e.seed([("parents".into(),object([("id",id("p")),("band",Value::Int(9))])),
            ("members".into(),object([("id",id("m")),("group",Value::Int(1)),("principal",id("alice"))])),
            ("children".into(),object([("id",id("a")),("parent",id("p")),("band",Value::Int(1))])),
            ("children".into(),object([("id",id("b")),("parent",id("p")),("band",Value::Int(9))]))]).unwrap();
    let r=e.query("list",Context::new("alice",BTreeMap::from([("parent".into(),id("p")),("c".into(),Value::Null)]))).unwrap();
    assert!(r.complete); assert_eq!(r.data.as_array().unwrap().len(),1);
    assert_eq!(r.data.as_array().unwrap()[0].field("id").unwrap(),&id("a"));
}

#[test]
fn parent_snapshot_keys_keep_completeness_when_the_page_is_assigned_and_cut_is_not_absence() {
    let source="table parents:\n  public 'parents'\n  sync public\ntable members:\n  group: int\n  principal: principal\n  unique group, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  sync to .principal\ntable children:\n  parent: parents\n  group: int\n  read <- exists members[.group, viewer]\n  sync to members[.group]\nquery direct(parent: parents, c: cursor ?):\n  p = parents[parent] ! else PARENT_MISSING\n  return children where .parent = p.id first 1 by .id after c\nquery assigned(parent: parents, c: cursor ?):\n  p = parents[parent] ! else PARENT_MISSING\n  page = children where .parent = p.id first 1 by .id after c\n  return page\n";
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);assert!(b.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v3"));
    let mut e=Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap();e.seed([("parents".into(),object([("id",id("p"))]))]).unwrap();
    let args=BTreeMap::from([("parent".into(),id("p")),("c".into(),Value::Null)]);
    for query in ["direct","assigned"] {
        let r=e.query(query,Context::new("alice",args.clone())).unwrap();assert!(r.complete&&r.data.as_array().unwrap().is_empty());
        e.set_ceilings(snapback4_core::Ceilings {examined:0,..Default::default()});
        let cut=e.query(query,Context::new("alice",args.clone())).unwrap();
        assert!(!cut.complete && cut.unavailable && cut.data==Value::Null && cut.next.is_none());
        e.set_ceilings(Default::default());
        let mut missing=args.clone();missing.insert("parent".into(),id("missing"));
        assert_eq!(e.query(query,Context::new("alice",missing)).unwrap_err().code,"PARENT_MISSING");
    }
}

#[test]
fn negative_covering_grant_requires_the_new_capability_and_local_negation_is_analyzer_only() {
    let source="table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable docs:\n  owner: principal\n  at: int\n  read <- .owner = viewer or not exists roles[viewer]\n  online only\nquery list(c: cursor ?):\n  return docs first 1 by .at after c\n";
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty());assert!(b.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v3"));
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),"table docs:\n  flag: bool\n  read <- not .flag\n  online only\nquery list():\n  return docs first 10 by .id\n".into())]);
    let note=b.notes.iter().find(|n|n.code=="N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap();assert!(note.message.contains("analyzer limitation"));assert!(!note.message.contains("truly partial"));assert!(note.rewrite.contains(".flag = false"),"{note:?}");
    let repaired=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),"table docs:\n  flag: bool\n  read <- not .flag\n  online only\nquery list():\n  return docs where .flag = false first 10 by .id\n".into())]);assert!(repaired.diagnostics.is_empty());assert!(!repaired.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_NEVER_COMPLETE"));
}

#[test]
fn existing_scoped_word_index_can_gain_only_the_missing_reverse_source_index() {
    let source="table members:\n  group: int\n  principal: principal\n  unique group, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable docs:\n  group: int\n  body: text <=100\n  at: int\n  by scoped: group, words(body), at, id\n  read <- exists members[.group, viewer]\n  online only\nquery search(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):\n  return docs matching q first 1 by .at after c\n";
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
    assert!(snapback4_core::search_plan::ordinary::plan(&b.schema,&b.programs[0].program).is_some());
    assert_eq!(b.schema.tables["docs"].indexes.keys().filter(|name|name.as_str()=="scoped").count(),1);
    assert!(b.schema.tables["members"].indexes.values().any(|index|index.columns().starts_with(&["principal".into(),"group".into()])));
}

#[test]
fn named_plain_page_and_pinned_membership_keep_every_party_complete() {
    let source=UNION.replace("  at: int", "  at: int\n  by named: at, id").replace("first 1 by .at", "first 1 by named");
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source)]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty());assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_NEVER_COMPLETE"));
    let source=UNION.replace(".owner = viewer or .open = true or exists members[.group, viewer]", ".owner = viewer or exists members[.group, viewer]")
        .replace("query list(c: cursor ?):\n  return docs first 1", "query list(g: int, c: cursor ?):\n  return docs where .group = g first 1");
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source)]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_NEVER_COMPLETE"),"{:?}",b.notes);
    let mut e=Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap();
    e.seed([("docs".into(),object([("id",id("a")),("owner",id("alice")),("group",Value::Int(1)),("open",Value::Bool(false)),("at",Value::Int(1))]))]).unwrap();
    let r=e.query("list",Context::new("alice",BTreeMap::from([("g".into(),Value::Int(1)),("c".into(),Value::Null)]))).unwrap();assert!(r.complete&&r.data.as_array().unwrap().len()==1);

## 4. Completeness and disclosure

- **Exact.** The page is `complete` when no lane is non-uniform, the meter
  did not cut, and the existing query-level conditions hold (position loss,
  device coverage, horizons). By §2.1 its rows are then exactly the first N
  readable matches after the cursor.
- **Nothing outside the rule.** Every lane is admitted by its own prefix
  verdict (`prefix_verdict`) or its grant before any row is read; a false
  verdict reads nothing. This is the admission a direct page on that prefix
  takes.
- **What `complete: true` and the meter reveal.** For a party or `where`
  branch, what a direct page on that prefix reveals: a verdict decided by
  facts the caller could read. For a grant branch, whether the grant holds,
  which §12.1 already reveals through a staff page's `complete` and which
  `grant_decision` restricts to grants whose every fact the caller can read.
  The number of lanes opened (one when a grant holds) carries the same bit.
  A grant charges the rule's static probe count on either verdict.
- **The token.** The version-4 descriptor hashes the branch set's index
  names and grant flags: schema facts. It does not encode row data.
- **Across pages.** The position is shared by every plan of the page, so a
  grant gained or lost between pages continues correctly from the same
  cursor; completeness is per page, as for every page.

## 5. The cursor

The continuation is one position: the shared position values (the order
columns, then `id`) of a row. Every lane resumes strictly after it on its own
index. One position suffices because it ends in the row id, so it totally
orders the union: the first N rows after `p` in the union are the first N
after `p` merged from each branch's rows after `p`, including a row several
branches hold.

The token is **version 4**, or **version 5** when inclusive:

```
[4 | 5, table, "$or:<branch-set SHA-256>", order, suffix, values, scope]
```

the layout of version 3 (an inclusive direct token), so the device decodes it
with the same code (sharing it saved 646 B of the web device). The index
field hashes the branch set in lane order: a domain marker, lane count, and
each index name with its byte length and grant flag, all unambiguously framed;
`scope` carries the argument-presence variant as other tokens do; the suffix
is the shared typed position. Encoding picks 4 or 5 exactly for a branch
descriptor, and decoding re-encodes and compares, so a version-4 or -5 token
without one, or a branch descriptor in an older version, is refused. The descriptor is bounded even when eight lanes reuse a legal 507-byte
index name: it is `$or:` followed by a SHA-256 digest, never the labels themselves.

- **Inclusive positions.** An unfinished first returned shape records its
  row's position inclusively (`shaping.rs`), on merges too (GPT review,
  finding 2). Version 5 carries it; every lane then seeks at or after
  the position, the row is re-read (and de-duplicated), and the existing
  no-progress check (`E_SHAPE_EXCEEDS_METER`) compares the canonical tokens.
- **Another plan's cursor restarts the read.** A token minted by this read's
  earlier one-index plan, or for another branch set, is refused with
  `E_CURSOR_INDEX`: "cursor belongs to another plan of this read; it now
  merges its branches (owner or another party): restart this read with a null
  cursor". This is new in the merge path; ordinary pages still refuse a
  foreign descriptor as `E_INPUT` (Muse review, finding 2).
- **The token is scoped to every lane's pins.** Its scope carries a SHA-256
  digest of the evaluated prefixes of all lanes, in lane order (their
  canonical encoding), and the generation, as a direct page's carries its
  prefix. The digest, not the prefixes: lanes that each fit the index key
  bound can together exceed the token's 4,096 bytes (three 1,600-byte folder
  pins; GPT round 4, finding 3). A cursor followed with other arguments,
  or by another viewer (a party pin is the viewer), refuses `cursor: …
  different prefix or generation` (`E_INPUT`) instead of resuming lanes over
  ranges it never saw (Muse round 3, finding 1).
- **Older engines refuse the token, and the plan.** A 0.4.22 engine or device
  decodes versions 4 and 5 as "cursor token version 4 is unsupported"
  (`E_INPUT`). A backend with a merged plan requires the schema capability
  `branch-merge-v1` (from the operation fact `branchMerge`, set when a
  merge sits anywhere in the program's IR, including a returned row
  shape's field; GPT round 4, finding 2), so an older
  device refuses the whole backend by name ("upgrade the engine/device")
  rather than running the plan or decoding a server's cursor (GPT round 3,
  finding 2).

## 6. Metering and ceilings

The meter charges what the plan does, in the existing units:

- each lane's opening is an ordinary scan: its prefix verdict (the rule's
  static probe count, once), one `examined` per candidate read (a batch of
  `take + 1` per lane), index entries as the store reports;
- one `step` when a lane's first head enters the heap and one per pop,
  including duplicate pops; a refill's push is not charged (as in every lazy
  merge; Muse review, finding 7);
- a grant charges the read rule's static probe count once, on either
  verdict.

With at most 8 lanes and a `take + 1` batch per lane, a lane never runs out
before the page finds its `(N+1)`th distinct row, so a cut can only happen
while the lanes open (GPT review, finding 4). The merge then keeps every row
it can prove is next: each lane records how far it examined (`through`: its
last returned row, or, when the cut fell inside its read, the last
rule-visible candidate it examined, which may be a filter reject;
`scan_consumed` reports it through the runner's `scan_frontier`, armed with
the lane's own table and index so a nested read's key never stands in). The
**frontier** is the least such position over the lanes still open. The page
emits only rows at or before the frontier, so it is a true prefix of the
merged order, and its `next` is the frontier: every lane has examined that
far, so resuming strictly after it loses nothing. When a lane examined nothing
past the cursor (it was never reached), the frontier is the cursor itself:
the page is empty, incomplete, without `next` (a cut while opening, as LLP
3000.007.000 §2). A page that fills before the frontier continues from its
last row as usual. Only the read meters (`examined`, `index_entries`,
`rule_probes`) cut; `steps` and `heap_bytes` abort the read, as everywhere.

## 7. Devices

The device runs the same interpreter over both stores, so the plan is the
same. Each lane applies the existing per-scan coverage rules: a lane over a
held partition is trusted (`trust_synced_read`) and complete when its group is
acquired and its horizon allows; a lane over an online-only table, an
unacquired group or beyond an acquired horizon marks the read unknown, and
the client asks the server (LLP 3000.028). A grant lane whose grant probes a
table is never decided on a device (§12.1): the read is unknown. A **seat**
(`viewer = 'service:audit'`) reads no table and is decided on a device too
(GPT review, finding 7): `read <- .owner = viewer or viewer = 'service:audit'`
syncs to `.owner` (service disjuncts leave the device audience), so the
owner's page answers from the held partition, complete, on both stores.

UNION integration (LLP 3000.036.000.000) admits synced party/group unions.
Those source tables retain ordinary per-arm scan planning: a grant lane that
probes membership would defer on a device despite the union's acquired
coverage. `intent_branches::branches` excludes sources whose sync needs contain
multiple arms. Online sources, held-group where-merges and service-seat plans
retain this RFC's merged-page behavior. The UNION simulator's snapshot-limit,
epoch and exhaustive owner/group worlds qualify the integration; the branch
merge suite continues to qualify its own surfaces.

The device oracle (`run-devices`) originally did not draw group-synced tables:
the first run that did found that a held group's pinned page charges one rule
probe on the server and none on a device, merged or not (the plain pin shows
it alone). That is a meter-parity gap older than this change. CUT (2026-10-12) repairs it:
delivered uniform pages reserve the server's logical prefix verdict
(LLP 3000.007 §20.24.7); the generator now draws group-synced tables.
The resolution is in
`issues/closed/20261012-snapback4-device-group-page-rule-probe-meter.md`.

## 8. Interactions

| Shape | Behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| `first` / `last` | Both directions; the heap orders by the shared position. |
| `after c` | §5. A cursorless top-N merge is complete when it fills (LLP 3000.027 §1). |
| Returned shapes | The page owns the merge site; shape positions use the shared suffix; inclusive positions as §5. |
| `exists t where W` | Case 1 only: `exists (branch 1) or … or exists (branch k)`, each a decided existence read, so `true` or `false`, never §15.12's "true or unavailable". With a grant, unchanged (§12 Q3). |
| `count`, `sum`, `min`, `max` | Unchanged (`E_QUARRY_BOUND` for an undecided total; §12 Q4). |
| Search (`matching`) | Unchanged; `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` keeps the membership-search rewrite (§12 Q5). |
| Maintained views, row views, ephemeral and timeline tables, `read through` | Unchanged (excluded in §2). |
| Mutations, jobs, shared helpers and shapes | Unchanged: an undecided page in a mutation stays `E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT`; a job reads as the system. |
| A capped membership branch (`.owner = viewer or exists shares[.parent, viewer]`) | Expanded under §14 when the row-dependent key is an ordinary target column. A target `id` key keeps the original scan. |
| Arithmetic, calls, `!`, a nested read or a member through a reference in the predicate | Unchanged (not total). |
| An order column a party or alternative would pin | Unchanged (the lane keeps it as a seek, so it would not decide). |
| `check` | `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` iterates the bound scans; a merge's lanes are decided and its grant lane is skipped, so the note and its per-party rewrite are not printed for a page the merge covers, and remain for every shape above. |
| `test.cases.jsonl` | A merged page answers `complete`; the default expectation accepts it. |

## 9. The Lean model

OPEN C8 said "a page is uniform exactly when every row column its read rule
reads is pinned; a non-uniform page is never complete". With 0.4.23 (LN) it
says *decided*, per caller: every column pinned, or a page guard that holds
over the caller's view (`decided`, `granted`). This change adds two syntactic
verdicts (`Eval.lean`): `decidedPins` (`Rule.byForm`, the pins' form, so an
argument that merely equals the viewer decides nothing, as in the engine) and
`branchDecided` (§2's cases 1 and 2 over the model's surface syntax:
totality, eligible pins, first pin per column, no order or `id` pins, the
width bound, 2–8 alternatives, at most 8 branches; queries only, never in a
helper's body; case 2 takes usable guards, `Rule.usableGuard`, which mirrors
the core's prefix simplification and `disclosing`: it substitutes literal and
viewer pins, turns known-false terms into `deny` before folding `and`, `or`
and `not` (so `or` drops a false arm), and
admits a negated Boolean comparison whose remaining field is pinned by a
stable required-Boolean argument. A
nullable `null` pin is never normalized to Boolean `false`, and a negated
probe remains ineligible. The verdict counts the grant lane toward the 8 and declines a
guarded page whose first order column is pinned). `exists` over branches
(case 1 only) is decided too (`existsRead`'s verdict).
The executable model carries those original expression pins into `granted`:
the disjunct must pass the same static `usableGuard` test before its evaluated
scalar pins are normalized. A runtime argument value therefore cannot turn a
compound negation that the planner rejected into a complete page.
The party-and-guard half of "nothing missed" is §2.1's argument; the proofs
cover the party half (`parties_cover_view`). `pageRead` takes the syntactic verdict and
ors it with LN's; the page's rows, order and cursor do not change, and a
merged page keeps `next` after a full page only when another matching row
follows (OPEN C2's merged arm). `pageRead_complete` concludes `decided ∨
form`, `pageRead_complete_store` (LN) holds for a page neither form nor
branches decide, and `pageRead_visible`, `page_rows_readable` and
`pageRead_perm` are restated over the new arguments; `Proofs/Branches.lean`
adds `partyLeaves_cover` and `parties_cover_view` (the party ranges miss no
readable row). `granted_readable` connects the executable model to the
original rule: normalization preserves the rule verdict, the normalized
positive guard lifts from the caller's view to the store, and pin substitution
preserves the selected row. Three examples run the shapes; LN's two examples of a guarded
page's non-holder now expect `complete` (45 examples in all).

The model checks completeness and data; its cut positions are abstract (OPEN
M1), so the frontier continuation is checked by the engine's meter oracle and
the tests, not by the model.

## 10. Verification

empty results nor completeness reveal hidden witness rows. Read-meter cuts
and frontier rules are unchanged; heap exhaustion remains a refusal.

A nullable small enum's `field != null` has a finite positive expansion into
its declared non-null values. This retains null exclusion exactly; ordinary
nullable inequalities against other values keep their original scan. The
wave-10 archive with four species therefore gets four enum ranges plus its
owner, readable reviewer and system ranges. Compound volunteer-open and
parent-teacher/status rules preserve each status condition when opening lanes.

**Readable static predicates.** A `where` disjunction may repeat a readable
static point, such as `.gleaner = viewer or exists coordinators[viewer]`.
Every point-key expression must be an infallible, read-free name or literal
(including canonical signed numeric literals); arithmetic, assertions, calls
and member reads keep the original scan and short circuit. The point must be
a total unique with fixed terms, readable by those pins or
its identical self-witness rule, without a lifetime or read-through boundary.
It remains in the original predicate for every caller, including the system.
This extension belongs to static party/where branch merges only. Membership
discovery and compound-rule expansion retain their read-free total filter
requirement, mirrored separately by model cases 3 and 4. The physical point
can cut even though its logical predicate is total. The
branch frontier therefore excludes an interrupted filter candidate; it may
continue only from an earlier fully evaluated candidate, so retry cannot
skip the interrupted row. A source its holders cannot read keeps its scan.

A discovered/guard-admitted merge range pays its admission reservation once
on both runtime hosts, including empty ranges. Device acquired-row trust does
not add a second prefix-rule reservation to an already admitted lane.

**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v2` fences guarded descriptors,
held-union merge behavior and fully pinned point-lane admission; it is derived
from bound plans, serialized as an optional operation fact, and older engines
refuse it through the ordinary handshake. Existing descriptor bytes remain
unchanged when none of these new lane types is present. Membership-only plans
retain the existing membership capability rules. The Lean surface model
removes its party requirement, and models the admitted positive rule ranges.
Enums refuse the entire model module with the existing explicit `type-enum`
reason before any program is declared supported. Compound where-branch
composition, nullable constant pins and physically nonportable/ambiguous
compound plans have their own named emitter exclusions. Native static
predicate keys that are arguments or signed expression nodes use the named
`page-static-key-expression` model boundary until their separate readability
classifier is extended; they are unsupported, never counted as oracle passes;
unsupported fragments remain explicitly named rather than accepted by the
oracle. Its logical leaf soundness/coverage proof includes pure membership.

**Hidden-source boundary and exact repairs.** PAGE3 does not scan private
parent rows whose owner cannot necessarily read them. No cap/index alone
repairs that absence proof. Copy the parent's immutable owner onto the child,
keep that copy and its parent reference immutable, keep the parent non-expiring,
and retain the reference with `on delete restrict`, and check the copy against the parent in the child's
insert rule (and in every trusted seed/backfill). Replace the parent-owner
read-rule leaf with the copied owner comparison. The copy preserves authority
only while the parent owner is immutable; a mutable owner needs transactional
propagation or the original rule. Existing data must be backfilled before
adoption. The diagnostic names the actual table, reference and owner column
and supplies exact source edits. It never recommends making parents public.

**PAGE3 qualification and review dispositions (2026-10-14).** Round 1
repaired null-exempt bounds, parent reference/lifetime preservation, outer
conjunction guards and capability scope; round 2 repaired fallible static keys,
block-lowered probe fencing and defensive model bounds. Round 3 named static
argument/signed-key model exclusions, with zero-oracle Unsupported regressions.
Round 4 separated readable-static model filters from the read-free gates for
membership/compound plans, with actual native/model data and completeness
comparisons. Round 5's two independent reviewers marked the same revision
READY. No unsupported model case is counted as a pass.

All 50 copied odd wave-10 final schemas compile: 18 notes in 11 schemas
become 2 in one (both hidden parents). Applying the exact checked copy repair
to a further copied corpus gives 0 in all 50. Per-shape counts, input/output
hashes and source edits are in `snapback4/reports/20261013-page3/`. Final
400-case campaigns have 22,277 native and 12,686 device executions without
disagreements; Lean's 1,108 executions retain only the three existing C2
pagination occurrences, with no new signature. Lean audits 59 theorems with
standard axioms only, runs 74 examples and seven fixtures. Focused language,
model, simulator and both device-store suites pass.

The clean locked Linux web artifact at `f11a5e72cd` is 2,310,393 B raw /
704,662 B gzip / 532,356 B brotli; budget 705,162 B = measured gzip + 500 B.
Family/absence audits and wasm/client builds pass. Later edits affect only
language/model/tests, preserving these device bytes. No push or publication.

## 17. PAGE4: complete multi-audience pages and exact scopes

Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-11 fix #11. This amendment extends the
existing bounded planner. No read authority, replicated audience, runtime
ceiling or cursor codec changes. Query unions containing a public status
are separate from union *sync*: a public sync branch remains excluded.

**Mixed positive alternatives.** Positive distribution also applies to a
simple disjunction mixing party equality, public/status equality, exact
membership and readable static grants. Each alternative retains every
conjunct and the complete original query predicate. Equality lanes,
guarded ranges and exact discovered membership lanes share the existing
ordered id-tied merge, with duplicate positions emitted once. At most seven
alternatives plus the system range fit. Speculative source/target indexes
are retained only when the bound validator accepts the whole plan.

Named plain page indexes normalize to their declared column order only in
a speculative merge candidate. A declined candidate keeps its original read.
An exact owner/member rule whose varying component is necessarily pinned
can use the existing party/guard merge; alternative-only pins do not qualify.

**Exact where witnesses.** A positive whole `where` conjunct identical to a
whole read-rule disjunct can define an exact narrower audience: for example
`posts where exists authors[.id, viewer] first 20 by .at after c`. Discover
that readable witness under §§14–16's total point and complete-discovery
proofs. Other audiences cannot contribute rows to W. Every other conjunct
must be read-free and total. The original predicate remains in admission
validation; the system descriptor retains the authored witness, because
system bypasses R but never W. Its prefix also binds the fixed target pins
into the shared cursor scope. A hidden source keeps the original scan.

**Pinned components of exact witnesses.** A positive alternative may pin
one component of a multi-column rule probe. Substitute only a necessary
infallible fixed equality, leaving one varying source component; the core
validator independently checks the original field term against that exact
target prefix. Example: `.manager = viewer and exists reporting[report =
.report, manager = .manager]` discovers reporting rows with manager=viewer,
then their report values. The other party's alternative inverts the other
component. Extra named point equalities survive. No arithmetic, missing
pin, second varying component or hidden source is treated as proof.

**Parent snapshots.** Selecting `s.id` or a stored scalar field of a required
point-read row image is a fixed lexical value, not a new row read. In a
standalone page's predicate (returned or assigned), the compiler binds those
selections once before planning. Optional rows, reference hops, private/maintained fields,
rebindings and joined paths keep the original predicate. A snapshot does
not prove that a child's copied band/manager equals its parent's field;
source repairs must say when their extra pins narrow the authored scope.

**Membership word search.** The existing compact ordinary server search may
derive its exact two-component total membership audience on an online-only
table too. Provision the reverse readable source index and scoped words
index in one checked speculative candidate. Synced targets retain their
same exact audience requirement. The device program and sync holdings do
not acquire a predicate; empty needles retain the original browse arm.
Readability, non-expiring source, portable keys, opening meter refusal,
word-head merge and original cursor binding remain LLP 3000.028 §3.3's.

**Honest incomplete forms.** A fallback note distinguishes an analyzer
limitation (the current admitted ranges cannot prove this positive shape)
from a truly partial read (its range/source cannot establish absence under
its declared authority). It names the exact complete line when one can be
verified; scope-narrowing pins say that they narrow the answer. A hidden
parent retains PAGE3's checked copy/backfill repair. No cap/index-only
suggestion is offered for an unreadable witness, and no copied relationship
is assumed from a successful parent read alone.

**Readable negative static points and finite enums.** A negated static
probe is a page guard only when its selected key is total unique, its source
is readable at that key and has no maintained-state, read lifetime or read-through boundary.
Its absence is then an observable authorized fact. A hidden negative probe
still cannot open a complete range. Required enum inequality against a
literal expands to its exact remaining variants; optional enum inequality
against a non-null value stays residual. No varying negative source is
inverted. This completes the renovation selectionsFor page (its unpaged budget aggregation
retains an analyzer limitation) without
turning timed privacy into an unsupported absence claim.

A discovered/guard-admitted merge range pays its admission reservation once
on both runtime hosts, including empty ranges. Device acquired-row trust does
not add a second prefix-rule reservation to an already admitted lane.

**Compatibility and model.** Existing descriptor bytes and semantics serve
mixed unions and exact where witnesses. `branch-pages-v3` fences pinned multi-column witnesses, readable negative
static guarded/covering ranges and the compiler-owned `RequireUncapped`
primitive before an older validator or decoder can encounter them. A nullable
row assertion in a query checks the read meter after evaluating its point;
a cut raises the internal E_BOUND that the query boundary reports as an
unavailable incomplete answer, instead of inventing the author's absence error.
Ordinary absent/hidden rows still use the authored error, and mutation
assertions retain their transactional policy.
The independent Lean surface classifier must reproduce positive mixed
ranges, pinned witness substitution and exact where scope. Static snapshot
binding is an explicit syntax normalization, like where-guard lowering.
Word search stays a named unsupported model fragment; it must execute zero
Lean oracle cases, while native/device tests cover its existing words engine.
The direct-grant soundness theorem remains positive: negative-grant scalar
absence/aggregation, fallible-filter fallback and descriptor overflow have the
explicit `page-negative-grant-fallback` model boundary, including negative
probes nested in conjunctions and made static by prefix pins, and overflow of
distributed alternatives inside conjunctions, with zero oracle
executions. Standard bounded negative-grant pages use the independent range
classifier and are exercised against the model. No unsupported case is counted
as a pass. Fresh Muse and Codex reviews both mark semantic revision
`193f94ea9da7a046f4ce6fe76d7b527e1686ef90` READY. See the
[verbatim paired reviews and provenance](reviews/3000.036-page4.provenance.md).
Qualification is recorded separately in the PAGE4 workstream report.

Model fidelity: lexical witness pins use an opaque classifier-only parameter
term, preserving variable identity instead of converting observed argument
values to literals or viewer proofs. The oracle rejects that term in authored
rule JSON; executable authority always evaluates the original rule. Named
plain orders normalize only within the independent unfiltered mixed-merge
fragment. Filtered/one-party named candidates outside that fragment (including declined
native candidates) have the explicit
`page-named-index-fallback` boundary and execute zero oracle cases. No such excluded candidate is claimed to be a native failure. This
retains native fallback semantics instead of claiming a complete owner pin
on an authored fixed index. Both review counterexamples have regressions;
the generator includes argument-valued pinned pairs.

Delivered-history integration: an acquired device history is an audience in
its own right under LLP 3000.007 §18.2, even after live membership expires.
A membership merge on such a target selects its existing whole-range system
descriptor on the device without changing the actual viewer. It retains the
authored W, acquisition, lifetime, retention, meters and shared cursor scope;
only current-source rediscovery is bypassed. Server execution is unchanged.
`branch-pages-v3` fences this device interpretation. Existing expiry/paged
snapshot history regressions run on both stores. NARR retains ownership of
P9's general unbounded-sync/empty-partition narration; this compatibility
correction arose from PAGE4 named-order planning.

## 18. PAGE5: bounded products, fixed guards and exact vacancies

Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-12 fix #12. PAGE5 extends the bounded PAGE
planner only where every admitted lane still has a constructive exhaustion
argument. It does not turn arbitrary Boolean authorization into a complete
query plan.

**Bounded products.** The planner distributes independent positive
alternatives in the authorization rule across conjunctions before it
classifies the resulting lanes.

//! Bind key operands once, retaining authored sequencing and contextual literals.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    pub(super) fn bind_key_parts(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, end: usize,
        keys: &mut Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
        for (position, part) in read.keys.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(keys.len()) {
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let expected = part.column.as_deref().or_else(|| read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)).and_then(|i| i.columns.get(position)).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column));
            let (e, t) = match expected {
                Some(expected) => self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup, &expected)?,
                None => self.expr(&part.value, &mut setup)?,
            };
            self.sequence_setup(keys.iter_mut().map(|(_, e, _)| e), setup, out);
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            keys.push((part.column.clone(), e, t));
        }
        self.contextualize_bound_keys(info, read, keys)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Apply the selected index's identity context to operands already bound
    /// for type-directed positional selection. This changes only their
    /// retained IR/type and never evaluates the authored expression again.
    pub(super) fn contextualize_bound_keys(&self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        if let Some(index) = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)) {
            for (position,(column,value,ty)) in keys.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().or_else(|| index.columns.get(position).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|c| key_identity(info,c)) {
                    if let Expr::Literal(Value::String(text)) = value {
                        if text.is_empty() {
                            return Err(type_error(read.keys[position].value.site(), "an empty text literal cannot be an id", "use a non-empty id literal, or null"));
                        }
                        *value = Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone()));
                        *ty = expected.required();
                    } else {
                        (*value,*ty) = self.row_identity(value.clone(),ty.clone(),&expected);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // Mixed-key normalization names an already-bound positional prefix. Only
    // a literal acquires new context; compound expressions keep their IR/setup.
    pub(super) fn name_bound_keys(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        for ((column, expr, ty), part) in keys.iter_mut().zip(&read.keys) {
            *column = part.column.clone();
            if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column))
                && matches!(&part.value, A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(_), .. }) {
                (*expr, *ty) = self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup::Body::default(), &expected)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn key_identity(info: &TableInfo, column: &str) -> Option<Ty> {
    let ty = if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
        else { Ty::from_scalar(info.columns.get(column)?) };
    ty.required().is_identity().then_some(ty)
}
//! Compiled examples first; diagnostic details are under `why`.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Reads

Read authorized rows. `sync … last N` bounds acquisition; cursors page server history (offline: acquired rows).

```quarry
query readForms(roomId: rooms, messageId: messages, c: cursor ?):
  one = messages[messageId]
  must = messages[messageId] ! else NOT_FOUND
  roster = members where .roomId = roomId
  member = exists members[roomId, viewer]
  admin = exists members[roomId = roomId, principal = viewer, role = 'admin']
  return { one, must, roster, member, admin }
query newest(roomId: rooms, c: cursor ?):
  return messages where .roomId = roomId and .body != null last 20 by .at after c
query recentTotal(roomId: rooms, start: time, end: time):
  return count(messages where .roomId = roomId and .at >= start and .at <= end)
```

`one` returns a row/null; `must` refuses missing/hidden rows.
`roster` reads the capped room; `member`/`admin` test membership.
`newest` pages newest-first; `recentTotal` counts under the meter.
Repeated `where` clauses combine with `and` in order.

## Limits

- A positional key names one row; use `where` for populations: E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN.
- Add a cursor if the screen pages: N_NO_CURSOR.
- Unmerged pages with unpinned rule columns cannot certify all callers. N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE names a readable pinned-parent guard; N_NEVER_COMPLETE has none (separate grants may still admit callers).
- A nested read with ambiguous modifiers needs parentheses: E_QUARRY_MODIFIER_SCOPE.
- N_SHAPE_METER: maintained totals (guide views), previews, or queries.

## Details

Unreadable query references act absent: null points or complete empty pages. Mutation references refuse; ids grant no authority.

Reads select authorized rows by id, predicate or search text.
Add an order, page size and cursor to browse a population.

Projections drop `id` unless listed: `{ title }` omits id; `{ id, title }` preserves it. Whole rows retain id.


### Pages and cursors

State the rows, then the order and page. `messages where .roomId = roomId
last 20 by .at after c` means the newest matching messages. The compiler
chooses the index. Positional keys select identity for `id`/same-table references,
otherwise the declared unique key accepting types in order; ambiguous types need named columns.
The rules run before any projection, count or order reaches the caller.
Declare `c: cursor ?`, read `after c`, pass each answer's `next` as `c`; `usePage` does that.

### Completeness

Pinned-parent guards are caller-dependent (`N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE`; `guide recipes`).
`T[]` requests a complete fitting group: `return children[] where .parent = parent`.
It needs authority and a cap/unique bound. Update capped cases that now answer complete.

`complete` means the query answered its declared request within known coverage and execution limits.
Filling a declared page bound is complete.

Every ready `Read` carries `cut: 'meter' | 'words' | null` beside `complete`,
on the server and either device store. An empty page with `cut: 'meter'`
means partial: retry narrower, or continue if `next` exists; never "no matches".
`words` means the first term expanded past 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope: narrow that
term or use scoped search (guide search). If both limits cut, `meter` wins.
Device reads and uniform server reads keep precise reasons.
Non-uniform server reads report `cut: null`: the reason is withheld, with `complete: false` still truthful.
`null` means "not disclosed here", not "not cut"; it also covers a horizon,
missing coverage. For `complete: false` with
`cut: null`, render "Results may be incomplete", even when empty; never "no matches".
A complete empty page has `cut: null` and `complete: true`.
Same-key observations retain their last answer marked `revalidating` during
plain refetch, reconnect and transport failure. Omitted `keep` and `keep: true`
share this policy; `keep: false` withdraws immediately. Inserts and non-policy updates retain through retries; deletes, policy-input changes and unknown evidence start a nonrenewable one-second bound. Bounded failure/timeout withdraws; replacements can still publish.
A notified cut keeps older replica answers ineligible until sync publishes it.
A whole server answer at that cut may replace sooner. Expiry, sign-out,
credential, store, schema and generation resets still withdraw immediately.
`fresh` and `since` describe server freshness, independently of revalidation.
With `keep: true`, a watch also lingers for 30 seconds after its last subscriber
leaves, sharing the same observation, expiry, invalidation and reset fences.
Local commits still revalidate it while unmounted; account/backend changes,
`clear_rows`, `settle_exit`, ephemeral resets and close end its authority.
See `guide client` for the complete retention and screen-lifetime recipe.
A ready list read always carries an array; an absent point can still be null.
`usePage` returns `items: []` while its first page is loading; earlier pages
remain visible while a later page is unavailable.

For device reads, online fallback asks only for missing facts or unknown presence.
Meter/word cuts and cursorless pages stay local, as do held queries and retained left groups.
Predictable pending writes block server fallback for dependent reads; unknown prediction
footprints block every query. Unrelated writes do not block. Withheld speculation
(past-horizon totals, extrema or points) stays `loading`; local cuts stay partial.
For one-shots, see `guide client`.
Keep a screen query locally complete (`snapback4 why <query>`); put server-needing
decorations in their own query so the main list never waits on them.
Keep the screen shell and draft mounted; render current reads.

For an expiring table, return the deadline with each row:

```quarry
query expiringMessages(roomId: rooms, c: cursor ?):
  return messages where .roomId = roomId after c last 20 by .at { id, body, expiresAt }
```

Held device observations republish at `nextExpiry`, including offline; render
those rows directly. Timers follow the row's deadline; a suspended page or a
busy host can delay the callback.
Only server-answered reads need a render-deadline filter to hide a returned
row while its next network answer is pending. Return `expiresAt`, as above.
In React, `useNow(deadline)` owns the timer and responds to clock estimates:

```ts
import { useClient, useNow } from "snapback4/react";
function useExpiringRows<T extends { expiresAt: number | null }>(rows: T[]) {
  const client = useClient();
  const deadline = Math.min(...rows.flatMap(row => row.expiresAt !== null && row.expiresAt > client.now() ? [row.expiresAt] : []));
  const now = useNow(Number.isFinite(deadline) ? deadline : null);
  return rows.filter(row => row.expiresAt === null || row.expiresAt > now);
}
```

Render the rows returned by `useExpiringRows(rows)`. `client.now()` estimates the server's time and never
moves backward; `null` never expires. The hook cleans up on unmount.

For typing and viewing indicators, use an ephemeral table (`guide ephemeral`,
also `guide typing`). Keep its server query separate from the device-held
chats list. The expiring-rows recipe above is for durable disappearing messages.

## Measure live propagation

Mounted reads wake from `/changes`, sync and re-evaluate affected queries, including parent and authorization dependencies.
Measure two signed-in clients with queries mounted: record sender invocation, `sent` acknowledgement, changed `observe` snapshot, React `useLayoutEffect` and image `onLoad` separately.
Acknowledgement follows commit; `waitSeconds: 0` measures custody. Inspect `/changes`, `/sync` and query requests in Network to separate transport from application work.
Measure media separately; repeat 20+ times, idle and loaded.

## Nullable predicates and enum lanes

For a required enum with at most eight variants, `.status != 'done'` opens
the same pinned value lanes as `.status in ['todo', 'doing']`. A bound value
can also select the excluded variant. Fallible expressions remain residual
predicates so their guards still run first. Nullable enums retain a residual
inequality, including null rows; larger enums also stay residual.
Value lanes are chosen only when every lane's prefix decides the source read
rule. Otherwise both `!=` and `in` remain residual filters on one scan, which
can continue through visible rows with `next`; the non-uniform read still
reports `complete: false`.

```quarry
table workTasks:
  owner: principal
  cap 100 by owner
  due: time ?
  status: enum('todo', 'doing', 'done')
  assignee: principal ?
  createdAt: time
