fn rule_term(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, expr: &Expr, bindings: &RuleBindings) -> Fallible<(RuleTerm, Ty)> {
    let site = expr.site();
    match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "viewer" => Ok((RuleTerm::Viewer, Ty::Principal)),
        Expr::Member { base, name, .. } => match base.as_ref() {
            Expr::Read(read) if read.keys.len() == 1 && read.keys[0].column.as_deref().is_none_or(|c| c == "id")
                && read.range.is_none() && read.filter.is_none() && read.matching.is_none()
                && read.index.is_none() && read.seeks.is_empty() => {
                let result = rule_reference(catalog, info, &read.keys[0].value, name, bindings, site)?;
                if result.0.references().last().is_some_and(|(table, _)| table == read.table) { Ok(result) }
                else { Err(type_error(site, "the lookup table differs from the reference type", "use the reference's declared table")) }
            }
            Expr::Focal { .. } => {
                if bindings.update && bindings.old.is_some() {
                    return Err(unknown(site, "an update (old, next) rule names its images; . is not bound", format!("next.{name}")));
                }
                let ty = column_ty(info, name).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("rule reads undeclared column {}.{name}", info.decl.name), name_rewrite(name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)))?;
                Ok((RuleTerm::Field(name.clone()), ty))
            }
            Expr::Name { name: binder, .. } if bindings.old.as_deref() == Some(binder) => {
                let ty = column_ty(info, name).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("rule reads undeclared column {}.{name}", info.decl.name), name_rewrite(name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)))?;
                Ok((RuleTerm::OldField(name.clone()), ty))
            }
            Expr::Name { name: binder, .. } if bindings.next.as_deref() == Some(binder) => {
                let ty = column_ty(info, name).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("rule reads undeclared column {}.{name}", info.decl.name), name_rewrite(name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)))?;
                Ok((RuleTerm::NextField(name.clone()), ty))
            }
            Expr::Name { name: binder, .. } if bindings.update && bindings.old.is_none()
                && matches!(binder.as_str(), "old" | "next") => Err(Diagnostic::new(site,
                    "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR",
                    format!("`{binder}.{name}` names a row image, but this update rule did not bind old and next"),
                    "write this as a named transition").guide("rules")),
            other => rule_reference(catalog, info, other, name, bindings, site),
        },
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if bindings.update && (bindings.old.as_deref() == Some(name) || bindings.next.as_deref() == Some(name)) => Err(unknown(site, format!("select a field of {name} explicitly"), format!("{name}.id"))),
        Expr::Name { name, .. } => Err(unknown(site, format!("`{name}` is not a rule term; rules read the row as .{name}"), format!(".{name}"))),
        Expr::Literal { value, .. } => Ok(match value {
            Literal::Str(text) => (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(text.clone())), Ty::Text(text.chars().count() as u32)),
            Literal::Number(text) => (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(super::integer_literal(text, site)?)), Ty::Int),
            Literal::Bool(b) => (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Bool(*b)), Ty::Bool),
            Literal::Null => (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Null), Ty::Null),
        }),
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "-" => {
            let (term, _) = rule_term(catalog, info, value, bindings)?;
            match term {
                RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(n)) => Ok((RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(-n)), Ty::Int)),
                _ => Err(type_error(site, "a negative rule literal needs an integer", "compare .column with an integer literal")),
            }
        }
        other => {
            let _ = catalog;
            Err(unknown(site, format!("`{}` is not a rule term", crate::print::expression(other)), "compare .column with viewer or a literal"))
        }
    }
}

fn column_ty(info: &TableInfo, name: &str) -> Option<Ty> {
    if name == "id" {
        return Some(Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()));
    }
    info.columns.get(name).or_else(|| info.captures.get(name)).map(Ty::from_scalar)

        && catalog.maintains.get(&read.table).filter(|m| m.row_key.is_none()).and_then(|maintain| catalog.tables.get(&maintain.source)).is_some_and(|source| source.decl.expire.is_some() || source.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.expiring)) {
        return Err(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_LIVE_MAINTAIN_PROBE", format!("a rule cannot probe {}.value because its source expires", read.table),
            "read the view in the program; its live value cannot be probed at one unit").guide("expire"));
    }
    if let Some(names) = &named {

            if !ty.comparable(&expected) && !(expected.is_identity() && matches!(term, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(_)))) { return Err(type_error(site, format!("{column} is {}; {}.{column} is {}", ty.name(), read.table, expected.name()), format!("use {} for {column}", expected.name()))); }
            if expected.required().is_identity() && let RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(text)) = term { *term = RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone())); *ty = Ty::Id; }
            if let (Ty::Enum(variants), RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(text))) = (expected.required(), &*term) {
                if !variants.contains(text) { return Err(type_error(site, format!("'{text}' is not one of {}.{column}'s variants {}", read.table, variants.join(", ")), format!("'{}'", variants[0]))); }
            }
        }
    }
    if named.is_none() {
        let typed = types.iter().cloned().zip(terms.iter().map(|(_, term)| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(_))))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let declared = super::positional_keys::candidates(target, read, &typed, &catalog.synthesized);
        if declared.len() != 1 {
            return Err(super::positional_keys::refusal(target, read, &typed, &declared, catalog));
        }
        if read.index.is_none() {
            let mut selected = read.clone();
            selected.index = Some(declared[0].name.clone());
            return probe(catalog, info, &selected, site, bindings, extras);
        }
    }
    let mut candidates: Vec<&IndexDecl> = target
        .indexes
        .iter()
        .filter(|index| index.is_plain())
        .filter(|index| named.is_some() || !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
        .filter(|index| match &read.index {
            Some(explicit) => &index.name == explicit,
            None => true,
        })
        .filter(|index| match &named {
            Some(names) => (index.columns.len() == names.len() && names.iter().all(|n| index.columns.contains(n))) || (extras && target.total_unique(index) && index.columns.iter().all(|column| names.contains(column))),
            None => (index.unique || read.index.is_some()) && index.columns.len() == terms.len(),
        })
        .filter(|index| {
            index.columns.iter().enumerate().all(|(position, column)| {
                let ty = match &named {
                    Some(names) => names.iter().position(|n| n == column).map(|p| &types[p]),
                    None => types.get(position),
                };
                let expected = if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(read.table.clone()) } else { target.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) };
                ty.is_some_and(|ty| ty.comparable(&expected) || (expected.is_identity() && matches!(terms[position].1, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(_)))))
            })
        })
        .collect();
    if extras && named.is_some() {
        if let Some(width) = candidates.iter().filter(|i| target.total_unique(i)).map(|i| i.columns.len()).min() {
            candidates.retain(|i| target.total_unique(i) && i.columns.len() == width);
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — named equalities covering two total unique keys
            // test one row either way (the other key's columns are extra
            // equalities on it), so the first declared key decides; no ambiguity.
            candidates.truncate(1);
        }
        else if candidates.iter().any(|i| i.unique) { candidates.retain(|i| i.unique); }
    }
    if named.is_none() {
        let unique: Vec<_> = candidates.iter().copied().filter(|index| index.unique && index.columns.len() == terms.len()).collect();
        if unique.len() == 1 { candidates = unique; }
    }
    let index = match candidates.as_slice() {
        [one] => (*one).clone(),
        [] => {
            if read.index.is_none() {
                if let Some(names) = &named {
                    let name = names.iter().map(|n| { let mut chars = n.chars(); chars.next().map(|c| c.to_uppercase().to_string()).unwrap_or_default() + chars.as_str() }).collect::<String>();
                    return Err(unknown(site, format!("{} has no index with {} columns matching this key", read.table, names.len()), format!("by by{name}: {}", names.join(", "))).guide("indexes"));
                }
            }
            let declared: Vec<String> = target.indexes.iter().map(|i| format!("{}: {}", i.name, i.columns.join(", "))).collect();
            return Err(unknown(site, format!("{} has no index with {} column{} matching this key", read.table, terms.len(), if terms.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" }), format!("declare `unique by{}: <columns>` on {}; its indexes are {}", read.table.chars().next().map(|c| c.to_uppercase().to_string()).unwrap_or_default(), read.table, if declared.is_empty() { "none".to_owned() } else { declared.join("; ") }) + if extras && named.is_some() { "; an index is needed when the named equalities do not cover a total unique key" } else { "" }));
        }
        many => {
            let names: Vec<&str> = many.iter().map(|i| i.name.as_str()).collect();
            let changes = many.iter().filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
                .map(|index| crate::repair::ReadChange::Index(index.name.clone())).collect();
            return Err(ambiguous(site, format!("{} has {} indexes matching this key: {}; choose a unique key with admitted arguments", read.table, many.len(), names.join(", ")), "")
                .repair_read(&read.site, changes));
        }
    };
    super::point_key::check(target, &index, read, &terms.iter().zip(&types).map(|((column, term), ty)|
        (column.as_deref(), (!ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown) || bindings.present.contains(term))).collect::<Vec<_>>(), true)?;
    let equalities = terms.iter().filter_map(|(column, term)| column.as_ref().filter(|col| !index.columns.contains(col)).map(|col| (col.clone(), term.clone()))).collect();
    let mut ordered: Vec<RuleTerm> = match &named {
        Some(names) => index.columns.iter().map(|column| {
            let position = names.iter().position(|n| n == column).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("key omits column {column} of {}.{}", read.table, index.name), format!("name each column once: {}", index.columns.join(", "))))?;
            Ok(terms[position].1.clone())
        }).collect::<Fallible<_>>()?,
        None => terms.into_iter().map(|(_, term)| term).collect(),
    };
    // Literals take the column's nominal form: an id in an identity column,
    // a checked variant in an enum column.
    for (term, column) in ordered.iter_mut().zip(&index.columns) {
        let expected = if column == "id" { Some(ScalarType { kind: TypeKind::Ref { table: read.table.clone() }, optional: false }) } else { target.columns.get(column).cloned() };
        if let (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(text)), Some(expected)) = (&*term, expected) {
            match &expected.kind {
                TypeKind::Id | TypeKind::Principal | TypeKind::Ref { .. } => *term = RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone())),
                TypeKind::Enum { variants } if !variants.contains(text) => {
                    return Err(type_error(site, format!("'{text}' is not one of {}.{column}'s variants {}", read.table, variants.iter().map(|v| format!("'{v}'")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")), format!("'{}'", variants.first().cloned().unwrap_or_default())));
                }
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    Ok((read.table.clone(), index.name, ordered, equalities))
}

## 11. Alternatives

- **Keep the per-party rewrite** (status quo). Correct, but every client
  implements a two-cursor merge with de-duplication.
- **Per-branch cursors** (a vector of positions). Not needed: one shared
  position is exact (§5).
- **The eager merge** (`take + 1` from every lane, sort, truncate). It is the
  older engines' downgrade path; it cannot continue a cut.
- **New IR fields** (`Merge.branches`, `MergeLane.grant`, the draft's
  proposal). The lane identity needs no new field, keeps every digest, and an
  older decoder's behaviour is the same either way (it ignores unknown
  fields); a capability fence would refuse the whole backend on an older
  device for a plan it runs correctly (GPT review, finding 6).
- **Union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000 §4). Orthogonal: a device could
  hold a two-party table, and the server page would still need this plan.
- **A readers table** (one row per party). The offline recipe; it adds writes
  and a table the author maintains.
- **Expand single-party rules too.** Deferred (Q1).

## 12. Open questions for the author

- **Q1.** Should a single party (`read <- .owner = viewer`) also expand, so
  `todos last 50 by .at` is complete without the pin? It replans every
  unpinned owner page and changes the `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` teaching.
- **Q2.** Is 8 the right branch bound?
- **Q3.** A grant-guarded existence lane, so owner-or-staff `exists` answers
  `false` too.
- **Q4.** Decided totals over branches by inclusion–exclusion
  (`count(a) + count(b) - count(a ∧ b)`).
- **Q5.** Branch merges of search pages (a merge of word merges).
- **Q6 resolved (PAGE, 2026-10-12).** A capped membership branch on an
  ordinary target column is a pinned lane merged with the per-membership
  lanes in §14. A target `id` key remains outside the expansion.
- **Q7 resolved (CUT, 2026-10-12).** Held-group uniform pages reserve the server's prefix-rule charge, including query admission (§7; LLP 3000.007 §20.24.7).

## 14. PAGE: capped owner-or-membership pages

PAGE resolves Q6 for the common rule

```quarry
read <- .owner = viewer or exists members[.parent, viewer]
```

when the membership lookup names an exact unique key and the membership table
declares a finite cap whose grouping columns are fixed by the viewer or other
row-independent expressions. More formally, the read rule's top-level
disjunction has at least one principal-party equality and at least one such
membership probe, and every disjunct is one of those two forms. Every
qualifying probe has one row-dependent exact-key component naming an ordinary
target column (not `id`) that the page has not already pinned, and otherwise
fixed components; its cap group is wholly among the fixed components and its
read rule is decided true by them. The §2–§3 exclusions still apply: total
page predicates, shared order and total cursor suffix, portable keys and
labels, at most eight physical components in both source and target indexes,
and fallback without speculative indexes. The planner first lowers
bare booleans and negated booleans to equality pins, as it does for every
other intent plan. It emits the party lanes, one descriptor per membership
probe, and one system lane. Their static descriptor count remains at most
eight; each declared membership cap bounds its descriptor's dynamic fanout
separately and may itself be greater than eight.

The dynamic descriptor scans the membership range to completion before the
page may emit a row. Each distinct parent in that result opens a target range
pinned to that parent. The membership row is the witness for the rule branch,
so target rows in that range need no second membership probe; their ordinary
target coverage, horizon and expiry checks still apply. The owner and parent
ranges merge in the requested order and `id`, deduplicate overlaps, and share
one version-4/5 position. Source discovery and every opened target scan use
the query's one store transaction/snapshot, so a membership cannot be revoked
between proving a lane and reading it. The system lane reads the whole
original range and suppresses every other lane for a system context.

The cursor descriptor includes the static lanes and the membership source's
table and index. Its scope includes the evaluated static pins and the source
range's fixed viewer/argument pins. It deliberately does not digest the set of
parents returned by that range: membership rows, like target rows, may change
between requests. This is ordinary non-snapshot pagination. The next request
answers completeness for its current remaining range strictly after the
shared position; it makes no claim about rows at or before that position. A
new membership therefore contributes only currently readable rows after the
cursor, and a removed membership contributes no further rows. Schema or
fixed-principal changes still restart because they change the descriptor or
scope.

The backend carries `branch-membership-v1` in addition to `branch-merge-v1`.
An older device understands the static branch cursor but not the mandatory
discovery-before-output step, so it refuses this backend instead of attempting
the eager merge. Handshake negotiation uses the ordinary schema-capability
path.

Membership discovery is an all-or-nothing admission step. Source rows,
distinct-parent retention and every dynamic lane descriptor charge the
ordinary examined, step and heap meters. A meter cut, cap
latch, unknown presence, incomplete source coverage or source horizon before
the source range is known complete returns an empty incomplete page and no
continuation. Minting either a normal continuation or a skip continuation at
that point could omit an undiscovered parent whose first row sorts before an
already emitted owner row. Once discovery finishes, ordinary per-range meter
cuts use the §6 frontier and may return a safe continuation.

The heap ledger applies to the whole membership merge, including owner and
system lane buffers as well as dynamic target buffers. It reserves source
distinct sets, evaluated prefixes and bounds, merge-position and heap slots,
deduplication, the emitted-page vector, frontier decode, and cursor/page-record
scratch before allocation. A source distinct key is first sized by the
allocation-free canonical JSON writer, then charged and encoded into one
bounded buffer of exactly that requested capacity; escaped parent text cannot
cause geometric growth after admission. Compiled scans remain shared; dynamic
descriptors borrow one compiled target scan rather than cloning its filter tree
per parent.

On a device the membership source and every opened target range retain their
normal per-scan coverage decisions. PAGE is an explicit exception to §7's
blanket synced-union exclusion for this proved source shape: it has a
system-only lane rather than a probe-grant lane, so acquired membership and
target union groups can use the same plan and produce the same complete page
as the server. An online or unacquired source makes discovery unknown and
returns the empty incomplete answer above. An unheld or horizon-incomplete
target range makes the result incomplete and triggers the ordinary server
fallback; no partial device result is marked complete. The plan does not
treat a merely uniform denial as an empty membership set: binding validates
that the source pins decide its read rule true. Expiry from membership
discovery joins target expiry at the existing minimum.

The planner and Lean classifier mirror point-probe resolution before accepting
the branch. A positional probe selects the one declared unique whose ordered
column types accept its arguments; equal-arity keys with different types do not
make it ambiguous, while multiple matching keys still refuse the positional
spelling. Named probe columns are duplicate-free and contain every column of a
duplicate-free declared unique, additional columns are fixed equalities, and a
cap covers the fixed discovery prefix. The descriptor bound counts each party

/bin/bash -lc "git show d4e7e4fcf9:llp/3000.036.001-merged-pages-for-owner-or-x-lists.rfc.md | sed -n '800,1130p'" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-lang
 succeeded in 0ms:
cap covers the fixed discovery prefix. The descriptor bound counts each party
column once, matching runtime party-lane deduplication while retaining every
distinct membership branch. Its soundness lemma says every emitted
party or parent lane row is admitted under the branch-witness assumption; the
logical theorem does not prove that runtime discovery establishes that
witness. Its coverage lemma splits every admitted row into a party witness or
membership witness. Differential cases include
caps above eight and overlap. Runtime meter regressions cover discovery cuts;
simulator regressions include revocation between pages and missing acquisition;
server and both device stores exercise the same shared-cursor walk.

The implicit `byId` also proves an exact named probe containing `id`; any
additional named components remain required residual equalities. This does not
invent a multi-part positional key: positional probes still resolve through
their typed declared unique, apart from the ordinary single-ID spelling.

A fixed named component may remain an exact equality in the discovery scan's
residual filter rather than its capped prefix, as in
`members[id = 'm1', parent = .parent, principal = viewer]`. PAGE accepts that
component only when the equality is a necessary conjunct over the source row;
an alternative or computed implication is not witness evidence. The retained
filter therefore restricts discovery to `m1`, and a row with the same parent
and principal under another id cannot open a target lane. The planner checks
that every fixed probe component survives as a prefix pin or such a residual
before committing candidate indexes, and the bound-IR validator repeats the
same check without copying the filter. A denial, missing witness, expired
witness, incomplete source horizon or unsupported residual cannot expose an
unproved target lane. Uniform denial and unsupported residuals keep the
ordinary fallback without speculative PAGE indexes. A missing or expired
witness opens no lane under complete discovery, so the owner merge remains
complete; an incomplete source horizon returns the empty incomplete result
described above.

Fixed scalar equalities compare their canonical values across the rule and
expression representations. In particular, unary minus applied to an integer
literal is canonicalized to that negative integer before PAGE validates the
residual and before candidate indexes are committed. Thus `slot = -1` is the
same necessary residual in planning, bound validation and the Lean model; it
does not cause a late fallback that leaves speculative indexes behind.

The intrinsic source `id` is always such a residual, even when fixed. It stays
in the exact logical witness and its equality must hold, but it is not a
discovery-prefix component and cannot satisfy the discovery cap. Thus `cap by
id` alone keeps the ordinary incomplete scan; a separate cap over fixed
discovery columns such as `principal` is required for PAGE.

An optional discovered parent may be `null` only when the exact point probe
identifies the source row without that parent component. For example,
`members[id = 'm1', parent = .parent, principal = viewer]` addresses `m1`
through `byId` and checks `parent = null` as a residual equality, so a matching
source row opens one null-parent target range. Discovery uses its ordinary
null-covering physical index, including the stored row-id suffix, and applies
the fixed residual before distinct-parent deduplication. If the varying parent
is instead a component of the probe's nullable unique key, a null argument has
the ordinary point result of no row and cannot open a PAGE lane. The same is
true when any other component of that point key is null. This rule neither
turns nullable uniques into total uniques nor changes their storage or
collision semantics.

A varying source field may be the discovered parent even when it is not the
next physical index component, provided the fixed discovery prefix covers a
plain total unique index. Such a prefix selects at most one source row, so PAGE
may project any field from that row: the implicit `id` in
`groups[id = .parent, principal = viewer]`, or an ordinary `parent` in
`groups[parent = .parent, principal = viewer]` under `unique principal`.
The total unique may be a proper subset of the probe's fixed source columns:
`unique principal` also admits
`groups[parent = .parent, slot = 0, principal = viewer]`. PAGE keeps `slot =
0` as an exact residual on the selected source row; a missing row, `slot =
null`, or any other slot does not open a target range. The provisional wider
discovery index is discarded before requests are committed. When several
covered total uniques exist, PAGE chooses only among points whose prefix
independently proves the source read rule and the discovery cap; an earlier
`unique a` cannot displace an eligible `unique principal` by moving
`principal = viewer` into the residual.
The projected field may be optional under the null-witness rule above. The
source cap, readability, complete-discovery, resource and horizon checks still
apply. Every component of the one-row unique itself must be non-optional; a
nullable unique does not supply this proof and declines before PAGE indexes
are committed. In all other cases the parent remains the next physical
source-index component.

The Lean surface gate prices the same physical PAGE candidate before invoking
the logical oracle. Equality-prefix columns use the planner's canonical sorted
order followed by the exact scan-order suffix; the permutation allowed for an
extremum is not available to a PAGE discovery scan. A total declared unique in
that exact order may omit the trailing physical `id`, because its components
already identify the row. If two declared indexes both match the candidate and
the author selected neither, native optional planning declines the ambiguous
PAGE candidate and the emitter reports `page-membership-index-selection`
instead of asking an index-free logical model to choose one. Component, byte or
label overflow reports `page-membership-key-bound`; a reused nullable unique
that cannot prove the varying-`id` one-row case reports
`page-membership-source-unique`. Each stops before the index-free model.

## 15. PAGE2: ordinary exact memberships, row ids and parent owners

Wave 10 showed that §14's cap requirement excluded the ordinary membership
schema it was meant to serve:

```quarry
table members:
  parent: parents
  principal: principal
  unique parent, principal
  read <- .principal = viewer

table documents:
  owner: principal
  parent: parents
  read <- .owner = viewer or exists members[.parent, viewer]
```

`unique parent, principal` proves that the rule's point probe has at most one
witness for one document and caller. It does **not** prove that a principal has
only finitely many membership rows: fixing `principal` leaves arbitrarily many
`parent` values. PAGE2 therefore does not call this source range statically
capped. It admits the exact total-unique witness and runs the inverted
`members where .principal = viewer` discovery under the ordinary read meter.
The §14 discovery fence already gives the required failure semantics: until
the source range is known complete, no owner or membership target row is
emitted; an `examined`, `index_entries`, `rule_probes` or discovery `steps`
cut, unknown device coverage or incomplete horizon returns an empty
`complete: false` page with no continuation. `heap_bytes` exhaustion retains
the existing `E_BOUND` read refusal. Every
distinct value, evaluated prefix and dynamic lane is sized and charged through
§14's heap ledger before allocation, so an uncapped population cannot allocate
past the request ceiling. Retrying a read-meter cut may complete under a larger
budget or smaller population. It cannot skip an undiscovered lane or turn
partial discovery into a negative authorization fact.

This admission proves a safe plan, not completion for every population size.
For a caller with `K` memberships, discovery and target-head work grow with
`K`; once a read ceiling is crossed the fenced result is empty and incomplete,
so a sufficiently large audience can be temporarily unavailable under the
ordinary budget. A cap is therefore optional for correctness and remains the
product's availability bound. If an app must guarantee that the page completes
within a chosen budget, the writer that grants memberships must enforce a cap
on the fixed enrollment group (for example `principal`). A writer allowed to
grant arbitrary memberships controls `K`; point uniqueness alone does not.

A declared cap is a separate static proof only when all of its grouping
columns are fixed by the discovery scan and null exemption cannot apply. Any
maximum is admissible: a cap above the request's examined ceiling may still
cut safely under the preceding rule. A cap grouped by `parent` does not bound a
scan that fixes only `principal`; a cap grouped by `principal` does. A fully
pinned total unique remains the one-row case. Planner, bound-IR validator and
Lean emitter use these same alternatives:

1. the rule probe selects the intrinsic `id` or one declared plain unique,
   and every other probe component is an exact necessary equality;
2. either that selected point is total, the discovery itself is a fully pinned
   total unique, or a declared cap covers the fixed discovery prefix;
3. the fixed pins decide the source read rule true, and all existing index,
   key, label, descriptor, total-predicate and shared-position checks pass.

A nullable original unique is not a total point proof. It can still use PAGE
when discovery pins every column of a separate total unique, or an applicable
cap bounds its discovery group. For example, nullable `unique parent, principal`
remains the positional rule probe while required `unique principal` bounds the
inverted `principal = viewer` discovery to one row. This does not rewrite the
original probe or its null semantics: a null in that selected point retains the
ordinary result of no witness. If a cap is the only missing condition,
`N_NEVER_COMPLETE` gives the exact declaration to add, using the source's
fixed non-null columns: for the usual shape, `cap 50 by principal`; with no
fixed group, `cap 50`. The note also says that a cap is a stored write
constraint: adding it can refuse existing adoption or later inserts once that
group holds 50 live rows (and physically retained rows where cap validation
requires them), so the author should add it only when it is a real product
limit. The note is withheld when a cap cannot repair the
shape, including multiple row-dependent components, a hidden source, a
non-exact or ambiguous probe, a multi-hop target, incompatible ordering, or a
physical key bound. It must not prescribe code that still cannot plan.

The varying target column may now be the target row's own `id`:

```quarry
read <- .owner = viewer or exists shares[document = .id, principal = viewer]
```

Discovery yields document ids, and each dynamic target lane pins `id`. A
one-row id range is trivially ordered, but it still reports the page's shared
logical position, for example `.at, id`: the row supplies `.at`, and `id` is
the final tie-breaker just as in every other lane. All lanes therefore share
the existing version-4/5 cursor and frontier rules. Before any row is emitted,
the engine opens every discovered id lane and reads its one possible head. If
an `examined`, `index_entries`, `rule_probes` or `steps` cut leaves even one
unfinished lane without a head/`through`, §6's frontier is absent and the page
is empty, incomplete and has no continuation: no returned row can sort ahead
of an unread point. Only
after every point lane is exhausted or has a head can the heap emit the true
`.at, id` prefix. Heap exhaustion remains `E_BOUND` as above. The implicit
`byId` is a valid exact source point too, which admits the parent-owner form

```quarry
read <- .owner = viewer or exists offers[id = .offer, baker = viewer]
```

when `baker = viewer` decides the offer source readable. Its inverted source
scan uses or synthesizes the plain `baker, id` discovery order (within the
existing width, byte, label and ambiguity checks), discovers the caller's
offer ids in the same transaction snapshot, and target lanes pin `.offer`; it
does not require `cap N by baker`. Both forms preserve the rule's exact
witness, snapshot, expiry, device-coverage and meter requirements.

Discovery and target scans use their ordinary lifetime filter. A source row
before `not before` is not a witness and opens no lane; a future target row is
not a lane head and cannot be emitted early. PAGE contributes no lifetime
bypass or separate clock. Source and target opening commits invalidate the
ordinary watched table revisions; expiry joins the existing minimum deadline.
No opening timestamp is exported in the cursor or `nextExpiry`.

The schema requires `branch-membership-v2` when a membership operation uses a
metered discovery source or pins the target row's intrinsic `id`. The optional
operation fact preserves that classification across schema serialization;
the existing `branch-membership-v1` remains required for every membership
merge, so capped §14 plans remain v1-compatible. A v1-only engine or device
refuses a PAGE2 backend by the ordinary capability handshake instead of
applying its older admission validator or downgrading the plan to an eager
merge.

The Lean classifier admits row-`id` targets and the same three source proofs:
a total original point probe, fully pinned total-unique discovery, or an
applicable cap. The fully pinned proof is checked on the discovery pins and
does not replace a nullable original positional probe. Its logical `ownerMembership_sound` and
`ownerMembership_cover` theorems remain about the exact rule leaves; executable
examples add uncapped composite membership, row-id membership and parent-owner
pages. Native, Lean and device differential generators draw the same forms,
including source populations that cross the examined meter and must return the
empty incomplete discovery result, and id populations whose target-head phase
cuts before every lane opens. The language, simulator and both device stores
walk each form through one shared cursor and test overlap de-duplication.

### PAGE2 implementation and qualification — 2026-10-13

§15 is implemented at `24a644c511fc5a6888597af9335d894790eaeb97`. Both
independent [Muse](./reviews/3000.036-page2.r12.muse.md) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.036-page2.r12.codex.md) mark this amendment READY,
using the same frozen §15 and implementation checkpoint. This records the
amendment's implementation/readiness; it leaves the parent RFC's legacy Review
status intact and makes no author-acceptance or publication claim.

The concrete fully pinned discovery-unique parity case passes after its old
model control fails completion. The intrinsic-id competing-unique case passes
after its old planner control has no merge. The discovery step sweep passes
after positive old-code ceilings 2/3/6/8/11/12/14/15 produce E_BOUND. Final
checks: language page2 membership 5/5 and branch merge 57/57; Lean difftests 32/32;
smoke 17/17; Lean 58-theorem standard-axiom audit, 71/71 examples, 7/7 fixtures;
core discovery 1/1 and meter 9/9; simulator membership 8/8; device branch merge 12/12
on Memory/SQLite. Heap and non-step/pending errors retain their semantics.

Final 400-case native/device/Lean campaigns have no new signatures. Native
20,619 and device 13,114 executions have zero disagreements; Lean 1,072
executions has only three instances of its two pre-existing classified
pagination signatures. Additional generated native seed 715 and Lean 551
actually compare the discovery-unique shape, with zero signatures; its
uncovered device source remains unknown/incomplete. Full results and commands
are in [PAGE2 evidence](../snapback4/reports/20261013-page2/README.md).

All 50 copied odd wave-10 final schemas compile. `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` falls from 20 notes in 13
schemas to 18 notes in 11 schemas ; 001 incomingRequests and 037 myChildren clear, and named
even 002/098 each clear their sole note. The remaining hidden-source, pure
membership, role-grant and compound shapes are explicitly excluded; no claim
is made that the raw 20-note corpus or all membership-shaped notes met the
large-majority target. [Final survey](../snapback4/reports/20261013-page2/final-schema-survey.md)
binds source/binary/output hashes at the final semantic checkpoint.

The clean Linux web artifact at `10d27f62cc` is 2,305,135 B raw / 703,255 B gzip /
531,512 B brotli, budget 703,755 B = measured gzip + 500 B. Serializer-family and
forbidden-absence audits and locked wasm/client builds pass. No push.

## 16. PAGE3: finish the ordinary private-page plans

Commissioned 2026-10-13; implemented locally. Muse and fresh Codex both
READY in round 5 at `65d505289f`; verbatim reviews and frozen brief are
`llp/reviews/3000.036-page3.r5.{muse,codex,brief}.md`.
PAGE2 left 18 notes in 11 of the 50 odd wave-10 final schemas: 12
party/static-grant pages, three compound rules, two hidden parent sources
and one pure membership. This amendment extends the existing plan rather
than asking builders to merge lists themselves. It changes no read authority,
replicated data or runtime resource ceiling.

**Static grants and whole-group reads.** An owner-or-readable-static-grant
rule uses the existing party/grant merge on synced union tables too. The
server evaluates the positive readable grant once; its holder reads the
original whole range, everyone else the party ranges. An explicit `first` or
`last` is a page bound, not a table cap: a role holder may page an unbounded
table, under the usual request meter. An implicit whole-group read is expanded
only if its original fixed prefix has an applicable stored cap or fully
pinned total unique, at or below the examined ceiling. That original bound
applies to the union. A cap on owner does not bound a staff's whole table.
A fully pinned total unique may supply a one-row lane with the implicit id
position, even when its physical index has no unpinned component.

**Full union devices.** The same descriptor and scope serve server and device
cursors. On a device a full union target's grant lane reads its held authorized
rows, with the ordinary acquisition, coverage and meter checks; it does not
probe a remote grant. That held range covers every other target lane, so the
others are not opened. Missing acquisition stays unknown. Online-only and
single-arm narrowed sync retain their existing grant deferral. This amends
LLP 3000.036.000.000 §5.3's planner restriction, while preserving its holding
and acquisition invariants.

**Pure membership.** Remove the syntactic party requirement from §15. A rule
`exists members[.id, viewer]` or `exists members[.parent, viewer]` has a system
range and exact membership descriptors, no party descriptors. All source
readability, unique/cap proofs, total filters, index/key bounds, shared
positions and discovery/head fences remain. If the source discovery is cut,
no target rows or cursor are emitted. An empty complete discovery means an
empty complete page. Point uniqueness still does not cap memberships.

**Positive compound rules.** Distribute positive `and`/`or` into at most seven
alternatives plus the original system range. Each alternative retains all
of its row equality conditions. It contains either one exact membership
witness, or row equality pins and positive row-independent guards. A membership
conjunct supplies precisely its own authority; all remaining conjuncts must
be true by the target lane's pins. A pinned membership target instead uses
a readable page guard, without discovering hidden source rows. No negated
probe, arithmetic, fallible predicate or second varying source component is
admitted. Candidates are speculative and fall back when the physical plan
or bound-IR validator cannot prove the whole admitted shape.

A new guarded-range descriptor evaluates `page_guard_decision` on its own
pins. A true readable guard opens only that range, not the other alternatives;
a false guard opens none. It does not cover other lanes. The system range
covers every lane only for the system. Status/party lanes, discovered parent
lanes and guarded lanes share one ordered id-tied position and deduplicate
there, as in §§3–6. Thus if `R = ∨i (Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi)` and W is the total page
predicate, each lane returns exactly `W ∧ Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi`; their union is W ∧ R.
Guard facts and membership sources must be readable, as in §15, so neither
empty results nor completeness reveal hidden witness rows. Read-meter cuts

        for column in &parties {
            let mut lane = vec![viewer_pin(column, &read.site)];
            lane.extend(branch.iter().cloned());
            out.push((lane, false));
        }

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets

fn validate_key_names(read: &crate::ast::ReadExpr) -> crate::diag::Fallible<()> {
    let mut names = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    for key in &read.keys {
        if let Some(name) = &key.column && !names.insert(name) {
            return Err(crate::diag::unknown(&key.site, format!("key repeats column {name}"), format!("name each {} index column once; replace the repeated {name} with the missing column", read.table)));
        }
    }
    if !names.is_empty() && names.len() != read.keys.len() {
        return Err(crate::diag::unknown(&read.site, "a key cannot mix named and positional components", "name every key component, or supply all components in index order"));
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// A schedule fires a job or a service-authorized action; either may take
/// `periodStart: time` and `periodEnd: time`, and nothing else.
fn bind_schedule(decl: &crate::ast::ScheduleDecl, programs: &[BoundProgram]) -> Result<snapback4_core::schema::Schedule, Diagnostic> {
    use snapback4_core::ir::{ArgType, ProgramKind};
    let rewrite = format!("job {}(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):\n  for p in profiles:\n    ...", decl.target);
    let Some(target) = programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == decl.target) else {
        let candidates: Vec<_> = programs.iter().filter(|p| matches!(p.program.kind, ProgramKind::Job | ProgramKind::Action) && p.program.name.rsplit_once('.').is_some_and(|(_, name)| name == decl.target)).collect();
        if let [target] = candidates.as_slice() {
            return Err(crate::diag::unknown(&decl.site, format!("schedule {} names bare {} {}; {} declares it as {}", decl.name, target.program.kind.word(), decl.target, target.site.file, target.program.name), target.program.name.clone()).guide("jobs").mechanical());
        }
    if decided(tables, &planned) { return None; }
    // Each lane evaluates the predicate's parts on its own rows: they must be
    // total, so moving an alternative into its own lane cannot raise a refusal
    // that the original's short circuit skipped (`or (… and 1 / n = 1)`).
    if read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(|filter| !super::intent_compound::total_readable(tables, synthesized, table, filter)) { return None; }
    let conjuncts = read.filter.as_ref().map(|filter| { let mut out = Vec::new(); intent::split(*filter.clone(), &mut out); out }).unwrap_or_default();
    let alternatives = where_branches(table, &conjuncts);
    // Case 1: every alternative of the one `or` in `where` decides the rule.
    if let Some(alternatives) = &alternatives
        && alternatives.iter().all(|alternative| {
            let mut lane = read.clone();
            lane.filter = intent::conjunction(alternative.clone());
            let mut probe = requests.clone();
            probe.plan_lane(tables, &mut lane);
            decided(tables, &lane)
        }) {
        return Some(alternatives.iter().map(|alternative| (alternative.clone(), false)).collect());
    }
    // Case 2: the read rule is parties and page guards: readable grants, and
    // disjuncts the page's own pins decide (LLP 3000.036 §16.3), which the
    // binding check confirms with the core's `covering_grant`.
    let mut disjunct_list = Vec::new();
    disjuncts(rule, &mut disjunct_list);
    if disjunct_list.len() < 2 { return None; }
    let pinned: Vec<String> = conjuncts.iter().filter_map(|conjunct| intent::equality(conjunct, table).map(|(column, _)| column)).collect();
    let mut parties = Vec::new();
    for disjunct in &disjunct_list {
        if let Some(column) = party(table, disjunct) { if !parties.contains(&column) { parties.push(column); } }
        else if columns_within(disjunct, &pinned) { /* a guard: the grant lane reads it */ }
        else { return None; }
    }
    // `exists` takes case 1 only: an `or` of party reads misses rows for the
    // system, which every rule admits, and a guard lane has no existence form.
